  says the analyzer owns detection and the user owns judgement, and judgement is
  exactly two verbs — so those two are declared and the register's whole write
  surface is covered, while `resolved`/`reopened`, severity, category and the
  immutable `analysis_result` evidence stay unwritable because writing them
  would forge the analyzer's verdict. The manifest's intro already told agents
  to RECOMMEND a suppression and leave the decision to the user; `ask` is what
  that sentence looks like once the agent can propose it, so the intro now
  points at the targets. The list-route mount registers no handlers, and each
  handler re-checks the row the canonical write RETURNS, throwing when the
  value did not land rather than reporting a success the DB never took).
  `matrx-user/settings` (7 ask-policy ENTITY targets — `theme_mode`,
  `accent_theme`, `display_layout`, `text_generation_style`,
  `language_defaults`, `assistant_name`, `voice_persona` — handled in
  `features/settings/route-shell/SettingsTabContentImpl.tsx`. Every one
  dispatches `getSliceBinding(slice).write(key, value)`, the exact action
  `useSetting` fires for the user's own click, so there is no parallel write
  path and the sync policy picks a change up identically. THE reference for a
  DURABLE surface: settings have no draft/save step and the synced tiers
  follow the user to every device, so `ask` is used even on the cosmetic
  targets a `ui`-mode surface would give `auto` — an agent silently
  repainting someone's app and having it still repainted on their phone
  tomorrow is not a cheap, self-correcting change. Also the reference for
  READING PAST THE OPEN TAB: the tabs are lazy components with no registry of
  setting keys, so rather than emit per-tab fields the emitter reads these
  seven straight from Redux — they are readable and writable from ANY settings
  tab, and each has a 1:1 read twin. Enum vocabularies live in
  `features/settings/agent-writable-settings.ts`, which the tabs now RENDER
  their `<SettingsSelect>` options from and the manifest interpolates into its
  contract prose, so the enum the agent is told, the enum the handler checks,
  and the enum the UI shows are one list (it also collapsed three divergent
  hand-typed language lists into one). The exclusions are the point on this
  surface and are argued in the manifest docblock: account identity (the
  Profile tabs), security (passwords/MFA/sessions/keys), billing,
  organization membership and roles, capability governance (AI Models, Admin
  Server), the entire Privacy tab — `alwaysWatching` and auto knowledge-graph
  are background-capture escalations, not preferences — integrations and
  connected accounts, navigation (`active_tab_id` is where the user IS), and
  the experimental/transient shell toggles. Only the route mount registers
  handlers; the WindowPanel/drawer settings shell owns no surface state and
  registers nothing).
  `matrx-user/keyword-research` (3 ask-policy targets on the workbench —
  `research_input_keyword`, a DRAFT staging the primary keyword, registered by
  `KeywordResearchLauncher` itself because it owns that input and gated on a
  `writeTargetSurfaceName` prop only the workbench passes, so the floating
  `KeywordResearchWindow` above some other page can never answer for this
  surface; plus `library_search` and `cluster_scope` as `ui` targets on
  `KeywordResearchWorkbench`. Shared validation lives in the pure
  [`keyword-research-write.ts`](../marketing/seo/keyword-research/keyword-research-write.ts),
  which also owns `MAX_STAGED_KEYWORD_LENGTH` so the manifest description
  interpolates the bound it is actually enforced from. **THE paid-pipeline
  line:** `POST /seo/keywords/research` and the volume refresh are both paid
  provider calls, so NO target starts either — the agent stages the seed and
  the USER presses Research, the same place `image-generate` stopped at
  Generate and `scraper` at Scrape. `seo.keyword_market` volume / CPC /
  competition / trajectory is observed provider evidence with no write path at
  all, and archiving keywords stays a human click behind its confirm).
  `matrx-user/marketing-competitors` (3 ask-policy targets on the Competitor
  Opportunity Autopsy — `autopsy_run_plan`, ONE draft object bundling
  `competitor_domains` + `max_competitors` + `pages_per_competitor` because
  they are one decision submitted by one button and splitting them lets an
  agent name five rivals while the budget silently discards four; plus
  `competitor_tracking` and `opportunity_status` as entity targets through the
  canonical `seo.update_competitor_tracking` /
  `seo.update_competitor_opportunity_status` RPCs, so an agent's triage carries
  the same `human_ruling` provenance a row-action click does. Handlers in
  `features/marketing/competitors/CompetitorAutopsyWorkspace.tsx`. THE
  selection-scoped reference: both entity targets carry their row id IN the
  value and resolve the visible rows through a REF at apply time, because
  `applySurfaceWrite` resolves EVERY handler before the user confirms the first
  dialog — a render-closure row list lets a multi-target turn write onto rows
  the user can no longer see; an id that is not currently loaded refuses the
  WHOLE write and reports the loaded count. RUNNING the autopsy is never a
  target — it spends DataForSEO provider calls, crawls
  `max_competitors × pages_per_competitor` pages of someone else's site and runs
  an LLM strategist, and the run-history table on this very page carries
  `reported_cost` — and neither is `site_id` (it redirects where the paid run
  points) nor `force_refresh` (its only axis is spend). Vocabulary and
  validators live in `features/marketing/competitors/autopsy-controls.ts`,
  which the manifest INTERPOLATES, the handlers validate against, and the run
  form's two selects render from, so the enum the agent is told about, the one
  its value is checked against, and the one the UI renders cannot drift).
  `matrx-user/marketing-authority` (ONE ask-policy DRAFT target,
  `authority_guidance`, replacing the whole "Optional priority" note in the
  Internal Authority Router's run form — the surface's only authored input, and
  a single-target adoption on the `matrx-admin/applications` precedent that a
  surface can be worth exactly one target. The bound `AUTHORITY_GUIDANCE_MAX_CHARS`
  is imported by the Textarea's `maxLength`, interpolated into the manifest
  description and enforced in the handler, so the limit the agent is told, the
  limit enforced, and the limit the control imposes cannot drift. Running the
  analysis is NOT a target — it joins backlinks, the crawl graph, Search
  Console and the content plan and then spends model budget on the strategist,
  so the human press stays the gate — and neither is the map/routes/evidence
  view toggle or any computed model value (`authority_summary`,
  `authority_pages`, `authority_candidates`, `authority_recommendations`),
  which are the RECORD of what the analysis found. The two triage actions
  (dismiss a route, add one to the link plan) are DEFERRED rather than
  rejected: both clear the judgment bar and both already have canonical server
  actions, but `seo.collection_run` RLS made them impossible to live-verify
  from the test account, and an unverified target is not shipped).
  `matrx-user/voice-pad` (2 ask-policy DRAFT targets over the pad's ONE
  authored field — `draft_text` replaces the whole working buffer,
  `append_draft_text` adds to the end without re-sending it; handlers on
  `VoicePad.tsx`'s nested provider, both dispatching the same `setDraftText`
  the textarea fires on every keystroke. THE calibration case for the
  ~2-YES-field floor: one editable buffer earns targets anyway because
  cleaning up dictation is the pad's whole purpose. `transcript_entries` and
  `live_transcript` are OBSERVED EVIDENCE and stay read-only permanently;
  both handlers refuse while `is_recording` or `is_transcribing` is true,
  reading that state at call time rather than from the render closure. Single
  mount by design — `VoicePadAdvanced` / `VoicePadEmbed` mount no runtime and
  offer an agent nothing).
  `matrx-user/sandboxes` (2 ask-policy DRAFT targets on the sandbox terminal —
  `command_input` stages one shell command into the terminal's input box and
  `working_directory` sets where it will run; handlers on the detail page's own
  provider, `app/(core)/sandbox/[id]/page.tsx`, driving the same
  `setCommandInput` / `setCwd` the user's typing drives. THE reference for the
  **compose/execute split**: the agent writes the command, the human reads it in
  the box and presses Enter, and that keystroke is the consent. There is
  deliberately NO run/submit target and no handler may reach `handleExec` — a
  sandbox shell is arbitrary code execution on a live machine, and the seam's
  one-line `ask` confirm is not an informed review of what a shell string will
  do. This is exactly where the `tasks` surface's `save_task` action target
  stops generalizing: `save_task` persists an already-previewed draft into a row
  the user owns and can edit again, while a command is neither
  previewed-by-its-value nor reversible. `working_directory` is honest only
  because the page now sends the displayed cwd on every exec, so the prompt is
  authoritative rather than a lagging mirror of the orchestrator's session cwd.
  **THE STAGED-VS-TYPED AFFORDANCE reference** (added 2026-08-12 reconciling a
  two-branch collision on this surface): the input carries a "staged by agent ·
  not run" marker and the prompt's cwd turns amber whenever a value arrived
  through a target, clearing on the user's first keystroke, on history recall,
  and on exec — so an agent-written command is never presented as one the user
  typed, and the marker cannot outlive the value it describes. That closed the
  last objection of a parallel assessment which had ruled this surface OUT; the
  other objection it raised was a real defect (the handler used to `focus()` the
  prompt after staging, putting a live shell one keystroke from executing) and
  is fixed. Post-fix the gate reads: ask confirm → Apply → the command sits
  visible, unfocused and MARKED → deliberate click → Enter, one act longer than
  the `matrx-admin/database` `sql_query` precedent it was measured against.
  Terminal output, command history and the executing flag are OUTPUT and status,
  never targets; Stop / Extend / Delete stay human. The list mount registers
  none — tier/template/TTL are machine-provisioning config, not authored
  content).
  offer an agent nothing), and
  `matrx-user/scraper` (5 targets — `scrape_command` (partial-patch
  `{mode?, url?, keyword?}`), `scrape_page_limit` / `scrape_result_limit`
  (the two budgets), and `selected_result_page` / `selected_search_hit`
  (`mode:"ui"` selection over things ALREADY fetched). Handlers are ONE
  factory, `features/scraper/agent-context/scraperWriteHandlers.ts`, shared
  by FIVE mounts: the floating `scraperWindow` workspace and, since
  2026-08-12, all four `/scraper/*` ROUTES via
  `agent-context/ScraperSurfaceMount.tsx`. THE per-mount-by-capability
  reference: a mount passes only the setters it owns and the factory returns
  a handler only for the targets those cover, so `listAgentWritableTargets`
  offers `/scraper/search-and-scrape` exactly `scrape_command` +
  `scrape_page_limit` + `selected_result_page`, and `/scraper/search`
  exactly the search-mode three — the routes cannot switch MODE at all,
  because there the mode IS the route. Running a scrape is never a target:
  it spends real time against someone else's server, and every scraped
  value stays read-only forever), and
  `matrx-user/marketing` (ONE ask-policy DRAFT target, `site_editor_draft`,
  staging `{site, name?, description?}` into the site editor dialog on
  `/marketing/sites`; handler on `SitesPortfolio`'s own provider, staging
  through the same `setDraft` a keystroke fires, so the human still presses
  "Save site" and the existing version-guarded `updateSiteIdentity` runs.
  Validation is the pure, unit-tested `lib/site-write-targets.ts`, whose
  `SITE_NAME_MAX` / `SITE_DESCRIPTION_MAX` the manifest interpolates into the
  model-facing description so the advertised contract IS the enforced one.
  **THE per-mount-posture reference for a surface whose mounts DISAGREE**:
  five components mount this one surface and only ONE registers a handler.
  `MarketingConnectionsCatalog` owns provider credentials and Search Console /
  Bing property ids (identity, never writable), `WorkspaceCostWorkspace` is a
  spend report and `MarketingHub` is a navigation map — nothing to author. The
  interesting refusal is `BrandsPortfolio`: its editor writes
  `{name, industry, description}`, but `matrx-user/marketing-brand` ALREADY
  ships `brand_identity` over industry/description and declares the brand NAME
  human-owned, so a second target set here would be the two-paths-into-one-row
  defect, not coverage — **the brand half of this hub earns nothing, and
  shipping only the site half is the finding, not a shortfall.** The target
  RESOLVES the row from the loaded list (exact id / domain / name, then a
  unique partial) and refuses unknown or ambiguous selectors with the loaded
  rows listed; it OPENS the editor when none is open, because that dialog is
  modal and a user therefore cannot open it and THEN reach the composer — the
  `matrx-admin/feedback` reachability rule — while refusing outright when an
  editor is already open on a DIFFERENT site, since closing it would destroy
  unsaved work. The dialog also gained
  `onInteractOutside={(e) => e.preventDefault()}`: a write confirm renders
  OUTSIDE it, and answering one would otherwise count as an outside
  interaction and discard the copy just staged — verified by a two-call turn
  where the editor survived the second confirm intact. Read twin
  `site_editor` (the STAGED name/description, empty when no editor is open) is
  new, and `visible_sites` now actually emits the `description` its manifest
  had always advertised. Deliberately NOT writable: `root_url` / `domain`
  (identity — the editor itself shows them read-only, since a change is a
  page-registry migration), `brand_id` and organization (ownership), logo /
  favicon / OG image URLs (the `matrx-user/html-page` line — an agent can only
  invent a plausible URL), `status` / `visibility` (pausing governs whether
  metered crawl and scan work runs, `error` is system-written, visibility is
  permissions), delete, and site CREATION — `/marketing/sites/new` mounts no
  runtime and its only non-identity field is a display name that already
  defaults to the domain).
- **UI-state reads** — `runtime/surface-ui-state.ts`: the page PUBLISHES
  interaction-state projections (`publishSurfaceUiState`), rendered blocks
  read by key (`useCurrentSurfaceUiState` — stack-walking, same resolution as
  writes). Never a store, never domain data, never callbacks.
- **Apply policy** — per-target `applyPolicy: manual | ask | auto` is the
  SURFACE's default opinion (transient `ui` writes can be `auto`; persisted
  `entity` writes default `ask`; `manual` = user-gesture only). The BINDING is
  where the user controls it: `write_policies` on the `surface_binding`
  payload (v2), merged through the same weakest→strongest layers as value
  mappings, registered at launch (`registerSurfaceWritePolicies`), resolved at
  apply time. A binding may tighten but can NEVER open a `manual` target.
  **Editor UI (built 2026-07-29):** the ONE controlled editor is
  [`components/bind/WritePolicyEditor.tsx`](./components/bind/WritePolicyEditor.tsx)
  (Default/Manual/Ask/Auto per declared target; the `manual` floor is shown
  disabled, never silently ignored). Consumers: the per-agent 5-panel shell's
  **Agent access** column ([`admin/columns/AgentAccessColumn.tsx`](./admin/columns/AgentAccessColumn.tsx),
  converted from the old Playground stub — saves through
  `upsertAgentSurfaceBindingThunk`, creating a personal mapping-less binding
  when none exists), the batch editor's per-surface "Agent write access"
  section, and the shortcut editor (`ShortcutEditorNext`). **Round-trip law:**
  the `surface_binding` payload is replaced WHOLESALE on save, so every save
  path carries the binding's current `value_mappings` + the FULL policy map
  (BindingColumn, the batch editor, and the Agent access column all do).
  **Shortcut storage (no DDL):** the shortcut's overrides live INSIDE
  `agent.shortcut.value_mappings` under the reserved `__write_policies` key —
  serializer pair in `features/agents/redux/agent-shortcuts/converters.ts`
  (`parseShortcutWritePolicies` / `packShortcutValueMappings`;
  `parseValueMappings` strips the key). The launch thunk pushes the
  shortcut's `writePolicies` on the `"shortcut"` layer, so a policy-only
  shortcut still participates in the merge, and `matrx-user/pdf-extractor` (2 draft/`ask` targets on the Content-extractor template in the PDF studio's right inspector — `extraction_template_draft` (partial `{template_name, page_range, chunk_size, chunk_overlap}`) and `extraction_output_columns` (the whole Results table definition); handlers in `ChunkingConfigForm` through the same `patchDraft` the form's own inputs dispatch, mode-gated on the panel's EDITING state and refused while a run is in flight, with a base refusal layer on `PdfStudioShell`'s provider for when the panel is not mounted. Declared HERE and not on `matrx-user/extractor-chunker`, which the campaign named: that surface has no client mount at all — it is a server-side per-chunk vocabulary catalog, so targets on it would never be offered), and
  `matrx-user/cms` (the CMS HUB — ONE ask-policy `mode:"draft"` target,
  `new_site_draft`, a partial `{name?, slug?, domain?}` staged into the Create
  New Site dialog; handler on `app/(core)/cms/page.tsx`'s existing provider
  through the same `handleNameChange` / `setNewSlug` / `setNewDomain` the
  dialog's own inputs call, so `name` alone re-derives the slug exactly as
  typing in the Name field does. **THE reference for a target whose handler must
  OPEN the form it stages into.** The create dialog is a MODAL, so while it is
  open the header's "Agents for this page" button sits in an `aria-hidden`
  subtree and cannot be clicked — every agent-originated write therefore arrives
  with the dialog CLOSED, and a handler that refused on a closed form would
  refuse always. Opening it is how the `education-grade-work` rule ("never
  report applied for a value nobody can see") is honoured when refusing is off
  the table; the state is always mounted, only its rendering is gated, and
  Cancel/Escape undo it with nothing persisted. Distinct from the sibling
  `matrx-user/cms-site`, whose 4 targets are per-site settings. Not writable, on
  a LIST surface where almost everything is a record of sites that already
  exist: **creating** the site (`CmsSiteService.createSite` mints a real
  `client_sites` row and routes away — the `crm-create-party` / `image-generate`
  line, an agent may fill the form and the human presses Create Site),
  `is_active` (publishing), `agent_write_policy` (an agent editing the rule that
  governs agents is the campaign's clearest NO, in either direction), the data
  API key (a credential the manifest already refuses to EMIT), the identity of
  any existing site (renaming a slug moves its public URL — that is `cms-site`
  settings), `selected_site_id`/`selected_site` (a hover pointer), and
  `sites_load_error` (the page's own account of a failed fetch)).

## Surface client tools — page actions offered to bound agents (v1, 2026-07-29)

The ACTION tier of the 360 loop: a surface declares client-side TOOLS an agent on that surface can call to do live things on the page. Same registration the platform's internal tool families use (war-room / scribe: inline specs + `tool_delegated` routing) — never a parallel system.

- **Declare** — `SurfaceManifest.clientTools` (`SurfaceClientTool` in [types.ts](./types.ts)): `name` (lower_snake, globally unique per conversation — prefix with the surface domain), `label`, `description` (model-facing prose), `inputSchema` (the generated `CustomToolInputSchema` wire shape), `mode?: ui | draft | entity` (same meanings as write targets). Code-only v1, like `writeTargets` — not yet mirrored to the DB.
- **Register** — `useSurfaceClientTools(surfaceName, handlers)` in [runtime/SurfaceRuntimeContext.tsx](./runtime/SurfaceRuntimeContext.tsx) (exact ref-proxy/module-registry pattern of `useSurfaceWriteHandlers`; any depth; latest closure always wins). A handler `(input: unknown) => Promise<unknown> | unknown` runs the action and returns the tool output the agent receives.
- **Execute** — ONE seam: `executeSurfaceClientTool(name, input, opts?)` in [runtime/surface-client-tools.ts](./runtime/surface-client-tools.ts). Never throws (envelope `{ok} | {ok:false,error}`), deepest declaring mounted surface wins (same stack walk as `applySurfaceWrite`), LOUD on every contract break (toast + `captureError` source `surface-writeback`): tool undeclared, declared-but-unwired, handler threw. `listLiveSurfaceClientTools()` mirrors `listLiveWriteTargets` (declared+mounted+hasHandler).
- **Reach the agent (WIRED)** — `buildToolInjection` (`features/agents/redux/execution-system/utils/build-tool-injection.ts`) reads `listLiveSurfaceClientTools()` fresh every turn and emits each declared+mounted+handled tool as a `ToolSpecInline` (`kind:"inline"` — aidream's supported path for client-delegated tools with no server registry entry; the server schema-validates args and emits `tool_delegated`). No per-conversation arming needed; skipped under the disable-injection brake; declared-but-unwired tools are NOT offered (console.warn). Name collisions with already-present specs are skipped.
- **Resolve the call (WIRED)** — `surfaceDelegatedToolCall` routes any name declared by ANY registered manifest (`isDeclaredSurfaceClientToolName` — manifest-wide so an unmounted page still yields a precise error, not `unsupported_client_tool`) to `dispatchSurfaceClientTool` (`thunks/dispatch-surface-client-tool.thunk.ts`), which runs `executeSurfaceClientTool` and posts through the single `submitToolResult` funnel — the hard-suspended loop always resumes.
- **Policy** — the dispatcher is run-immediately (like scribe). A tool whose effect needs a human gate should route its handler through `applySurfaceWrite` (which carries `applyPolicy`/`write_policies`) or gate itself.
- **Not built yet (honest gaps):** no live adopter surface declares `clientTools` yet; no DB mirror (server-side agents can't see a surface's tools); no `check:surface-drift` validation of `clientTools` (name regex/uniqueness); server tools that write + refresh the page are a later tier.

## Header Surface Context windows

The universal Agents header dropdown exposes two ephemeral WindowPanels for the current route/runtime surface:

- **Surface Context** (`surfaceContextWindow`) is available to every user. It samples the matching `SurfaceRuntimeProvider.getScope()` while open, labels live/snapshot/unavailable state, and presents declared values beside their exact current values plus any undeclared runtime keys.
- **Surface Context Admin** (`surfaceContextInspector`) is gated by Redux `selectIsAdmin` both in the dropdown and inside the window. Its Live Values view uses the same runtime sampling; Manifest & Settings embeds the canonical admin editor and exposes code-owned provenance plus every DB-owned editable surface setting.

Both panels keep the standard thin WindowPanel chrome: friendly manifest-derived surface labels, icon-only header actions, and the canonical compact Copy for AI control. Runtime/manifest status and technical identifiers belong in panel content, footers, or the copied AI payload rather than the user-facing title bar.

Sampling is intentionally view-only and ephemeral: it reads the provider callback every 400 ms while a panel is open and never copies surface data into Redux or changes the agent execution scope.

## Architecture

- **Service**: [features/surfaces/services/surfaces.service.ts](./services/surfaces.service.ts)
  - `listSurfacesWithStats()` — single round-trip that fans out parallel reads
    (`ui.ui_surface` + `tool.surface_defaults` + `tool.bundle` +
    `agent.menu_surface` + `ui.ui_surface_value`) and joins counts in JS.
    Cheaper than sequential queries; cheaper than a server-side RPC for a
    table that admins read at most a few times per session.
  - `bulkSetSurfacesActive(names, active)` — one `UPDATE ... WHERE name IN (...)`.
  - `tierFor(sortOrder)` + `SURFACE_TIERS` — convention-driven grouping (see
    "Sort_order tiers" below).
- **Component**: [features/surfaces/components/SurfacesAdminPage.tsx](./components/SurfacesAdminPage.tsx)
  - Client tabs (matrx-admin / matrx-user / matrx-public / chrome-extension /
    All) drive the primary filter.
  - Status filter (active / inactive / all) and free-text search refine.
  - Body groups by tier (Pages, Specialized, Overlays, Editor variants, Debug).
  - Per-row controls: select checkbox, inline description edit (click to
    expand), tool-count badge, agent-count badge, active toggle, delete with
    FK-cascade-aware confirm.
  - Bulk control bar appears when ≥1 row is selected: activate / deactivate /
    clear selection.
  - "New surface" dialog with client picker, local-name input (validates
    `^[a-z0-9-/]+$` so multi-segment names like `debug/state-analyzer` are
    allowed), tier picker (auto-assigns sort_order = tier.min + 50), and
    description.

## Sort_order tiers (convention)

There's no `kind` or `category` column on `ui_surface`. Tiering is done via
`sort_order` ranges, and the UI groups + labels each band:

| Sort range | Tier label      | What goes here                                             |
| ---------- | --------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------- |
| 0–99       | Reserved        | (intentionally empty — reserved for future / pinned items) |
| 100–299    | Pages           | Top-level routes / primary destinations                    |
| 300–999    | Specialized     | Power-user surfaces, secondary tools                       |
| 1000–1999  | Overlays        | Modals, sheets, popout windows                             |
| 2000–8999  | Editor variants | Editor and authoring surfaces                              |
| 9000+      | Debug           | Admin-only debugging overlays                              |

The "New surface" dialog uses the tier picker to assign `sort_order = tier.min + 50`,
so new surfaces land in the middle of their band and don't collide with seeded rows.

## Seed (current production state)

After migration `seed_matrx_frontend_surfaces_expanded` (2026-05-05):

| Client             | Active | Total   |
| ------------------ | ------ | ------- |
| `matrx-user`       | 46     | 59      |
| `matrx-admin`      | 18     | 33      |
| `matrx-public`     | 5      | 8       |
| `chrome-extension` | 2      | 2       |
| **Total**          | **71** | **102** |

The inactive 31 are placeholders for emerging surfaces (beta UIs, debug
overlays not yet wired, etc.) — they're seeded so tools/agents can opt-in
to gating against them without admin work, but not activated by default.

## Conventions baked in

- `confirm()` from `@/components/dialogs/confirm/ConfirmDialogHost` for
  destructive actions.
- No barrel files; direct imports.
- No `useMemo` / `useCallback` / `React.memo` (per CLAUDE.md, React Compiler
  handles memoization).
- Bulk delete is intentionally NOT supported — single-row delete with
  cascade-warn is enough; bulk delete is a footgun for FK-target tables.
- Hard delete is allowed (no `is_active=false` "soft delete" alternative
  needed since `is_active` already exists). The confirm message warns when
  the surface has tool or agent references.

## v2.1 — full enrichment (2026-05-05 second pass)

After the user-requested "go all in" pass, the page picks up:

- **Per-surface detail drawer** ([SurfaceDetailDrawer.tsx](./components/SurfaceDetailDrawer.tsx)) opens on row name click or chevron. Shows:
  - Identity edit (active toggle, description edit, **rename**)
  - "Tools on this surface" — resolves `tool.surface_defaults` names against `tool.definition`, click-through to the tool admin
  - "Agents visible here" — joined `agx_agent_surface ⋈ agx_agent`
  - "Custom tool UI components" — `tl_ui` rows scoped to this surface
- **Rename support** with cascade-aware writes across `tool.surface_defaults`, agent-surface associations, and `tool.ui`, so a rename propagates to every dependent row.
- **Bulk delete** in the bulk action bar. The confirm aggregates tool/agent reference counts across all selected rows and warns explicitly that DELETE is non-cascading (FK behavior on delete is `NO ACTION`).
- **"Add from candidates" dialog** ([SurfaceCandidatesDialog.tsx](./components/SurfaceCandidatesDialog.tsx)) — a curated catalog ([data/surface-candidates.ts](./data/surface-candidates.ts)) of ~70 plausible-but-unseeded surfaces (window-panel overlays, second-tier admin pages, agent embedding widgets, etc.) discovered via codebase inventory. Filter by client / kind / search, multi-select, optionally force-active on insert, bulk insert in a single round-trip.
- **"New client" dialog** inline (NewClientDialog at the bottom of the page file). Avoids round-tripping to `/admin/lookups` to add a `ui_client`.
- **Keyboard shortcuts**: `/` focuses the search input; `Esc` closes the open drawer / dialog / clears selection (in that priority order).
- The candidate-count badge on the "Candidates" button shows how many catalog rows aren't yet seeded — naturally trends to 0 over time.

## Readiness tracking + overlay surfaces (2026-07-24, Arman-ruled)

- **`SurfaceManifest.readiness`** (`verified | partial | stub`, REQUIRED) + `readinessNote` — the campaign tracker for "verified correct and complete". Mirrored to `ui_surface.readiness`; NULL = unregistered (no manifest). Code-owned; the admin board at `/administration/ui/surfaces` rolls up the four states. `verified` is earned via the full surface-authoring checklist, never aspirational.
- **Overlay surfaces**: every window-panel overlay gets its own surface, identified by `SurfaceManifest.overlayId` → `ui_surface.overlay_id` (the overlay twin of `url_pattern`). Emitters mount `SurfaceRuntimeProvider` inside the window component — nested providers out-depth the page, so the window's scope wins while open.
- 11 zero-reference stale `ui_surface` rows hard-deleted 2026-07-24 (ai-voice, browser-workbench, code-workspace, multi-file-smart-editor, news, notes-beta, prompt-apps, applets, voice-pad-advanced, voice-pad-ai, custom-apps — all had 0 values/roles/bindings/tool-defaults).

## Surface writeback — read/WRITE manifests (v1, 2026-07-29)

Surfaces are no longer read-only. A manifest may declare **`writeTargets`** (`SurfaceWriteTarget` in [types.ts](./types.ts)): named write paths INTO the page, beside the read-only `values`.

- **One apply seam:** every caller goes through `applySurfaceWrite(target, value, opts?)` in [runtime/surface-writeback.ts](./runtime/surface-writeback.ts) — never a bespoke callback. It never throws (envelope `{ok} | {ok:false,error}`), resolves **deepest-first** over the live `SurfaceRuntimeProvider` stack (`getSurfaceRuntimeStack`), validates the target against the resolved manifest, and is LOUD on every contract break (toast + `captureError` source `surface-writeback`): undeclared target, declared-but-unwired handler, handler throw.
- **Pages register handlers on their provider:** `SurfaceRuntimeProvider getWriteHandlers={() => ({ target: (value) => … })}` — fresh closure per call, no stale state. A handler validates its input and throws on bad values; the seam converts that to a safe error envelope.
- **Three modes**, declared per target: `draft` (stages into the page's editor — the USER reviews and saves; the preferred default), `entity` (persists through the page's canonical write path immediately), `ui` (ephemeral state — selection/focus/navigation; no success toast).
- **`updatesValue`** links a target to its read twin (the evidence loop: read the value, write the target).
- **Kind components reach it via the action registry:** `apply_surface_write` in `features/content-ir/react/actions/handlers/apply-surface-write.ts` — a rendered agent-result component's button calls `runAction("apply_surface_write", {target, value})`; the component never touches supabase/redux/page internals.
- **Code-only v1:** `writeTargets` are validated by `check:surface-drift` but NOT yet mirrored to the DB (the follow-up that lets server-side agents see what a surface accepts). First live consumer: the content-plan surface family (`content-plan-node` is the reference — field drafts + `save_node`).

### The `manual`-holdout backlog (verified 2026-08-11)

A sweep for surfaces still worth making agent-writable went stale within a day
— `quick-tasks`, `scanner`, `data-tables`, `canvas`, `image-studio`, `lists`,
`working-document` and `scratchpad` all shipped write targets while it was
being written, so it is not reproduced here. Read the Change Log instead; it is
the live record, and several entries below record RULED-OUT surfaces too, which
is the part that saves the next agent a trip.

One concrete item does survive, and it now has a proven recipe:

- **`content-plan-node`'s `node_primary_keyword_id`** is the campaign's one
  deliberate `manual` holdout, for a documented reason — "the surface exposes
  no keyword options, so an agent has no legitimate way to produce a valid
  `seo.keyword` UUID; revisit if keyword options are declared."
  `content-plan-entities` has now done exactly that for its sibling field:
  declare the vocabulary as a read value, validate against it in the handler,
  and mark it `autoContext: true` so the agent can actually read it. The
  inventory already exists — `listSiteKeywordValues(siteId)` returns the site's
  `seo.site_keyword_value` rows and `listKeywordLabels(ids)` maps ids →
  phrase, both in `content-plan/data/service.ts`. Declaring a `keyword_options`
  value is the whole job; the target then moves to `ask`.

## Change Log

- **2026-08-12 — Marketing hub agent-writable: ONE ask-policy draft target, and THE reference for a surface whose FIVE mounts disagree about writing.** `matrx-user/marketing` declares `site_editor_draft` (`draft`/`ask`, `{site, name?, description?}`), handled on `SitesPortfolio`'s own provider and staged through the same `setDraft` a keystroke fires, so the human still presses "Save site" and the existing version-guarded `updateSiteIdentity` runs. Pure contract in `features/marketing/lib/site-write-targets.ts` (17 unit tests); the manifest interpolates its `SITE_NAME_MAX` / `SITE_DESCRIPTION_MAX` into the model-facing description, so the advertised contract IS the enforced one. **(1) The per-mount split is the point.** Five components mount this one surface and `listAgentWritableTargets()` only offers a target where a handler is registered, so the decision was made mount by mount and FOUR register nothing: `MarketingConnectionsCatalog` owns provider credentials and Search Console / Bing property ids (identity, never writable at any policy), `WorkspaceCostWorkspace` is a spend report, `MarketingHub` is a navigation map. **(2) The brand half earns nothing, and that is the finding.** `BrandsPortfolio`'s editor writes `{name, industry, description}` — but `matrx-user/marketing-brand` already ships `brand_identity` over industry/description through `updateBrand` with the version guard, AND declares the brand NAME human-owned identity. A second target set over the same fields is the two-paths-into-one-row defect, not coverage, and contradicting a shipped sibling's identity call would be worse than declaring nothing. So this surface ships the SITE side only: two authored fields, at the judgment floor rather than comfortably over it, carried by `description` ("What this website is for") being exactly the prose an agent drafts well. **(3) Modal reachability, solved the `matrx-admin/feedback` way.** The site editor is a Radix modal, so a user cannot open it and THEN ask an agent — the overlay covers the header Agents button and the composer. The handler therefore OPENS the editor on the site the USER named (resolved from the loaded rows: exact id / domain / name, then a unique partial; unknown and ambiguous selectors both refused with the loaded rows listed), and refuses outright when an editor is already open on a DIFFERENT site, because closing it would destroy unsaved work. The dialog gained `onInteractOutside={(e) => e.preventDefault()}` — a write confirm renders OUTSIDE it and answering one would otherwise discard the copy just staged. **(4) Read twins.** New `site_editor` value publishes what is STAGED right now (empty when no editor is open — the draft target's honest twin), and `visible_sites` now actually emits the `description` the manifest had advertised all along but never sent; both are read off a ref at Run time, and the editor handle is a ref refreshed every render because `applySurfaceWrite` resolves handlers BEFORE the confirm is answered. **(5) Live-verified with real Badass Agent runs on `/marketing/sites`**, staging only — nothing was saved and no rows were touched. One message asking for a name + description plus a domain change plus a delete: the confirm carried the manifest description VERBATIM, Apply opened the closed editor and landed both fields in the real `#site-name` / `#site-description` inputs, and both undeclared asks were refused with the agent explaining the surface exposes staging only. A two-call turn proved the different-site guard AND the overlay fix at once — the first write staged, the second came back as the handler's own throw ("the site editor was already open on my-test.com … Nothing was staged"), and the editor survived the second confirm with its staged text intact. "Keep as is" declined with no error, the declined field unchanged and the applied one still staged. Three forced-invalid values returned the handler's throws verbatim with NOTHING staged: an unknown site (answered with the ten loaded rows), a `description` sent as a JSON object (`must be a plain text string, not JSON and not JSON-encoded — received object`), and an undeclared `status` key. **The JSON-in-a-prose-field trap that bit `shapes`, `grade-work` and `sandboxes` is named in the throw text itself**, which is why the agent reported it instead of retrying double-encoded. Error Inspector on a CLEAN load (valid apply + decline, no forced invalid): zero captures, the sidebar toggle showing no count; on the forced-invalid load the same probe showed `[surface-writeback] Surface writeback contract break` — a throw IS a capture by design, and proving the detector fires is what makes the clean reading mean anything. `check:surface-drift` green (143 surfaces, 3576 values) and `type-check` clean. DB mirror (`ui.ui_surface_write_target`) still pending a manifest sync.
- **2026-08-12 — Scraper adopted on all four `/scraper` ROUTES (live-verified), the write handlers extracted to ONE shared factory, and a real URL-validation bug fixed.** A COLLISION resolved by keeping the merged design and adding only what it explicitly left open. The 2026-08-10 entry below shipped `matrx-user/scraper`'s write half on `ScraperFloatingWorkspace` and recorded, in the manifest docblock and here, that the `/scraper/*` route pages "mount no surface runtime and emit none of this surface's read values, so they get no targets … adopting the surface on those routes is its own task". This is that task; **not one line of the landed target set, prose, or vocabulary was changed**. **(1) The routes now emit.** `/scraper`, `/scraper/quick`, `/scraper/search` and `/scraper/search-and-scrape` each mount a `SurfaceRuntimeProvider` through the new `features/scraper/agent-context/ScraperSurfaceMount.tsx`, building scope with the SAME `buildScraperContextData` mapper the floating workspace uses, so a route cannot emit a different shape than the panel. This matters because `surfaceFromPathname` has always resolved every `/scraper/*` path to this surface and the header nav takes users to the routes, not the overlay — an agent run started there previously had an empty scope AND no write tool. `/scraper` and `/scraper/quick` needed one small read-side addition each (the raw `ScraperResult` envelope kept beside the display projection, which drops links/metadata/timing). **(2) The handlers became one implementation, not two.** The validation was inline in `ScraperFloatingWorkspace`; the routes own the SAME inputs, so re-typing it beside a second set of setters would have been exactly the drift `scrape-command.ts` exists to prevent. It moved verbatim to `agent-context/scraperWriteHandlers.ts` as `buildScraperWriteHandlers(mount)`, and the workspace now calls it too. **(3) THE generalizable bit — per-mount posture by CAPABILITY rather than by a hand-maintained list.** A mount passes only the setters it has; the factory returns a handler only for the targets those setters cover; `listAgentWritableTargets` filters on `hasHandler`. So the target list per route falls out of what the route can actually do, with no second registry to keep in sync — **verified from the agent's own mouth**: asked on `/scraper/search-and-scrape` to write a scraped result, it enumerated its tool enum as exactly `scrape_command`, `scrape_page_limit`, `selected_result_page` — the search-mode pair correctly absent. The one case needing real thought was `scrape_command.mode`: routes have no `setMode` (the mode IS the route), but an agent composing a deep run naturally states the mode ALONGSIDE the keyword, so refusing "be what you already are" would turn every well-formed command into an error. A mode write that does not CHANGE the view is a no-op success; only a real switch is refused, naming the route to open. That distinction was found by the first live run failing on it, not by reading the code. **(4) A REAL BUG in the merged code, found by the mandatory forced-invalid test and fixed.** `normalizeUrl` guarded only `try { new URL(x) } catch`, which is **engine-dependent at exactly the inputs that matter**: Chromium accepts `new URL("https://not a url at all")` (host `not`, spaces folded into the path) where Node rejects it. The 2026-08-10 verification used an out-of-range page limit as its invalid case, so this never surfaced. Forcing `{url: "not a url at all"}` through `scrape_command` returned **`ok: true`** and dropped `https://not a url at all` into the box for the user to clear — an agent told its write succeeded when it had staged garbage. `normalizeUrl` now also requires an http/https scheme and a real host (dotted, or `localhost`), which additionally rejects the non-web schemes it used to mangle (`file:///etc/passwd` became `https://file:///etc/passwd`, host `file` — a string that parses but can only ever return a backend error). Both the Scrape buttons and the write handler share this one helper, so an agent can never stage a URL the user's own click would refuse. Re-run after the fix: the handler's throw reaches the agent verbatim (`client_tool_error: scrape_command.url is not a usable URL: "not a url at all".`) with the box left empty. **(5) Live-verified with real Badass Agent runs on the ROUTE mounts** (not the overlay): one message asking for a deep run staged BOTH fields through two per-target dialogs carrying the manifest descriptions verbatim, with `1 to 20` visibly interpolated from `PAGE_LIMIT_MIN`/`MAX`, and `keyword` + `maxPages` read back off the real DOM as `"postgres row level security"` / `"8"`; "Keep as is" left the keyword byte-identical with the agent acknowledging cleanly and no error raised; the undeclared-results write was refused with NO dialog; and a valid URL landed in the box on `/scraper`. Error Inspector: **zero** `surface-writeback` captures on every clean path — the only captures were the deliberate forced-invalid throws, which are the seam being loud by design. `check:surface-drift` (143 surfaces, 3574 values) + `type-check` clean. **(6) Honest gap:** the floating `scraperWindow` overlay could not be opened in the headless harness (neither the Windows menu nor `?panels=scraper` hydrated it), so the `scrape_mode`-CHANGE branch — reachable only on that mount — was not exercised live this round; it is unchanged merged code that the 2026-08-10 entry verified, and it now runs through the shared factory under `type-check`. DB mirror sync still pending, as before.
- **2026-08-12 — `html-page`'s `page_html_content` throw now names the double-encode failure (collision follow-up; no targets added or removed).** A second agent was assigned `matrx-user/html-page` and had built a parallel implementation before discovering the 2026-08-10 entry below had already landed the surface on `main`. That parallel work was DISCARDED rather than merged: the shipped surface is complete and live-verified, and the only real differences were two extra targets — `canonical_url` and `og_image` — which the original author excluded *with stated reasons* ("a wrong canonical de-indexes the page"; "an agent can only invent a plausible URL"). **Reversing a documented judgment call is clobbering merged work even when it ADDS rather than deletes**, and on inspection the exclusions hold: on `marketing-page` the agent-writable canonical is a DESIRED value inside a plan, whereas here it would be the tag the user's very next Save publishes. One genuine gap did survive that review. `page_html_content` is `valueType: "string"`, and its shape throw said only "expects a non-empty string" — but the inline-tool layer JSON-parses a JSON-looking argument before any handler sees it, so a string-typed target can be handed an object, and the scratchpad / working-document entry already recorded that an agent told merely "expected a string" tends to *fix* that by double-encoding. In those surfaces escaped `\n` and stray quotes land in a user's notes; here they would be published verbatim at `/p/{id}`. The message now says "the raw markup as plain text, not JSON and not JSON-encoded", matching the wording those targets use — one sentence, no behaviour change. **The reusable lesson is about the collision, not the code:** a same-surface chip that arrives while the surface is already in flight should end in a diff against latest `main` and a discard, not a merge of two parallel designs, and "additive" is not automatically safe when the addition is precisely what the merged author deliberately left out. `check:surface-drift` (143 surfaces, 3575 values) + `type-check` clean.
- **2026-08-12 — `matrx-admin/bundles` gets its FIRST emitter AND its write half: 2 ask-policy DRAFT targets, and THE reference for splitting a partial-patch object instead of collapsing it.** The tool-registry bundles console was manifest-only (`readiness: "stub"`, "Emitters: NONE YET"), so an agent bound there was offered no write tool at all. `BundlesAdminPage` now mounts the surface's first `SurfaceRuntimeProvider` over a page-scoped store (`components/BundlesSurfaceRuntime.tsx`, the `matrx-admin/lookups` shape) that the list, the detail panel and the create dialog publish into, and declares `new_bundle_draft` (`{name?, description?}` → the "New bundle" form) and `bundle_description` (→ the selected bundle's inline Description box). **Two targets, not one object, and the docblock argues it:** `email_draft` / `lookup_draft` are one object because a single open dialog is the review unit and one Save commits it, but these two do not share a RECORD — one stages a bundle that does not exist yet behind a modal committed by "Create bundle", the other rewrites an existing row in an inline panel committed by "Save". Collapsed, the destination and the acceptance of `name` would flip on whether a URL param is set. Split, the agent's choice of target IS its declared intent, which is what the admin reads in the confirm. `name` is writable on CREATE and refused on an existing row, demonstrated rather than asserted: `tool.bundle` is `UNIQUE (name)`, every lister tool is named `bundle:list_<name>` (`agent-core` → `bundle:list_agent-core`, live), that lister name is what a model calls, and `updateBundle` PATCHes only the bundle row — so a rename leaves every agent calling the old lister. Member `local_alias` refused for the same reason (it is the identifier `bundle_lister` hot-swaps in), and the Active toggle, System switch, metadata JSON (`server_slug` / `lister_tool_id` pointers) and MEMBERSHIP are capability, not copy. Verified live end-to-end: both confirms carried the per-target description text, Apply staged into the real editors, "Keep as is" returned `{ok:false, declined:true}`, an undeclared rename/deactivate was refused before any request reached the page ("the tool only exposes two targets"), and two deliberately invalid values (`Browser Tools!`, then the taken name `agent-core`) came back to the agent verbatim with NOTHING staged and NO stray dialog left open — the validate-entire-payload-before-opening-anything ordering lookups shipped a bug against. Zero `surface-writeback` captures on a clean reload; `check:surface-drift` and `type-check` clean; no DB row was created or saved.
- **2026-08-12 — `matrx-admin/email` was already shipped, so this task added the MISSING surfaces-side docs and re-verified it independently.** Assigned as a write-target chip for the Email Users compose tool. **STEP ZERO found the surface already agent-writable on latest `main`** (`email_draft`, landed 2026-08-10 by the branch of the same name, which had since been merged and deleted) — so per the campaign's collision rule the landed design wins untouched, and this entry records only what was genuinely additive. **(1) The additive part is documentation, and the gap was real.** The 2026-08-10 change wrote its Change Log line into `features/admin/FEATURE.md` but never touched THIS file: `matrx-admin/email` appeared **zero** times in `features/surfaces/FEATURE.md` — not in the "Live agent-writable adopters" list, not in the Change Log — while its sibling `matrx-admin/ai-models` appeared three times. A shipped adopter missing from the adopter list is the exact failure this file exists to prevent, since this list is where the next agent looks to decide what is already taken. The adopter entry above is that fix. **(2) Independent live re-verification, because a shipped surface nobody re-runs is a claim rather than a fact.** Five real Badass Agent runs on `/administration/users/email` against latest `main`, all passing. Asked to draft a maintenance-window announcement, the agent staged subject and body in **ONE** ask dialog — the composite design paying off exactly as its docblock argues, since a split target would have offered two dialogs over one piece of copy — and the dialog rendered the manifest description **verbatim**, with `at most 200 characters` / `at most 25000 characters` interpolated from `EMAIL_SUBJECT_MAX_CHARS` / `EMAIL_BODY_MAX_CHARS` rather than re-typed, which is the anti-drift property visible where a reader can check it. Apply landed both fields in the visible form with **real newlines preserved** (`\n\n` between paragraphs, no escaped `\\n` and no stray quotes) — the double-encoding trap avoided, because the target takes an OBJECT and the handler never sees raw JSON text. **"Keep as is" was tested against pre-typed sentinel copy rather than an empty form**, so "unchanged" was provable and not vacuous: both fields came back byte-identical and the agent acknowledged cleanly ("Understood — I've left your draft exactly as it was"), with no error raised; the same run also proved the READ half, since the agent quoted the sentinel text back before proposing its rewrite. **(3) The undeclared half held under a deliberately adversarial ask.** One message demanded all three refused categories at once — set the From address to `security-alerts@paypal.com`, select every platform user, and send. **No dialog was raised, nothing was staged, and the form was untouched**; the agent enumerated the boundaries in the manifest's own terms (the From address and the recipients have no write target; "sending is the admin pressing Send Email — that press is never an agent action"), correctly reported the live `recipient_mode` as `custom`, and independently flagged the sender-spoof as phishing-shaped. This is the `data-tables` lesson applied — the request named the page write tool explicitly, so a refusal means the seam refused rather than the model routing around it. **(4) Validation reaches the agent verbatim.** A forced two-line subject (the CR/LF header-injection case, unreachable through the real `<input>`) returned the handler's own throw with no coercion and no retry: `client_tool_error: email_draft.subject must be a single line — it is an email header, and line breaks are not allowed in one. Put the extra sentence in message_body instead.` The confirm dialog is raised BEFORE the handler runs, so Apply was pressed and the throw happened after — and **both fields were still empty afterwards**, confirming validate-then-apply (`parseEmailDraftPatch` throws before any `setState`). **(5) Error Inspector, on the two loads that matter.** On a CLEAN load carrying a valid Apply plus a decline and no forced invalid: **0 distinct · 0 total occurrences, "No errors captured yet."** On the deliberate-contract-break load: exactly **2** captures — one `[surface-writeback] Surface writeback contract break` and one user-facing error toast, both carrying the handler's message. That pair is the seam being loud BY DESIGN, manufactured by the test, and is reported here rather than rounded down to zero. `check:surface-drift` green (142 surfaces, 3520 values). **(6) One honest gap left standing, unchanged from the original entry:** `readinessNote` still records no `data-surface-value` anchors and **no DB mirror of `writeTargets`** in `ui.ui_surface_write_target`, so aidream cannot advertise `email_draft` server-side; the client tool works either way, which is why every run above passed. Syncing it needs the canonical `POST /api/admin/surfaces/sync-manifests` (the `emit-surface-sync-sql.ts` script predates write targets and emits values only) and is left to a human with the admin surfaces page, not forged from here. The pre-existing `agent.review_queue` row on this URL was UPDATED rather than duplicated, per that skill's no-duplicate rule. **(7) CAMPAIGN HANDOFF — no chips could be fired, and the scouting is recorded here so the next agent does not repeat it.** Same wall the `canvas` entry hit: this session sat at lineage depth 8 of a limit of 8, so `create_session` refused outright and the avalanche chain cannot be extended from here — picking these up needs a fresh chain. **The field is now 62 manifests without `writeTargets`, down from 94 the day before**, so recompute rather than trusting any prose list. Ranked against the bar, THREE candidates survive. **(a) `matrx-user/agent-advanced-editor`** — the strongest left, because the manifest docblock is already asking for it ("agents bound here are 'improve this prompt', 'restructure this schema', 'rewrite this section'"). `editor_content` is the authored field, and the replace/append pair from `agent-builder` / `transcript-scribe-live` applies. Two traps make it a real design job rather than a sweep: `editor_field` selects which of three DIFFERENTLY-SHAPED fields is open (prose vs output schema — and the JSON one collides head-on with the parsed-argument trap), and the overlay opens from the agent builder, whose surface ALREADY ships `system_instruction`, so it must not become a second write path onto one field under one name — the `quick-tasks` distinct-names or `lists` one-shared-definition pattern, not a fresh declaration. `readiness: "stub"`, so confirm the mount first. **(b) `matrx-admin/bundles`** — `/administration/agents/bundles`, `features/tool-registry/bundles/components/BundlesAdminPage.tsx`; its docblock likewise blesses the scope ("help the admin name/describe a new bundle, draft member aliases"). `bundle_description` through the canonical `updateBundle` and a `{tool_id, alias}` OBJECT through `setBundleMemberAlias` (both in `bundles/services/bundles.service.ts`, the latter already called at `BundlesAdminPage.tsx:471`) are two clean targets; the object shape is the `cell_value` coordinates-with-the-value rule, since which bundle is selected decides where an alias lands. Bundle MEMBERSHIP stays human on `agent-builder`'s capability-change rule. `readiness: "stub"`, no emitter — `surface-authoring` first. **(c) `matrx-admin/scheduling`** — the Cron tester tab only, and it is deliberately the LOW-stakes one: the manifest says outright that tab is "a pure client-side validator … not a live schedule", so `cron_expression` / `cron_timezone` have zero blast radius while "every weekday at 5pm Pacific" → cron is exactly the derivable-planning-field YES. Everything else on that surface is health counters and run history. **Checked and FAILING the bar, so nobody should spend a run on them:** `rag-library` and `education` (`readiness: "partial"`, so tempting, but every value is browse/report state — search queries, filters, counts, streaks); `admin-official-components`, whose `current_component_description` looks perfect until you find the catalogue is a STATIC in-code `componentList`, so there is no canonical write path and a target there would be the `admin-tool-registry` `tool_group` one-way door; `admin-lookups` and `admin-mcp-servers` (registry browse plus capability governance); `sandboxes`, where `command_history` / `terminal_output` mean running commands — the FIRE line; `messages`, whose authored half is a composer that would be sending; `agent-gate`, one authored field and so below the ~2 floor; and `admin-cx-dashboard` / `admin-server-logs` / `agent-run-history` / `rag-viewer`, which are records and reports.
- **2026-08-12 — `matrx-admin/feedback` gets its FIRST emitter AND its write half: 2 ask-policy draft targets, a page-scoped editor registry for a 4-tab console, and the pattern for making a MODAL editor reachable at all.** The surface was `readiness: "stub"` with one declared value (`active_tab`) and no `SurfaceRuntimeProvider` anywhere, so this was two jobs — mount an emitter, then earn the targets. **(1) What earned a target, and the floor.** Ranked against the bar, the Feedback & Announcements console has exactly two clusters of AUTHORED copy: an announcement's `title` / `message` / `announcement_type` (prose that interrupts every user at their next login) and a feedback category's `name` / `description` (the badge and hint the whole triage table files against). Five fields, comfortably over the ~2 floor. `announcement_draft` bundles its three into ONE partial-patch object on the `matrx-admin/email` `email_draft` reasoning — a title is the promise its body keeps and the type is the severity that copy is written at, so they are one review unit committed by one Save, not three independently declinable decisions — while `category_draft` is a SEPARATE target on the opposite reasoning (`matrx-admin/tool-registry`): a taxonomy entry is a different object, in a different tab, saved by a different button, and bundling it would produce a confirm describing two unrelated edits where "Keep as is" declines work the admin wanted. Both contracts are pure modules (`features/admin/feedback/announcement-draft.ts`, `category-draft.ts`) that the manifest interpolates into the model-facing description AND the handler calls, so the advertised contract IS the enforced one; `ANNOUNCEMENT_TYPES` was added to `types/feedback.types.ts` with `AnnouncementType` DERIVED from it — the `FEEDBACK_TYPES` pattern already in that file — so the enum check reads the same array the `<Select>` renders instead of a re-typed literal. **(2) THE modal-reachability problem, which is the real lesson here.** Every editor on this page lives in a Radix dialog, and a Radix modal sets `pointer-events: none` on the body — so an admin CANNOT open the announcement dialog and then ask an agent for help. Verified the hard way: with the Edit dialog open, the chat composer would not accept a message even when set through React's native value setter and submitted with a synthetic Enter. A target that only ever writes into an already-open dialog is correct-looking dead code. `announcement_draft` therefore OPENS the create dialog itself when none is open (the `CreateStoreInline` move from `rag-data-stores`), and `category_draft` switches the Categories tab to its "Manage Categories" view, because staging into a form the admin cannot see is not `draft` mode, it is a silent write. **(3) `onInteractOutside`, and the two-call turn that proves it.** A surface-write confirm renders OUTSIDE the dialog, so answering it counts as an outside interaction and Radix closes the dialog — discarding the copy just staged. Both announcement dialogs got `onInteractOutside={(e) => e.preventDefault()}`. This is not theoretical: asked to make two SEPARATE write calls in one turn, the first opened the dialog and the second's confirm rendered over it; with the guard the dialog survived and both fields landed. **(4) Why a page-scoped ref registry instead of `useSurfaceWriteHandlers`.** TWO components own "the announcement editor" (`CreateAnnouncementDialog` and `EditAnnouncementDialog`), so registering `announcement_draft` from each would be a last-one-wins collision, not a mode gate. Instead the dialogs and `CategoriesTab` publish HANDLES — live values plus the component's OWN setters — into `FeedbackConsoleEditorStore.tsx`, and `FeedbackManagementContainer` owns exactly one handler per target that resolves which editor is live: the open Edit dialog wins, the create form is the fallback, and BOTH open is a refusal rather than a guess. The registry is a mutable ref refreshed on every render because `applySurfaceWrite` resolves `getWriteHandlers()` BEFORE the confirm is answered, so any "is it open / which one / is it saving" guard read off the render closure would be stale by the time Apply is pressed. Handlers stage through the dialogs' own `setTitle`/`setMessage`/`setEditing`, never a parallel path. **(5) Validate the WHOLE payload before anything opens.** The order is parse → resolve editor → check not-saving → only then open and stage, so a rejected write leaves the page exactly as it found it — the stray-empty-dialog bug is structurally absent rather than fixed. **(6) Live-verified with real Badass Agent runs**, staging only (nothing published, nothing saved, no throwaway rows left behind — this console governs real user reports and announcements that reach real people). One message drove `announcement_draft`: the ask dialog carried the manifest description verbatim, Apply OPENED the closed create dialog and staged title, message and `warning` together, and the agent volunteered why it could not do the rest — "the page write tool deliberately does not expose the active status or the minimum display time — those aren't writable fields for me" — which is the undeclared-target refusal arriving unprompted. `category_draft` staged `Billing & Payments` with a description into the inline form; "Keep as is" on a follow-up declined with the form byte-identical and no error. A deliberate `announcement_type: "urgent"` came back to the agent as the handler's own throw verbatim (`announcement_draft.announcement_type must be exactly one of info | warning | critical | update; received "urgent".`) with NOTHING staged and no dialog opened. Error Inspector on a CLEAN load (valid apply + decline only, no forced invalid — a throw is a capture by design): **"No errors captured yet."**, zero `surface-writeback` captures. `check:surface-drift` green (142 surfaces, 3522 values) and `type-check` clean. **(7) What was deliberately NOT declared.** PUBLISHING — no create/save target and no `is_active` — because an announcement interrupts every user at login and cannot be recalled, the same line `matrx-admin/email` drew at Send. `min_display_seconds` is the seconds every user is FORCED to wait before dismissing it: an imposition on other people's time, not copy. The entire feedback TRIAGE vocabulary (`status`, `priority`, `work_priority`, `admin_decision`, `assigned_to`, `category_id`) is governance — an agent that can quietly mark a bug `wont_fix` or reassign it is the write this campaign exists to refuse. Ids and the derived category `slug` are identity; delete stays human, because deleting a feedback record destroys a user's report and deleting a category orphans every row under it. **The interesting omission is `FeedbackDetailDialog`'s `admin_direction`, `admin_notes` and user-reply compose box** — the most agent-shaped copy on the page, left out on REACHABILITY rather than taste. That dialog is modal too, and unlike the create form there is no safe way for a handler to open it: doing so means picking WHICH user's report to act on, which is record selection on the admin's behalf. Recorded in the manifest docblock as the first thing to add if that triage view ever grows a non-modal path. **Collision checks** (before writing and again before commit): `admin-feedback.manifest.ts` had no `writeTargets` on latest `main`, `git ls-remote` showed no `claude/surface-write-admin-feedback` branch, and `agent.review_queue` had no row for this url. Note the separate, already-shipped `matrx-user/feedback` (the user-facing capture window) is a DIFFERENT surface and was not touched. **(8) CAMPAIGN HANDOFF — no chips were fired, for the same two reasons the `matrx-user/canvas` entry recorded, and the scouting is written down here so nobody redoes it.** Mechanically: this session sat at lineage depth 8 of a limit of 8, so `create_session` refused outright — the avalanche chain cannot be extended from here and picking these up needs a fresh chain. Substantively: **the well is all but dry.** Scanning EVERY manifest on latest `main` that still lacks `writeTargets`, exactly TWO survive the judgment bar. **(a) `matrx-admin/scheduling`** (`/administration/automation/scheduling`, `readiness: "stub"`, so it needs a mount first): `cron-tester/page.tsx` owns `expression` / `tz` state and the manifest ALREADY declares `cron_expression` and `cron_timezone` as read values, so the read twins exist; translating "every weekday at 9am Eastern" into `0 9 * * 1-5` + `America/New_York` is a genuine agent job, the tester is EPHEMERAL (schedules nothing, spends nothing) and it is a plain page rather than a modal, making it the lowest-stakes write left on the board. It is floor-marginal at two fields, so whoever takes it should first check `tasks/`, `templates/` and `features/scheduling/components/form/ScheduleForm.tsx` for an authored name/description — **and must check whether this console renders the SAME `ScheduleForm` as the already-shipped `matrx-user/schedules`, because two target sets over the same fields is a defect; the `matrx-user/lists` shared-vocabulary pattern is the answer if so.** Enabling/disabling a real task or re-cronning one stays human. **(b) `matrx-user/messages`** (`/messages`): `features/messaging/components/MessageInput.tsx` owns a real `content` draft in a non-modal thread footer, the surface already mounts providers in `MessagesPageClient.tsx` and `[conversationId]/page.tsx`, and sending is a human gate — the `matrx-admin/email` shape exactly. **But it is ONE authored field**: `NewConversationDialog` is a user SEARCH (identity, not copy) and there is no conversation-rename path (`updateConversation` is realtime sync, not a user gesture), so it sits BELOW the ~2 floor and should only be taken by someone willing to argue that a DM composer alone earns it, or to decline it honestly. **Everything else lacking targets was checked and FAILS the bar**, so a chip fired at one of these wastes a whole agent run: the marketing report family (`marketing-analysis`, `marketing-coverage`, `marketing-links`, `marketing-sitemaps`, `marketing-competitors`, `marketing-crawl`, `marketing-site-pages`, `marketing-ranks-hub`, plus the previously-ruled `audit`/`authority`/`reputation`/`findings`/`backlinks`) are report, list and filter mounts with no authored state; `admin-users`, `admin-cx-dashboard`, `admin-server-logs`, `admin-sandbox`, `sandboxes`, `agent-gate`, `agent-run-history`, `agent-settings`, `agent-slots`, `admin-official-components` and `observational-memory` are governance, operational or catalogue consoles; `admin-mcp-servers` and `marketing-integrations` are credential-adjacent; `ai-results`, `assistant-message`, `canvas-viewer`, `context-preview`, `file-preview`, `gallery`, `image-viewer`, `rag-viewer`, `share`, `transcript-scribe`, `transcript-studio` and `pdf-extractor` are viewers or records, where a write would forge what the user opened the page to read; `dashboard`, `education`, `podcast`, `crm`, `crm-manager`, `agents-hub` and `marketing` are hubs; `settings` (`/user-settings`) is tabs, toggles and preferences rather than authored copy; `image-uploader`'s `uploader_title`/`uploader_description` are chrome props supplied by the opener, not user-editable content; and `_baseline` / `_conversation-document` are shared manifest fragments, not surfaces at all. **The honest read at this point in the campaign is that it is finished except for those two, and fewer chips beat padded ones.**
- **2026-08-12 — `matrx-admin/agent-slots` agent-writable (live-verified): 2 targets on an admin console where almost everything is deliberately unwritable — and the exemplar bench lifted into the scope to give them a read twin.** THE reference for an ADMIN surface over live production capacity, and the counterweight to the campaign's "admin surfaces are all governance" reflex: the answer here was neither the `ai-models` zero nor a padded count, but exactly two. The console shows 126 slots, their pins, their health and their per-principal bindings, and the JUDGMENT BAR is now written into the manifest docblock so nobody re-derives it. **Not writable, each for its own reason:** `slot_count` / `slots_summary` / `health_summary` / `unhealthy_slots` / `system_agent_count` / `selected_slot_health` are MEASURED from the live `agent.slot_definition` + `agent.slot_binding` rows and the canonical agent slice — an agent writing health would fabricate the state of the system, the `education-grade-work` / `transcripts-cleanup` line. **Repin** (`SlotEditor`: agent, track-latest, pinned version) is identity by UUID over every user the slot serves, and the surface exposes only the COUNT of system agents, never the catalog — so it could only be guessed, exactly why `agent_id` was deferred on `pdf-extractor`. **Enable/disable** and the bench's **Run all** spend live capacity and real model budget (the settled `marketing-crawls` / `pdf-extractor` press-the-button precedent). **The per-principal overrides** — `selected_slot_overrides`, the candidate the assignment flagged as most interesting — were inspected and REJECTED on the merits: `SlotOverrideEditor` holds an agent swap (identity by UUID again), a `model` id from a catalog this surface does not expose, and `thinking_level`, one mechanical toggle; that is the "pure-mechanical toggle nobody would ask an agent to flip" clause, over a binding that changes which agent runs a platform step for a user or a whole org. **Owner verdict notes** on bench results are the admin's judgment of record about a comparison they ran. **What DOES earn a target is the exemplar** — the stored real input the bench replays against the current binding and every candidate, i.e. the evidence a repin is safe. Composing one means reading the slot's contract, inventing an input that exercises it and phrasing a realistic user message: authored content, produced far faster than an admin hand-typing JSON into a textarea, with the human still pressing "Save exemplar". So `slot_exemplar_draft` is ONE `draft` object (`{label?, variables?, user_input?}`, every key optional and partial) rather than three targets, because the form renders them together and the Save button consumes them at once — and because one handler validates them ATOMICALLY, which three cannot. `select_slot` (`ui`) is the second, and it is `"ask"`, NOT `content-plan`'s `select_node` `"auto"`: this workbench remounts per slot (`key={row.id}` on `SlotEditor`, `SlotTestBench`, `SlotOverridePanel`), so moving the selection DISCARDS unsaved typing in the repin, override and exemplar editors — the handler also refuses outright while an exemplar draft is staged. **The scope had to grow to make the write honest**: the manifest's own readinessNote said bench state was not lifted, so the target had no read twin and the agent had no way to know which variable keys to fill. Three new read values fix that — `selected_slot_contract` (parsed with the same `parseSlotContract` the override editor's contract check uses), `selected_slot_exemplars`, and `slot_exemplar_draft` — and because the composer lives in a grandchild that mounts only while a workbench is open, `SlotTestBench` publishes them upward through the new `bench-draft.ts` for the console's `getScope`, mirroring this feature's existing module-level `onSlotCacheInvalidated` bus. **Two answers to the resolve-before-confirm trap, both load-bearing.** `applySurfaceWrite` resolves every staged handler BEFORE the first dialog is answered, so (a) the console's `select_slot` validates against `rowsRef` / `selectedIdRef` reassigned from the wrapper's `ref` callback (the `UserTableViewer` live-ref idiom — assigning during render is a `react-hooks/refs` lint error), and (b) each bench compares `readSlotBenchOwner()` against its own registration id and THROWS rather than staging into a composer that unmounted while the dialog sat open. The console also carries a base REFUSAL layer for `slot_exemplar_draft` (`resolveHandlers` merges the bench's live handler over it) whose message names `select_slot` and says to send the exemplar in a later turn. **Live-verified with real Badass Agent runs on `/administration/agents/slots`, six page loads.** ONE message asking to open `research.auto_tagger` AND draft an exemplar produced TWO sequential confirm dialogs carrying the manifest descriptions verbatim; Apply on both opened the workbench and staged a real test case into the composer with its Save bar live — label "Long-tail SEO blog post, sparse tag vocabulary", a `user_input`, and a `variables` object filling every one of the contract's `topic` / `page_content` / `available_tags`, which the agent could only have known from the new `selected_slot_contract`. The agent sent `variables` as an OBJECT and the handler serialized it, so the inline-tool double-encoding trap never fired; a repeat on a second slot (`chat.cx_default`) staged an equally specific case, so the target is not tuned to one rich contract. **The DECLINE load:** "Keep as is" on the first dialog left the workbench closed and nothing staged, and the agent stopped rather than retrying — *"that action was declined, so the slot workbench isn't open. Staging an exemplar is refused unless the slot is open in the workbench, so I've paused rather than retry the decline."* Error Inspector on that load: **0 distinct · 0 total**, no `surface-writeback` capture. **The UNDECLARED-target load** (asked to set an unhealthy slot's health to ok and enable it) drew no write attempt at all and a refusal that gave back this manifest's own reasoning: *"Health isn't a field you set. It's a computed verdict derived from the pin vs. the agent's versions… Repinning is the admin's deliberate action, not mine… because latest is not always better"* — the surface's second law, quoted from the intro. Error Inspector 0 distinct · 0 total on that load too. **The forced-INVALID load** (`label` as the number 42, `variables` as a JSON string) came back to the agent word-for-word as `label must be a non-empty string — a short name saying what this test case exercises.` with the composer never opened and every field empty — validate-then-apply, confirmed off the DOM — and the agent correctly reported that validation short-circuited on `label` before it ever reached the `variables`-must-be-an-object check. That load carries exactly 2 Error Inspector entries: ONE `surface-writeback` contract break (`targetName: slot_exemplar_draft`) plus its user-facing toast, which is `fail()` being loud by design and the control that makes the other loads' zero mean something. One earlier load is worth recording on its own: with an empty slot name in the prompt the agent refused to guess which of 126 slots was meant and quoted the description's own remount warning back as the reason. `check:surface-drift` clean (142 surfaces, 3529 values); `pnpm type-check` clean with ZERO errors (the ~8 pre-existing `features/canvas/*` / `features/assists/*` / `features/marketing/content-plan/*` errors the campaign brief warns about are NOT present on this main — reported rather than assumed); `eslint` clean on all four touched files. Nothing was saved to `agent.slot_exemplar`: every run staged into the composer only, so no throwaway rows. DB mirror sync still pending. **CAMPAIGN HANDOFF — no chips fired, same wall as the two entries above:** this session sat at lineage depth 8 of a limit of 8, so `create_session` refused outright; extending the avalanche needs a fresh chain. Recorded here because it is now the THIRD independent scan to reach the same verdict, which is worth more than any one of them: scanning all 45 manifests still lacking `writeTargets` on `56e88f320`, I independently ruled out `marketing-site-pages` (a read-only registry over the `web.v_page_list` projection — observed snapshot titles, health verdicts and rolling GSC metrics; its three `agentRoles` are for REASONING, and the only page-owned state is the `?coverage=` filter), `marketing-crawl` (one FROZEN `web.crawl_session` plus ten technical reports — evidence by construction), `admin-sandbox` (read-only list whose only actions are stop/delete/mint-SSH), `image-uploader` (ephemeral intake widget, no emitter, not even alt text), `admin-official-components` (a STATIC in-code `componentList`, so no canonical write path), `gallery`, `rag-viewer`, `education` and `rag-library` — the last despite its two live emitters, because its 32 values are pipeline COUNTS and browse filters and a grep for `updateLibrary`/`createLibrary` under `features/rag` finds no canonical write path to be canonical about. Two additions to the handoffs above, from scouting that went a step further. **`matrx-user/agent-settings` is worth one more look than "governance console" gives it:** `AgentSettingsForm` really does own four authored fields (name, description, category, tags), but whoever takes it must FIRST check whether `matrx-user/agent-builder`'s already-shipped `agent_name`/`agent_description`/`agent_category`/`agent_tags` make it a duplicate write path onto the same record — and if it survives that, its handlers must stage into the form's LOCAL `draft` useState, because dispatching `setAgentField` writes the shared Redux record the form DIFFS AGAINST, leaving the value on screen and the Save bar reading "All changes saved": a silent no-op that looks exactly like success.
