It is also `readiness: "stub"`, window-only, and `route-to-surface.ts:34` maps a `/agents/settings` prefix that is not a route. **`matrx-user/transcript-scribe`'s custom dictionary** (`features/dictionary`, `DictionaryManager`) is the one piece of genuinely authored vocabulary the "viewers and records" bucket hides — terms an agent can propose from a transcript it has just read — but that surface declares a single value and no emitter, so it is `surface-authoring` before it is write-targets. Otherwise this scan agrees with the entries above: the remaining field is browse, report and governance state, and the campaign is essentially complete.
- **2026-08-12 — Agents Hub agent-writable (live-verified); THE composite-filter target, and a ROUTE-vs-MOUNT drift caught in the act.** `matrx-user/agents` declares ONE `mode:"ui"` / `applyPolicy:"ask"` target, `catalog_filters`, a partial object carrying all nine gallery filter fields (`search_query`, `deep_search`, `ownership_tab`, `sort_by`, `included_categories`, `included_tags`, `favorites_filter`, `archived_filter`, `favorites_first`). Handler lives on `AgentsGrid`'s existing provider and dispatches `setAgentConsumerFilter` — the exact action the search box, the tab buttons and every filter chip already fire — so an agent write and a user click are indistinguishable. Its read twin is the existing `filters` value, which is literally this object. **Why ONE composite instead of nine micro-targets:** these fields are one user thought ("show only my archived research agents, most recent first" is four of them in one breath), so bundling buys one confirm dialog instead of nine, makes validation atomic (one bad category rejects the WHOLE call, so a filter can never half-apply — the guarantee the arrays needed, extended free to every field), and removes write ORDERING as a hazard by construction: one call, one store read, one dispatch, no replace/append pair to race. The stated trade is that the user accepts or declines the object whole. **Enum checks run against the vocabulary the UI actually renders, never re-typed literals:** `sort_by` validates against (and the model-facing description interpolates) the same `SORT_OPTIONS` constant the sort panel maps over, and `included_categories` / `included_tags` validate against the LIVE `selectAllAgentCategories` / `selectAllAgentTags` read at call time — deliberately not the emitted `available_*` snapshot, because that vocabulary GROWS as the list loads, so the live set can only ever accept more. `"__none__"` (`AGENT_NONE_SENTINEL`) is accepted because `MultiSelectChips` really does render an Uncategorized/Untagged chip for it; `ownership_tab:"system"` is refused because this gallery renders no System tab, and `"shared"` is refused when nothing is shared with the user, since the tab row itself is conditional and either would strand the user in a view with no control to leave it. Unknown KEYS throw rather than being ignored — silently dropping `{archive_this:…}` would report success for a write that did nothing. **`ask`, not `auto`, on the `images` reasoning plus one of its own:** the gallery's `ReferencesBulkCopyButton` takes the filtered set as its live argument, and more sharply, every row carries a per-agent `…` menu with Archive and Delete — re-filtering under a user about to click a row MOVES the rows, which is the `images` "changed what gets deleted" hazard arriving through displacement rather than through a set argument. **Deliberately NOT targets:** everything that mutates an agent (favorite, archive, delete, duplicate, publish, share) — `archived_filter` means SHOW archived and the description says so twice; the derived read values (`visible_agents`, every count, `has_active_filters`, `version_lookup`); and `peeked_agent_id`, which turns out to be un-writable by construction — `agent-peek-tracker` is a REPORTING register written by each card's own modal, with no canonical opener the hub can reach, so a target there would have needed a parallel open path (the "no canonical write path" trap). **The finding worth reusing: this surface's emitter had drifted out from under its own route.** `surfaceFromPathname` maps the whole `/agents` prefix here, but `AgentsGrid` — the only `SurfaceRuntimeProvider` for `matrx-user/agents` — renders on `/agents/classic` ALONE; `/agents/all`, where `/agents` redirects an authenticated user, became the `lib/entity-list` browse shell (`AgentBrowsePage`) and mounts no runtime. The header popover on `/agents/all` therefore still names "Agents Hub" from the route map while offering neither live values nor the write tool — the `scraper`/`voice-pad` mount trap in a new form, and it would read exactly like a broken target to the next agent. The manifest docblock (which still claimed `AgentsGrid` drives `/agents/all`) and a new `readinessNote` now say this outright; adopting the emitter on `AgentBrowsePage` is left as `surface-authoring`, not smuggled in here. Live-verified with a real Badass Agent run on `/agents/classic`: ONE message asking for four changes produced ONE ask dialog carrying the manifest prose verbatim, and Apply landed all four in the REAL DOM (search box `QME`, Mine tab active, "6 results", the six QME agents alphabetical) — the agent then volunteered the description's own staleness warning ("the visible-agent list from before this change is stale, so I can't reliably tell you the new on-screen results"). "Keep as is" returned `{ok:false, declined:true}` with the page byte-identical and a graceful acknowledgement. A request to archive one agent and favorite another produced NO tool call at all, with the agent naming the view-only scope and repeating the distinction unprompted: "The `archived_filter` control I can touch only means 'show archived agents' — it doesn't archive anything." A forced `included_tags:["oncology-research"]` came back verbatim as `client_tool_error: catalog_filters.included_tags rejected: "oncology-research" is not a value this library uses, so no filter was changed. Available: ai-crawler, compliance, …` (34 live tags listed back) with the filters untouched and no double-encode retry. Error Inspector: **zero** captures on the clean load; the invalid-value load, run separately, produced exactly the one `surface-writeback` capture the guard is supposed to fire plus its paired toast. `check:surface-drift` (140 surfaces, 3,433 values) + `type-check` clean — the 11 remaining errors are pre-existing in `features/brokers/*`. DB mirror sync still pending. **Adjacent bug fixed in passing:** `useAgentConsumer` gained canonical `setIncludedCats` / `setIncludedTags` (one dispatch of the final set), because the handler needed a whole-set write and the existing whole-set path was `toAdd.forEach(toggleCategory)` — every `toggleCategory` computes its next array from the SAME render-closure snapshot, so two toggles in one tick keep only the last, and "Clear filter" with three categories active removed one. `AgentsGrid` now consumes the new setters; two copies of the same `applyArrayViaToggle` helper survive in `features/agents/agent-sets/` (`AgentLibraryRail`, `CreateSetDialog`) and carry the same latent bug — logged, not fixed off-mission.

- **2026-08-12 — `matrx-user/cms` (the CMS hub) added to the adopter list it was already missing from — a COLLISION resolved as docs-only, plus an independent live re-verification of the landed design.** Assigned as a write-target chip for the CMS hub; the STEP ZERO collision check found the work already merged to `main` (`27b21892`): `cms.manifest.ts` declares `new_site_draft`, `app/(core)/cms/page.tsx` handles it, `features/cms/utils/siteSlug.ts` exists as the shared vocabulary, `features/cms/FEATURE.md` carries its Change Log line, and `agent.review_queue` already holds a `/cms` row. **Per the skill's collision rule the landed design wins, so nothing was reimplemented** — and it is the design this chip would have produced anyway (one composite object target, not three scalars, on the `crm-create-party` reasoning the docblock cites). **The one genuinely additive thing was the gap the landing agent left: `features/surfaces/FEATURE.md` was never touched.** The hub appeared in neither the agent-writable adopter enumeration nor this Change Log, while `features/cms/FEATURE.md` closed with "See `features/surfaces/FEATURE.md` for the full adopter entry" — a cross-reference pointing at nothing. That entry now exists. **Worth generalizing: a surface whose own feature doc is complete can still be invisible in the campaign-wide one, and the adopter list is where the campaign looks to decide what is already taken** — so a missing entry does not just lose documentation, it invites the next chip to re-assign a surface that is already done. When a collision check finds a surface shipped, checking the surfaces doc for the entry is worth doing before concluding there is nothing additive left. **The landed work was then independently re-verified end-to-end with real Badass Agent runs on `/cms`** (not taken on trust from the merged entry's claims), five scenarios each on its own page load: (1) "draft a new site for Happy Paws Mobile Grooming, domain happypawsmobile.com" raised ONE ask dialog carrying the manifest description **verbatim** (checked against the manifest string, including the interpolated `CMS_SITE_SLUG_RULE` and `CMS_SITE_DOMAIN_RULE`), and Apply landed `Happy Paws Mobile Grooming` / `happy-paws-mobile-grooming` / `happypawsmobile.com` read back off the three real inputs, with the dialog **opened by the handler** from a closed start. (2) "Keep as is" on a Northside Yoga draft left the dialog CLOSED and nothing staged, and the agent acknowledged the decline without an error. (3) Asked to set a site's `agent_write_policy` to `full` and mark it active, the agent raised **no dialog at all** and refused both, naming `new_site_draft` as the only target and reproducing the manifest's own rationale ("letting me flip my own permission to full would defeat its entire purpose"). (4) A deliberately invalid `domain: "https://Example.COM/shop"` came back to the agent as the handler's own throw verbatim — `client_tool_error: new_site_draft.domain expects a bare hostname such as www.example.com — no scheme, no path, no port. Got "https://Example.COM/shop".` — with **nothing staged and the dialog never opened, even though the `name` in the same payload was valid**, confirming the validate-then-apply ordering rather than a partial apply. (5) A clean load with only a valid apply produced **zero `surface-writeback` captures** ("No errors captured yet"); the invalid-value load produced 2, which is the seam being loud by design. `check:surface-drift` green (142 surfaces, 3520 values) and `type-check` clean. **One harness note for the next agent on this surface:** because the create dialog is a modal, the Error Inspector button is genuinely unclickable while it is open — a capture check has to press Escape first, or it reads as an unreachable inspector rather than as the `aria-hidden` behaviour the manifest documents. **CAMPAIGN HANDOFF — no chips were fired, for the same mechanical reason the `matrx-user/canvas` entry records: this session sat at lineage depth 8 of a limit of 8, so `create_session` refused outright.** Picking these up needs a fresh chain, so the scouting is recorded here rather than lost. Of the 59 manifests still lacking `writeTargets` on `main`, FOUR survive the judgment bar, and the two with live mounts are the ones worth doing first. **(a) `matrx-user/messages` — the strongest remaining surface on the board.** Both mounts already register `<SurfaceRuntimeProvider>` with `getScope` and no `getWriteHandlers` (THREAD `app/(core)/messages/[conversationId]/page.tsx`, LIST `app/(core)/messages/MessagesPageClient.tsx`), and the manifest docblock already blesses the exact scope ("summarize the thread, draft a reply"). The compose box is `features/messaging/components/MessageInput.tsx` — local state, a deep child under `ChatThread`, so this is a `useSurfaceWriteHandlers` job, not a provider prop. A reply draft with SEND left human is the `new_site_draft` / `generation_request` shape, but the send gate is stricter than anywhere else in the campaign because the artifact goes to a REAL OTHER PERSON; `current_sender_name`/`last_message_text` are records of what someone actually said, so writing them would be forging another person's words — the clearest NO here. Two things to settle deliberately: whether Enter SENDS in `MessageInput` (a staged draft one keystroke from going out changes at least the description), and the per-mount split, since the LIST mount has no open conversation and no compose box and should register nothing. **(b) `matrx-user/sandboxes`** — `app/(core)/sandbox/[id]/page.tsx` already mounts the provider (no handlers), and the terminal has a real `commandInput`/`setCommandInput` input whose execution sits behind a separate `handleExec` Send button. Staging a command without running it is the `scraper` `scrape_command` precedent almost exactly, and it is the interesting judgment call of the two: everything else the surface emits is container identity, status and observed `terminal_output`, which are evidence and infrastructure rather than authored content. Check the Enter-key path before designing it. **(c) `matrx-admin/bundles` and (d) `matrx-admin/lookups`** — both admin surfaces whose OWN manifest docblocks already bless the scope ("help the admin name/describe a new bundle, draft member aliases"; "help the admin draft a name/description for a new client, surface, or executor"), but both are `readiness: "stub"` with NO emitter (all state is local `useState` in `BundlesAdminPage.tsx` / `LookupsAdminPage.tsx`), so each is a TWO-job chip: `surface-authoring` first, then this skill. Membership/executor wiring stays human on `agent-builder`'s reasoning that changing what a bundle REACHES is a capability change, not a copy edit. **Checked and FAILING the bar, so a chip fired at one wastes a run:** `rag-library` (32 values, but the page holds only search/status/catalog FILTER state and has no rename or description path — the `files` ruling), `matrx-admin/official-components` (its `current_component_name`/`_description` read a STATIC `componentList` catalogue file, not editable rows), `matrx-user/transcript-studio` (one value, and its docblock says the minimalism is deliberate to protect hand-coded scope), `matrx-user/education` (a landing/nav hub), plus the viewers and records already ruled out in the `canvas` entry.
- **2026-08-12 — CRM list agent-writable on BOTH mounts; THE reference for write targets NOT crossing `inheritsFrom`.** `matrx-user/crm` declares 4 `mode:"ui"` / `applyPolicy:"ask"` targets — `search_query`, `party_kind_filter`, `column_filters`, `list_sort` — handled on `features/crm/components/CrmListPage.tsx`'s own provider (`getWriteHandlers`), which owns every state atom they touch. Each lands through the setter the human control calls: `list.setQuery` for the toolbar search box, the People/Companies facet and the column-filter popovers (the same call `onTableState` makes), and `setPrefs` for the sort (a column-header click). **This is a FIND-AND-SURFACE surface, not an authoring one** — nothing here is authored copy — so it follows the shipped `images` adoption rather than `tasks`: the useful agent job is "put the records matching this description in front of me". **No selection target, deliberately:** `images` earns `image_selection` because tiles carry bulk checkboxes, but this table mounts `MatrxDataTable` with no selection model at all, so declaring one would have wired a target to nothing. Ranking the surface honestly is what set the target list — `scope_kind`/`selected_organization_id` were EXCLUDED as the ownership/visibility axis the judgment bar puts on the NO side (they change WHOSE records are in play, not which of the user's match), along with pagination mechanics, the trash view (no read twin), and every create/delete/restore/merge path. **The new lesson: write targets and GROUPS are both resolved per-manifest and neither crosses `inheritsFrom`.** `crm-manager` (the floating `CrmManagerWindow`, same `CrmListPage` component, same handlers, only `surfaceName` differs) inherits the read vocabulary but `findDeclaredTarget` reads `getManifest(name).writeTargets` with no chain walk, so the window mount would have been offered NO write tool at all; `crm.manifest.ts` therefore exports `crmWriteTargets` **and** `crmGroups` and `crm-manager.manifest.ts` re-declares both — `check:surface-drift` catches the groups half loudly ("write target references undeclared group"), the targets half silently. That is the opposite call from `rag-data-stores`, where two mounts own genuinely different state; here one component means one contract, so splitting would be a lie. Every enum check reads the constants `crm.manifest.ts` interpolates into the prose the model sees (`PARTY_KIND_FILTER_ENUM_TEXT`, `PARTY_SORT_KEY_ENUM_TEXT`, `DATE_BUCKET_ENUM_TEXT`, … added to `features/crm/types.ts`), so contract and guard cannot drift — the `scrape-command.ts` discipline. `column_filters` REPLACES the whole bag and REJECTS unrecognised keys rather than dropping them (a misspelled `name` would otherwise silently do nothing), validates the entire object before touching state, and tolerates the double-encoded JSON-string form; `list_sort` folds `sort_key` + `sort_direction` into one object because one header click sets both, and reads the current sort **through a ref** — `applySurfaceWrite` captures the handler closure BEFORE awaiting the confirm dialog, so a partial write folding in a render-closure value would silently undo a header click made while the dialog was open. `ask` over `auto` despite all four being `ui`: every row carries a "…" menu whose Delete acts on THAT row, so re-filtering moves which record sits under the user's cursor — same call `images` and `markdown-studio` made. Live-verified with a real Badass Agent run on `/crm` against a 3-record list: one message asking for three changes produced three dialogs each carrying the manifest description verbatim, Apply narrowed the DOM from 3 rows to 1 (`1-1 of 1`, search box reading `acme` on a later run) — asserted on the table, not the tool result; "Keep as is" returned `{ok:false, declined:true}` with the search box and rows untouched and the agent replying "You declined that, so I've left it alone"; a "delete Sarah Mitchell permanently" request was REFUSED with no tool call attempted ("none of them create, edit, or delete records"); and four forced invalid values came back to the agent verbatim with the list unmoved — e.g. `list_sort.key expects one of display_name | party_kind | job_title | primary_domain | created_at | updated_at — received "employer". These are database columns; Employer is a joined embed and cannot be sorted on.` and `column_filters rejected: unrecognised key(s) name.` Error Inspector on the clean load (valid applies + a decline only, no forced invalid): **`0 distinct · 0 total occurrences`** — zero captures of any kind. `check:surface-drift` + `type-check` clean (0 errors; the `features/brokers/*` baseline is already fixed on main). Nothing in the CRM was created or deleted. DB mirror sync still pending.

Resolve constants too (`grep 'surfaceName={[A-Z_]*}'`, then map each via `export const FOO_SURFACE_NAME = "…"`); doing so took the detected mount set from 59 to 99. **The refreshed list: 62 of 142 manifests still lack `writeTargets`, but only four are worth a chip.** MOUNTED and ready: **`matrx-user/marketing-findings`** (provider already mounted twice — `FindingsTable.tsx` list + `FindingDetail.tsx` detail; canonical mutations already exported from `features/marketing/data/finding-mutations.ts` — `acknowledgeFinding`/`resolveFinding`/`reopenFinding`/`suppressFinding(reason)` — plus the REAL vocabulary constant `LIVE_FINDING_STATUSES = ["open","acknowledged","reopened"]`; and the manifest itself declares a `suppression_reviewer` agent role whose description says it "recommends which suppressions to keep, revisit, or lift — the user makes the final call", i.e. an agent that can only recommend and never stage is precisely the gap. That is now TWICE on this surface, so it is a property of the chain rather than bad luck — the four candidates above are recorded here precisely because the avalanche cannot carry them, and picking them up requires a fresh chain. **Two verification-harness traps** to add to the list below, both of which silently fake a result: (a) on these pages the word **"Processing" is permanently in the DOM**, so an idle-check that requires the busy marker to clear NEVER returns and burns the full timeout on every message — require a long FROZEN body-text stretch (~45s with no length change) as an independent done-signal and log a heartbeat so you can tell a stalled harness from a thinking agent; (b) the deliberate-invalid-value step must click **Apply** on the confirm dialog — declining it means the handler never runs, and what you capture is an approval-declined message rather than your validation error (the agent correctly said so, which is the only reason it was caught).
- **2026-08-12 — `matrx-user/files` agent-writable (9 targets, live-verified); the rename is a LABEL, the selection is a LOADED GUN.** The largest manifest in the directory (~40 values) gets its write half. Handlers: `features/files/components/surfaces/useFilesSurfaceWriteHandlers.ts`, registered via `getWriteHandlers` on PageShell's provider. **Not a per-mount posture job, and the check is worth recording:** three components name `matrx-user/files`, but only `PageShell` mounts a `SurfaceRuntimeProvider` — `RowContextMenu` and `FileRightClickMenu` pass the surface name to the context-menu-v3 READ seam (row scope/`contextData`), which is a different seam entirely, so there is exactly one handler set and `listAgentWritableTargets()` offers it on every route in the family. The mobile `MobileStack` mounts no provider and stays read-only, the gap `readinessNote` already records. **The target that earns the surface** is the rename: "rename these screenshots to something I can find later" is a real single-message ask, and a rename is a LABEL — bytes, location and audience all untouched. `active_file_name` / `active_folder_name` (`entity`, `ask`) call `validateRenameInput` and then the exact thunks `RenameDialog.handleSubmit` dispatches (`renameFile`, `updateFolder`), rebuilding the dialog's sibling list the same way, so the agent inherits every guard the human gets rather than getting a second, weaker validator. **`renameAny` was the wrong-looking-right path** — it is source-aware and used by `useFileActions`, but it is NOT what the visible Rename control dispatches, so synthetic (virtual-source) ids are refused by name instead of being quietly routed somewhere the user's own Rename never goes. **Mid-run refusal:** both rename handlers throw when `tree_status !== "loaded"` or an upload is in flight, because the collision check is only as good as the rows in the store and a file landing mid-rename can collide. `search_query`, `chip_filter`, `kind_filter`, `list_sort`, `view_mode` are `ui`+`auto` — they clear the `knowledge` bar exactly (client-only, instantly visible, one on-screen control undoes it, no server request, no user position) and dispatch the same slice actions the header controls dispatch. `list_sort` is ONE object target `{sort_by, sort_direction}`, not two, because `setSort` is atomic and a column-header click always sets both — the "multiple values in one field object" rule applied to an action's real shape. `active_folder_id` / `active_file_id` are `ui` but **`ask`**: activation moves the user and, for a real folder, fires `loadFolderContents` and rewrites the URL, failing two halves of the auto bar at once — `ui` mode does not imply `auto`. **Vocabularies come from the RUNTIME lists the controls render from, not the types:** `CHIP_FILTER_KEYS`, `KIND_FILTER_VALUES` and `VIEW_MODE_VALUES` are new exports derived from the very `CHIPS` / `OPTIONS` arrays those components map over, and sort keys come from `COLUMN_SPECS[].sortKey`. That distinction is load-bearing: `ViewMode` has three members but `ViewModeToggle` deliberately renders two (`columns` has no renderer), so validating against the type would have let an agent park the table in a state with no button to leave it. `column_filters` / `visible_columns` were REJECTED on the same rule from the other side — open-ended objects with no enumerable vocabulary in scope, so no target. **Exclusions stated, not omitted:** `selected_file_ids` is the `extraction_selected_row_ids` case — the selection arms `BulkActionsBar`'s delete and move, and staging a selection is loading a gun even though the trigger stays human; `active_file_visibility` / `active_folder_visibility` PUBLISH, which is a capability change and the line `agent-builder` and `admin-tool-registry` both drew; move/delete/restore/upload stay human because a move changes where the user's data lives; `details_level` has no on-screen control at all; and `visible_files`/`visible_folders`/`recent_uploads`/`upload_progress_percent`/every count are read projections with no write path. **Live-verified with real Badass Agent runs on `/files`.** One message drove `search_query`+`view_mode`+`kind_filter`+`list_sort` (auto, landed instantly — the header read "Showing 1 result from all folders for “surface”") plus a rename; each ask dialog carried the manifest description verbatim; Apply persisted the rename through the thunk and `files.files` confirmed it. The mid-run guard fired for real and unscripted: the first rename attempt hit `active_file_name refused: no file is open in the preview pane. Open the file first (active_file_id) and try again.`, the agent quoted it back and recovered by calling `active_file_id` — the refusal text doing its job as a repair instruction. "Keep as is" returned `{ok:false, declined:true}`, the agent reported the decline and did not retry, and the file was byte-identical in the DB. The undeclared visibility ask was refused with the agent enumerating **exactly** the nine offered targets. Forced invalid values came back verbatim — `view_mode expects one of: list | grid.`, `kind_filter expects one of: all | folders | files.`, `list_sort.sort_by expects one of: name | type | … | created_at.` — with nothing applied. Error Inspector **in the same browser session as a valid run: zero `surface-writeback` captures**; the invalid-value load manufactured three, which is the seam working and the control that makes the zero mean something. `pnpm check:surface-drift` green (142 surfaces, 3522 values) and `pnpm type-check` clean (the `features/brokers/*` generated-RPC errors that were the pre-existing baseline earlier in the day are gone from `main`). **Collision checks** before writing and again before commit: no `writeTargets` on latest `origin/main`, no files/folders/storage branch in the FULL `git ls-remote --heads origin` list, and no `agent.review_queue` row covering this surface. DB mirror sync still pending.
- **2026-08-12 — Image Studio gains its third target, `image_description`; THE per-FILE addressing reference, and a worked example of REOPENING a documented "not writable" decision.** `matrx-user/image-studio` already shipped `conversion_settings` + `selected_presets` (2026-08-10/11). This adds the one the manifest had explicitly ruled out: the per-image authored metadata — `alt_text` / `caption` / `title` / `description` / `keywords` / `filename_base` — landing through the same `updateImageMetadata` the Metadata panel's own inputs call. **The reopening is the transferable part.** That docblock gave two reasons to withhold it, and exactly one survived scrutiny. The real one was structural: there was no read twin, because `source_files` carried `metadata_status` and nothing else, and a write nobody can verify from a read value is not worth declaring. That was FIXED rather than waived — `source_files` now carries all six authored fields (empty until something writes them), so an agent reads what is there before writing what is missing; the `quick-note-save` `all_folder_names` move, applied to rows instead of to a vocabulary. The second reason — "this surface already has a dedicated path, the `image-studio-describe-01` shortcut" — argued the OPPOSITE way once examined: pressing that shortcut is a COMMIT (it spends a model call and uploads a preview into the user's cloud library), while writing the text is not. An existing human button for a value is a reason to give the agent the direct authoring path, not to withhold it. **THE per-file rule.** This is the campaign's first target whose value must name WHICH ROW it lands on, on a surface whose rows have no durable id — studio sources are browser-local `File` objects until "Save to library" mints `cld_files` rows. So it carries `file`, matched case-insensitively against the `name` / `filename_base` the surface already reports, and throws listing the real filenames on a miss or a tie (`image_description.file: no source image called "nonexistent-photo.jpg". Files currently in the studio: "harbor-sunrise.png" (filename_base "harbor-sunrise").`). It resolves that against `filesRef.current`, assigned **during render** rather than in the shell's existing sync effect, because the identifying strings change WITHOUT the file count changing — the effect's deps would not fire for a rename. That is the seam's ordering trap in its sharpest form yet: unlike the two conversion targets, this handler genuinely reads page state to decide where a value lands, an agent legitimately calls it once per image in ONE turn, every closure is resolved before the first dialog is confirmed, and a `filename_base` write renames the very string the next lookup matches on. Validation lives in a pure module (`features/image-studio/lib/image-studio-write-targets.ts`) while its two siblings validate inline — deliberately, because it is the only rule here that must RESOLVE a row before it can check anything, and the throw has to land synchronously where the seam catches it rather than inside a React updater. **`dominant_colors` is the instructive NO:** it sits inside the very same `ImageMetadata` object as the text fields, the describe shortcut fills it in, and it is still rejected BY NAME — those hex codes are measured off the pixels by a model that can SEE the image, so an agent working from the surface scope would be inventing swatches. The one field in the group that is observed rather than authored, and worth copying as a test: "is this value authored, or is it an observation that merely lives beside authored ones?" Live-verified with a real Badass Agent run on a generated 1200×800 PNG: the ask dialog carried the manifest description verbatim, Apply landed alt text, caption, title, meta description, six keyword chips and a new filename slug into the visible Metadata panel (the panel's action flipping from "Describe all with AI" to "Re-describe all with AI") with nothing generated and nothing saved; two deliberately invalid calls came back to the agent verbatim as the handler's own throws — the unknown-file one above, and ``image_description does not accept `dominant_colors`. Those hex codes are measured off the image's pixels by the Describe with AI action, not authored…``; and "Keep as is" declined with no error. Error Inspector on the clean valid-write load: **zero** `surface-writeback` captures — the single capture present is an unrelated `POST /files/upload` network error, the studio's background source auto-save failing through the sandbox's egress proxy. (The invalid-call run produced two `surface-writeback` captures, which is the seam being loud by design.) `check:surface-drift` (142 surfaces, 3,522 values) clean; `type-check` clean for the touched files. DB mirror sync still pending. **Note for the campaign:** this surface landed on `main` from a sibling agent WHILE a second agent was building the same thing. The right resolution was to keep the merged work wholesale — including its `conversion_settings` fit/anchor enforcement, which is stronger than the competing draft's — and add only the genuinely additive target plus the read twin it needed. The competing branch's own vocabulary module was discarded rather than merged, because `constants/conversion-options.ts` already did that job on main. Two `pending` `agent.review_queue` rows already existed on `/images/convert` from that collision; the older one was updated to describe the shipped three-target surface rather than inserting a third.
- **2026-08-12 — Scouting result: the pool of surfaces that can take write targets TODAY is nearly exhausted, and the remaining authored content sits behind MISSING EMITTERS.** Recorded here rather than as fan-out chips because the run that scouted it hit the session lineage-depth limit and could not spawn them; the next agent should treat this as a ready-made assignment list. Ten unclaimed manifests were checked against live code — provider mount, page component, and every declared value cross-referenced to a real visible control and its canonical setter. **Six are disqualified outright for the `agent-advanced-editor` reason: `admin-feedback`, `admin-lookups`, `admin-mcp-servers`, `admin-bundles`, `admin-scheduling`, `admin-users` mount NO `SurfaceRuntimeProvider` anywhere.** Verified two ways — their `create*Scope` helpers have zero call sites outside their own manifest file, and each self-declares `readiness:"stub"` with a note saying no emitter is wired. Declaring `writeTargets` on any of them would produce targets that look done while offering the agent literally no tool. **Of the four that ARE live, three fail the judgment bar and are ruled out:** `marketing-reputation` (`ReputationWorkspace.tsx` mounts the provider, but only ONE YES field — reputation-case lifecycle status via `setCaseStatus` → `useUpdateReputationCase` — and the page contains not a single text `Input`/`Textarea`; every narrative, brief, headline and recommended-action field is rendered read-only); `marketing-authority` (`AuthorityRouterWorkspace.tsx` mounts the provider but has ZERO YES fields — `anchor_text` looks ideal but is display-only through `<Detail>`, the only controls are add-to-link-plan / dismiss buttons, and approval state is local `useState` that persists nothing; the one genuinely authored control, the optional-priority `guidance` Textarea, is not a declared read value); and `share` (every declared value is ids plus permissions and access grants — the exclusion list — and the manifest itself sets `skipBaselineValues` with the comment "Access-management widget — no text/content/selection concept"). **Only `matrx-user/marketing-competitors` remains assignable**, and honestly so: `CompetitorAutopsyWorkspace.tsx` mounts the provider, and it has two real levers behind real buttons — opportunity status (`mutateOpportunity` → `updateOpportunityStatus`, RPC `seo.update_competitor_opportunity_status`) and competitor tracking (`mutateTracking` → `updateCompetitorTracking`, RPC `seo.update_competitor_tracking`) — which is a LENIENT two, because both are enum status transitions rather than authored prose. Its stronger play needs one extra step: the page has real authored run inputs (a competitor-domains Textarea plus max-competitors and pages-per-competitor selects) that are not yet declared read values, so adding the twin and declaring ONE composite `autopsy_request` target would follow the `image-generate` `generation_request` / `image-studio` `conversion_settings` shape, with firing the run left to the human. **The generalizable finding is where the good fields actually are.** The six disqualified admin pages are precisely the ones holding rich, human-editable, agent-draftable content — bundle name + description (`BundlesAdminPage.tsx`), label/description pairs (`LookupsAdminPage.tsx`), MCP server config (`AddMcpServerDialog.tsx`), announcement title/type/body (`CreateAnnouncementDialog.tsx`). They are blocked purely on emitter wiring, not on field quality. So the highest-value remaining work in this campaign is no longer `surface-write-targets` on live surfaces — it is `surface-authoring` to mount `SurfaceRuntimeProvider` on `admin-bundles` and `admin-lookups` FIRST, then write targets on top. An agent picking up this campaign should assign that two-phase job rather than hunt for another live surface; there is roughly one left.

- **2026-08-12 — `matrx-user/image-studio` gains a THIRD target, `filename_base`; a collision-checked partial rename map, and a worked example of REVISITING another pass's documented NO.** The 2026-08-11 pass shipped `selected_presets` + `conversion_settings` and explicitly declined per-file names, grouping them with the AI metadata and blocking both on "no read twin to close the evidence loop — `source_files` carries only `metadata_status`, and files are addressable solely by a browser-local name". That reasoning holds for the METADATA and is left standing, but it does not hold for the NAME, and the difference is checkable rather than a matter of taste: `source_files` already emits BOTH `name` (the original upload filename) and `filename_base` on every entry — its own value description says so — so the evidence loop closes exactly the way every other target's does, and `updatesValue: "source_files"` is a real twin. The browser-local-id problem IS real, but it is a HANDLER problem and is solved there instead of by declining the target: the handler resolves whichever coordinate the agent actually saw — 1-based POSITION in `source_files`, the original `name`, or the current `filename_base` — back to the internal file id, and refuses a key matching no image or MORE than one rather than guessing, so an agent can address a file that has no durable id without the surface inventing one. Nor is it a duplicate of the existing `image-studio-describe-01` shortcut: that is a PAID model call the user presses which writes name + alt + caption together, while this renames only, for free, under an ask dialog. **It earns the work because the base is load-bearing twice over** — it is the slug of every variant the image produces AND the per-image subfolder a save writes into — and because the shell already interposes a rename banner that blocks the first Generate whenever files still carry auto-derived names like `img-4821`, so this target answers a nag the page itself raises. Validation follows `workbooks`' `workbook_sheet_names` shape: the WHOLE map is validated before the first rename lands, so a map with one bad entry renames nothing. Collisions are checked against the RESULT of applying the whole map (swapping two images' bases is legal; colliding with an image the map leaves alone is not), and only a duplicate the map is PARTY to is refused — a pre-existing collision between two untouched images is the user's own state, not this call's business. Bases are rejected, never coerced: `slugifyFilename` silently falls back to `"image"` for input with no alphanumerics and truncates at 60, so the handler checks the UN-truncated slug and throws on both, and refuses any `"."` because the slugifier would strip a trailing `.v2` as an extension. `FILENAME_BASE_MAX_CHARS` moved onto `utils/slugify-filename.ts` beside the function that applies it, and the manifest interpolates that same constant, so the bound the agent is told and the bound enforced cannot drift. The handler is registered in `ImageStudioShell`'s existing `getWriteHandlers` alongside the other two and calls the SAME `setFilenameBase(fileId, base)` a file card's inline rename field calls; it reads the live file list through a new `filesRef` for the reason the 2026-08-11 entry documents (the seam resolves every handler closure BEFORE the user confirms, so a rename staged against a render-snapshot list could hit the wrong image), and refuses while a conversion or a save is in flight because the base is baked into the variants and the folder already moving. Live-verified with a real Badass Agent run on `/images/convert` with two seeded images: the ask dialog carried the manifest description verbatim, Apply renamed both cards in place (`img-4821`/`dsc-0007` → `terracotta-wall-texture`/`cobalt-solid-swatch`, with the "auto-named" badges and the rename banner disappearing), "Keep as is" on the same target left both bases untouched and the agent reported the decline without an error, a deliberate both-images-to-one-base call came back to the agent as the handler's own throw verbatim (`filename_base: applying these renames would leave 2 images with the base "duplicate-name", and their variants would overwrite each other. Give each image a distinct base.`) with nothing renamed, and asking to Save to library / run AI Describe produced NO tool call at all — the agent enumerated the targets it had and explained both buttons are the user's. A forced `output_format: "heic"` likewise returned `conversion_settings: output_format must be one of webp | avif | jpeg | png` verbatim, confirming the sibling target still behaves. Error Inspector on a clean load (valid applies + a decline, no forced invalid value): **zero** `surface-writeback` captures — the only two entries were sandbox `POST /files/upload` network failures from the egress proxy. `check:surface-drift` clean (142 surfaces, 3,526 values); `type-check` shows only pre-existing failures in `features/assists`, `features/canvas` and `features/marketing/content-plan`, none in the touched files. DB mirror still pending. **`matrx-user/image-edit` was independently re-assessed and its NO is CONFIRMED, not overturned** — see that manifest's WRITE DOCTRINE. The tempting target is the AI edit prompt, and a handler for it does "work" in the sense that `promptText`/`setPromptText` are real state; but the whole popover renders behind `IMAGE_STUDIO_BACKEND_CAPABILITIES.promptEdit`, which is a hardcoded `false`, so the value would land in a control the user can never see, edit, or run. Of what remains rendered, `save_folder` is a mount PROP with no editor anywhere (the `admin-tool-registry` `tool_group` one-way-door case), mask painting is a canvas gesture, and the rest is identity or status. The one field worth flagging for whoever revisits it: `image_file_name` IS authored naming with a real canonical path (`renameFile`, the same thunk the header's inline rename field dispatches, extension locked) and is the closest thing this surface has to a second YES — it was left undeclared here only because one target is under the floor and the prompt target cannot ship while `promptEdit` is false.
- **2026-08-12 — `matrx-admin/lookups` was ALREADY BUILT AND STRANDED on an unmerged branch; landed unchanged and independently re-verified. The campaign lesson: "my code auto-merges" is an assumption, not a guarantee — CHECK THE BRANCH, not just `main`.** A second agent drew the lookups chip, ran the collision check the skill mandates, and found `writeTargets` absent from `main` — the all-clear that normally means "nobody has done this." It was wrong. `git ls-remote` showed `claude/surface-write-admin-lookups` already existed, carrying a COMPLETE, live-verified implementation (the entry below) plus its `agent.review_queue` row, pushed ~20 hours earlier and never merged, while `main` moved 40+ commits past it. **The manifest check alone would have greenlit a full re-implementation of finished work** — the exact waste this campaign keeps paying for, and the reason the branch probe is not optional garnish on the `main` probe. Per the collision rule the existing design WON outright: nothing was re-authored, no competing target set was written, and the two original commits were cherry-picked onto current `main` with authorship intact. The `features/surfaces/FEATURE.md` conflict landed exactly where the two prior incidents said it would, and was resolved by keeping BOTH sides with a dated-entry count before and after (118 → 119, zero of `main`'s entries dropped) rather than taking either side wholesale. **Re-verified against today's `main` rather than trusted, because nobody had ever run this code there:** `type-check` is now fully CLEAN (0 errors — the 11 pre-existing `features/brokers/*` errors cited in the entry below have since been fixed on `main`), `check:surface-drift` clean at 142 surfaces, and a fresh Badass Agent run reproduced every claim — the ask dialog carried the manifest description verbatim with the per-tab patterns interpolated from `lookupsVocabulary.ts`, Apply auto-opened a New UI Client form with `matrx-tablet` + its description in the REAL inputs (read out of `input.value`, which never appears in `innerText`) while leaving Sort order at its default, "Keep as is" returned a decline the agent acknowledged without an error, a sort-order + Active request produced NO tool call and handed the clicks back, and a forced `Bad Name With Spaces!!` came back verbatim as `… is not a valid name for a ui.ui_client row. Required: lowercase letters, digits and hyphens, starting with a letter (e.g. \`matrx-mobile\`)` with nothing staged and NO stray dialog. Error Inspector on a clean load (valid apply + a decline, no forced-invalid value): **"No errors captured" — zero of any kind.** `ui.ui_client` still holds its original 6 rows and zero test rows, confirmed by SQL — `draft` never touched the database. The stranded branch was left untouched (never force-push over another agent's work); this landed from a distinct branch. **If your work matters, verify it actually reached `main` — a green push is not a merge.**

- **2026-08-12 — `matrx-user/marketing-findings` follow-up: the `open` half of `finding_lifecycle_status` wrote the WRONG status and then reported failure — plus the user twin it was missing.** The target above shipped `acknowledged | open`, and the `open` branch routed through `reopenFinding`, which writes `status: "reopened"`. So "undo the acknowledgement" stored `reopened` in `web.finding`, and the handler's own landing check (`updated.status !== next`) then threw `did not land — the finding is still reopened` — **a write that corrupted the row and told the agent it had failed, in one call.** `unacknowledgeFinding` is the canonical verb for `open` and was simply never imported; one line. **Why it survived a live verification:** the shipped run exercised suppress, acknowledge, a decline, `resolved`, a malformed suppression and a severity refusal — every path except the one value whose routing was wrong. A target's vocabulary is not verified until EVERY member of it has been written live; enumerating the enum is the cheap check that would have caught this. **The second gap was the read twin.** The detail header renders "I'm on it" and nothing else, so an agent could propose `open` on a page where the user had no control to set or undo it — a write the user can watch land and cannot reverse where they are standing. The header now renders "Not on it after all" while a finding is `acknowledged`, calling the same `unacknowledgeFinding`; it is the exact verb the shared row menu `analysis/finding-actions.ts` already defines (that builder has **zero consumers** — its verbs exist in code and nowhere in the product, which is a separate thread worth pulling). The first pushed `b10b0d17f` at 03:28 UTC while the second was mid-verification on an independent design (a 4-value `finding_status` plus a same-named `finding_suppression`). The pushed design won on the skill's rule — two target sets over the same fields is a defect, not a merge — and the second design was discarded rather than reconciled, leaving only what was genuinely additive: this fix, the twin, and the doc corrections.

- **2026-08-11 — Marketing findings register agent-writable; THE reference for letting an EXISTING ownership line pick the target set.** `matrx-user/marketing-findings` declares 2 ask-policy `mode:"entity"` targets on the finding DETAIL route: `finding_suppression` — one `{suppressed: boolean, reason?: string}` object, because suppressing and lifting are one decision with a direction and the reason is inseparable from the act — and `finding_lifecycle_status` (`acknowledged | open`). Handlers in `features/marketing/components/analysis/FindingWriteTargets.tsx`, registered with `useSurfaceWriteHandlers` and mounted by `FindingDetail` inside its own provider; they call `suppressFinding` / `unsuppressFinding` / `acknowledgeFinding` / `unacknowledgeFinding` — the same `finding-mutations.ts` functions the header's "I'm on it" button and the remedy card's suppress dialog call. **The target set was not chosen by counting inputs; it was already written down.** `finding-mutations.ts` opens by drawing the line: the analyzer owns detection (open / refresh / resolve / reopen) and never touches suppression, and the user owns judgement — "I've seen this" and "this is intentional, stop telling me". Those two verbs are the surface's ENTIRE write vocabulary, so declaring both covers it completely and there was nothing to pad with: severity and category are the catalogue's, `analysis_result` is immutable evidence, and `resolved`/`reopened` would forge the analyzer's verdict. The manifest's intro had ALREADY told agents to recommend a suppression and leave the decision to the user; `applyPolicy: "ask"` is precisely that sentence once an agent can propose it, so the intro now names the two targets instead of only describing the etiquette. **Vocabulary promoted to a pure module:** the finding-status words lived as a re-typed literal in `AnalysisBadges.tsx` AND as a second literal `Set` in `analysis-service.ts`; both now read `features/marketing/data/finding-lifecycle.ts`, which also owns the validators and the `500`-character reason bound the manifest interpolates into its own description — so what the agent is told, what the handler accepts, and what the filters render cannot drift. **Per-mount split:** `FindingsTable` mounts this same surface on the list route and registers NO handlers, so `listAgentWritableTargets()` offers an agent no write tool there (the `schedules` posture). **Optimistic-write guard:** every canonical mutation RETURNS the row the DB actually holds, and each handler re-checks that row and throws when the value did not land, so an RLS-filtered update can never be reported to the agent as done; the current row is read through a REF, not the render closure, because the seam resolves handlers BEFORE the confirm dialog and both dialogs queue when an agent applies both targets in one message. Transition guards mirror the page's own controls (acknowledge only from open/reopened, undo only from acknowledged). Live-verified with a real Badass Agent run against a throwaway finding created and deleted for the test: ONE message asking for both changes produced two per-target ask dialogs carrying the manifest prose verbatim, Apply persisted both (`suppressed=true` with the exact reason, `status=acknowledged`, version 3, confirmed by SQL) with the read twins updating on screen — Suppressed + Acknowledged badges and the `Suppression: …` line; "Keep as is" on a follow-up unsuppress declined with no error and the state untouched; `finding_lifecycle_status: "resolved"` came back to the model as the validator's own throw verbatim (`"resolved" is written by the analysis pipeline, not from this page. Only acknowledged | open may be set here.`) and `{suppressed: "yes please"}` as `suppressed is required and must be a boolean`, both validate-then-apply with nothing written; asking to change the finding's SEVERITY produced no tool call at all and the agent named the four targets actually available to it (these two plus the inherited `site_name` / `site_description`). Error Inspector on the CLEAN all-valid session: **zero** `surface-writeback` captures with the "No errors captured" banner; the probe session's captures were the deliberately induced ones, in their own browser session. `check:surface-drift` (140 surfaces, 3,411 values) + `type-check` clean. DB mirror (`ui.ui_surface_write_target`) still pending a manifest sync.
- **2026-08-11 — Content Plan Entities: `source_type_id` unblocked by declaring its vocabulary, `open_entity_editor` closes the modal precondition trap, and a draft-LOSING modal bug fixed.** Builds on the reconciliation entry below rather than redoing it: that pass restored the docs and correctly deleted `lib/entity-write-targets.ts` as debris, because at the time nothing imported it and it certified a superseded contract. The module is back with a narrower one — TWO validators, both imported by `EntityManager.tsx`, both covering the only checks that need LIVE page state rather than a constant (an offered category id, a live entity id), with 12 tests. **`source_type_id` is now writable, and the how generalizes.** It was excluded for exactly the reason `content-plan-node` still keeps `node_primary_keyword_id` manual — a category UUID with no options exposed — and the fix is to DECLARE THE VOCABULARY, not to loosen the check: the new `source_type_options` value publishes `{id, name}` from the same `useCategories(CATEGORY_DIMENSIONS.planSourceType)` read the dialog's `CategorySelect` renders from, and the handler refuses any id absent from it, including while the dimension is still loading, because an empty list cannot distinguish a dangling FK from a real one. **The `autoContext` detail is the trap worth stealing:** shipped first as `autoContext: false` ("look it up if you need it"), the live run came back `2 of 2 ctx_batch sub-request(s) failed: 'source_type_options' is not attached to the current turn` and the agent — correctly — refused to guess a UUID. A vocabulary that a target's description tells the agent to read must be ATTACHED, not fetch-on-demand; it is `autoContext: true` now. **`open_entity_editor`** (`mode:"ui"`, `auto`) answers the open question the adopter clause left pending: `entity_draft` needs an open editor, the editor is modal and its overlay covers the composer, so only a human could open one and an agent that spotted a bad label had nowhere to put the fix. It takes an id from `entities_detail` or null for a blank dialog, validates against the live roster, and lets the agent do the open and the stage in ONE turn. **The defect every draft-into-a-modal surface has until it does this:** applying a staged draft DISMISSED the editor it was staging into. `ConfirmDialogHost` portals its alertdialog as a sibling of the editor `Dialog`, so Radix counted the Apply click as an interact-outside, ran `onOpenChange(false)` → `onClose()` → `setDraftState(null)`, and threw the draft away while the agent was told `ok: true`. Caught only because the run read the input's value back off the DOM instead of trusting the envelope — an `ok: true` is not evidence. Fixed with an `onInteractOutside` guard that `preventDefault()`s when the originating event sits inside a `[role="alertdialog"]`. Live-verified with real Badass Agent runs on cosmeticinjectables.com: `add_entities` persisted a row; `open_entity_editor` applied silently as `auto`; `entity_draft` carrying a real category id staged through and, after Apply, the editor was STILL OPEN with the label staged and the Source type picker reading "Study / Research Paper" — the picker resolving the UUID the agent sent, which is the entire point of publishing the options; a forced `{"source_type_id":"11111111-…"}` came back to the agent verbatim as ``entity_draft: "11111111-…" is not one of this workspace's plan_source_type category ids. Pick an id from the surface's source_type_options value (match on its name), or send null.`` with the picker uncoerced. Error Inspector on the clean run: **zero** captures of any kind. Both test entities were deleted afterwards, restoring the roster to empty. `check:surface-drift` (140 surfaces, 3,411 values) + `jest` clean; `type-check` clean in the touched files, the remaining 11 errors pre-existing and all in `features/brokers/*`. DB mirror still pending.

- **2026-08-12 — `matrx-user/marketing-competitors` is agent-writable — and was not a surface at all until today.** Assigned as a write-target chip for `matrx-user/rag-library`; that surface was ranked and **declined on the judgment bar** (see the rag-library note at the end of this entry), and the work moved here. **The blocking discovery: `marketing-competitors.manifest.ts` existed, claimed `readiness: "verified"`, and was NEVER ADDED TO `RAW_MANIFESTS` in `manifests/registry.ts`** — one of only two unregistered manifests in the directory (145 files, 142 registered). `getManifest()` therefore returned nothing, so `listLiveWriteTargets()` skipped the surface entirely and no `apply_surface_write` tool was ever offered. The first live agent run produced **zero** confirm dialogs with a fully-correct manifest and fully-correct handlers, which is exactly what a broken target looks like; the registry line is the fix (drift check 142 → 143 surfaces). Worth generalizing: a manifest file is not a surface, and `check:surface-drift` validates what is REGISTERED, so it cannot catch this. **The targets (3, all `ask`):** `autopsy_run_plan` is ONE `draft` object bundling `competitor_domains` + `max_competitors` + `pages_per_competitor` — they are one decision ("who do we look at, and how much do we buy looking at them") submitted by one button, and splitting them lets an agent name five rivals while a stale budget silently discards four; every key is optional so narrowing the domain list does not force restating budgets. `competitor_tracking` and `opportunity_status` are `entity` targets through the canonical `seo.update_competitor_tracking` / `seo.update_competitor_opportunity_status` RPCs, so an agent's triage is indistinguishable from a row-action click, `human_ruling` provenance included. **The line: RUNNING the autopsy is never a target.** It spends DataForSEO provider calls, crawls `max_competitors × pages_per_competitor` pages of someone else's site and runs an LLM strategist — the run-history tab on this page carries a `reported_cost` column, so the spend is not hypothetical. Same line as `image-generate` at Generate, `scraper` at Scrape, `marketing-crawls` at starting a crawl, `matrx-admin/email` at Send, `matrx-user/keyword-research` at the Research button. Also undeclared: `site_id` (tenancy identity, and it redirects WHERE the paid run points — the failure mode where a user presses a button they already reviewed and buys evidence about the wrong property) and `force_refresh` (its only axis is spend; it has a read value so an agent can still reason about the plan). Everything downstream of a run — provider metrics, verdicts, recommended actions, `latest_autopsy`, run history — stays read-only EVIDENCE; only the human-status column of those rows is writable, which is the page's own footer promise ("Every provider fact, crawl observation, AI judgment, and human status remains separate") enforced in code. **THE SELECTION-SCOPED PATTERN, which is the reusable half:** `applySurfaceWrite` resolves EVERY handler closure BEFORE the user confirms the first dialog, so a handler that picks its row from a render closure can act on a stale snapshot and change a row the user cannot see. Both entity targets therefore carry their row id IN the value, and the handler resolves the visible rows through a REF at apply time; an id not currently loaded refuses the WHOLE write and reports the loaded count rather than doing a best-effort partial. A run in flight refuses all three (a finishing run replaces exactly these rows, and it disables the button the staged plan exists to feed). **New canonical vocabulary module `features/marketing/competitors/autopsy-controls.ts`** (mirroring `scrape-command.ts` and `rag/search-controls.ts`) owns the two status enums, the run bounds and the domain normalizer, and is runtime-dependency-free; the manifest INTERPOLATES it into contract prose, the handlers validate against it, and the run form's two selects render from it — so the enum an agent is told about, the one its value is checked against, and the one the UI renders cannot drift. Validators live in that PURE module rather than inside the React updaters, because a throw raised inside a `setState` callback fires during React's commit instead of inside the writeback seam's try/catch, and the agent would read a success envelope for a value that never landed. **Two real pre-existing bugs fell out of building that module** — see the marketing FEATURE.md entry: the UI sent `tracking_status: "tracking"`, which the CHECK constraint and the RPC both reject, so **the Track row action failed on every click**, and the footer's "N competitors currently tracked" counted the same non-existent value and always read 0. Fixed to the server's `tracked` at all three sites. **Live-verified** with a real Badass Agent run on `/marketing/competitors`: one message asking for three changes produced three confirm dialogs, each rendering this manifest's own contract prose verbatim with the correct per-mode footer (`It is staged for you to review — nothing is saved until you save.` on the draft, `This is saved immediately.` on the two entity targets); Apply staged `shredit.com` / `ironmountain.com` with budgets 4 and 2 into the real form, and persisted the opportunity to `accepted` (timestamp matched the click to the second); **Keep as is** declined the competitor write with no error and the row stayed `candidate`; asking to switch site, enable `force_refresh` and run the autopsy was refused (none is a declared target); and a deliberately invalid `max_competitors: 9` + `competitor_domains: ["Acme Corporation"]` returned the handler's own throw text to the model with nothing staged. Zero page errors. `pnpm check:surface-drift` green (143 surfaces, 3541 values); `type-check` clean for these files against a clean-main baseline. **Collision checks** (before writing and again before commit): no `writeTargets` on latest `main`, `git ls-remote --heads origin` matched no competitors branch, and `agent.review_queue` had no row for `/marketing/competitors`. **Why `matrx-user/rag-library` was declined:** its one hypothesized planning field, `bulkConfirmStatus`, is not a status transition at all — it opens the confirm for `fn_bulk_delete_library_documents`, i.e. it IS the destructive path, and nothing in the app ever writes a document's status (it is derived by the ingest pipeline; one `.update()` exists in the whole library tree). The detail sheet exposes no description, tags or summary — only a rename dialog whose own copy says "The name is the display label only". Four of the surface's five inputs are search/filter boxes (the list one is a NAME substring filter, not retrieval), and the catalog half is browse state plus an entitlement toggle over libraries it states are read-only. Zero YES fields, one borderline; below the ~2 floor, so it does not earn the work.
- **2026-08-12 — `matrx-user/quick-tasks` gains `selected_task_draft`, the live read twin its `panel_*` drafts were missing.** ADDITIVE follow-up to the 8-target design in `9f13bf72` and to the label-vocabulary extraction in `83e3a87b`; **both of those win and are untouched here.** The 8-target entry's own `readinessNote` recorded the gap: "TaskDetailsPanel's UNSAVED field drafts are still not emitted, so a staged value is visible on screen but not readable back through the `selected_task_*` values", and its intro told the agent "do not read them to confirm a stage landed". **Why that is worth closing rather than living with:** three of its target descriptions instruct the agent to read the current value first *if you mean to build on it* (`panel_task_title`, `panel_task_description`, `panel_task_labels`). Follow that instruction after a stage and the flat `selected_task_*` values hand back the PRE-stage text, so a second write built on them silently reverts the agent's own first write — the failure is worst exactly where the advice is followed. `selected_task_draft` emits `{task_id, title, description, due_date, priority, labels, is_dirty}` from the panel's live buffer, published from an EFFECT into a `surfaceDraftRef` the window's `getScope` reads (the `task-create` / `TaskQuickCreateCore` pattern) and **nulled on unmount** — this panel comes and goes while its window stays open, so a stale draft left behind would be emitted as the live edits of a task nobody has open; the emitter additionally guards on `task_id === selected_task_id` to cover the frame between a selection change and the panel's next publish. `is_dirty` is the panel's own Save-button flag, so an agent can tell "staged, unsaved" from "this is what is on the record". No write target was added, renamed, or re-pointed; the three "read X first" sentences now name `selected_task_draft`, and the intro separates the saved values (what the user would lose) from the draft (what is staged). Live-verified on the rebased tree with real Badass Agent runs on the open window: the ask dialog carried the edited description verbatim, Apply staged title + description into the real inputs (description 0 → 1,672 chars) and armed the panel's Save bar with nothing persisted, a separate run staged due date + priority + labels together (due `""` → `2026-08-28`, priority Medium → High), "Keep as is" left the panel byte-identical and returned a clean decline, and asks to save / complete / delete were refused with the agent naming the limits out of the target prose. **NOT closed, stated honestly:** no run got an agent to quote `selected_task_draft` back. Three attempts were defeated by a pre-existing runtime race — a run launched from the header **binds to the ROUTE surface (`matrx-user/dashboard`) instead of this overlay**, and the agent then truthfully reports it cannot see any Quick Tasks value or target; a fourth, multi-turn attempt died on a platform "This agent's tool configuration is inconsistent and can't run" error on the SECOND turn. **Both reproduce on unmodified `main`** (the conversation-id collision below was confirmed by stashing all local changes and re-running), so neither is caused by this change, but the read-back leg of the evidence loop is verified by construction and type-check only, not by a model. Two more runtime notes for whoever picks this up: a stray click landing on page chrome BEHIND the floating window starts its own agent run, after which the next launch dies with "Conversation already exists" and Retry cannot fix it (it re-sends the same id) — scope every automated click to the window's bounds; and tasks created through the quick-add box vanish from the sidebar after a refetch because `selectFilteredTasks` filters by the APP-CONTEXT org, not the window's selected org, so a task created under any other org is invisible. `check:surface-drift` clean (142 surfaces, 3527 values); `type-check` shows no errors in the touched files (the failures on `main` are pre-existing, in `features/assists`, `features/canvas` and `features/marketing`). **Process note for the campaign:** this surface was scouted as unclaimed (`writeTargets` absent on `main` at `1240fc91`, re-checked immediately before implementation) and a full competing 2-target design — one composite `selected_task_edits` plus `new_task_title`, with pane-gated refusals and a pure validation core — was built and live-verified before `9f13bf72` appeared on `origin/main`. It was parked, the branch reset onto `main`, and only the additive remainder reapplied; then the same thing happened AGAIN with the label extraction, which had been pushed onto this very branch name while the rebase was in flight, and that side won too. **The `writeTargets`-presence test is necessary but not sufficient — it cannot see a design that is finished but unpushed, and it cannot see a sibling pushing to the branch you are standing on.** A `git fetch` immediately before committing, and a `git push` that is allowed to FAIL rather than force, are what caught both.

- **2026-08-12 — `matrx-user/quick-tasks` was ALREADY DONE on `main`; the additive contribution is killing the re-typed label enum in TWO manifests. No competing target set was written.** Assigned as a write-target chip that scouted the surface as having no `writeTargets`. It has eight, and they landed before this task started (`agent.review_queue` row `128e59b2`, tasks FEATURE.md `2026-08-10`) — **the collision the skill's trap section warns about, caught by STEP ZERO rather than mid-flight.** The landed design was checked against the chip's own brief and answers every question it posed, so **it wins outright and nothing was re-implemented**: the composite-vs-atomic call is made (`quick_create_task` takes one `{title, description?, priority?, due_date?}` object, matching the `task-create` precedent, while `quick_add_title` stays atomic because it stages into a box the user submits); the panel edit path is covered (`panel_task_*` drafts + `panel_add_subtasks`); org/scope/project selection and `selected_task_id` are ruled out BY NAME with the `task-create` reasoning (the window emits selected ids but no pick lists, so an agent cannot name a legal value); `search_query` / `show_all_projects` are ruled out as mechanical view state; delete and complete-toggle stay human; and both campaign gotchas are handled — `quick_create_task` says "Pass the object itself — not a JSON string" for the inline-tool JSON-parse trap, `QuickTasksWorkspace` reads org/project/scopes via `store.getState()` at call time rather than off its render closure, and saving is deliberately NOT a target precisely because `TaskDetailsPanel`'s draft is local React state that a stale closure would clobber. **What WAS genuinely additive: the label vocabulary.** `TASK_LABEL_OPTIONS` lived in `features/tasks/services/taskService.ts`, which reaches supabase, the files handler, the comments service and the scopes service. `manifests/registry.ts` imports all 142 manifests and is itself imported by the agent execution system's client-capability providers, so **no manifest can name that constant without dragging the service graph into every authenticated route** — verified as a house convention, not a guess: zero manifests import from any `services/` module. Both task manifests therefore re-typed the eight labels into their agent-facing prose (`quick-tasks` `panel_task_labels`, `tasks` `task_labels`) while both handlers validated against the real constant — a silent divergence where adding a label hides it from agents and removing one leaves agents proposing a value the handler rejects. Fixed the way `constants/priority.ts` already documents as the pattern (its docblock explicitly says it is React-free *so that surface manifests can spell the vocabulary out from the real constant*): the set moved to `features/tasks/constants/labels.ts`, free of React, icons and services, exporting `TASK_LABEL_OPTIONS` + a `TASK_LABELS` value tuple; `taskService` re-exports it so all six component import sites are untouched; both manifests now interpolate `TASK_LABELS.join(" | ")`, producing prose **byte-identical** to the literals removed. **Independent live re-verification of the landed work** (real Badass Agent runs on the open window, one scenario per run): `quick_add_title` raised an ask dialog carrying the manifest description verbatim and Apply put "Prepare Q3 security audit" into the real quick-add input; `panel_task_labels` carried the now constant-derived vocabulary and the agent reasoned over that exact set before applying (`"Nothing in the set is a dedicated 'security' label"`, `Ok true`, mode `draft`); `panel_task_description` staged a paragraph and the panel's Save bar appeared; `panel_task_due_date` answered "Keep as is" with a clean `Ok false / Declined true / The user declined the change to "Task priority"`-shaped envelope and the agent did not retry; a forced invalid `["security","urgent"]` returned the handler's throw verbatim and the agent confirmed it "never reached a staged/approval state" (validate-then-apply); and a forced undeclared target returned `No mounted surface declares write target "panel_task_status"` with **no dialog raised**. Error Inspector on a clean load (valid apply + decline only, no forced invalid): **0 distinct · 0 total occurrences**. `check:surface-drift` green (142 surfaces, 3526 values). `type-check` is unchanged by this work: 8 errors before and after, none in any touched file — all pre-existing on `main` in `features/assists/`, `features/canvas/maps/` and `features/marketing/content-plan/`. **Two reusable notes for the next agent verifying an OVERLAY surface.** (1) `quickTasksWindow` is NOT reachable from `?panels=quick_tasks` (URL panel hydration is off) and the header menu's "Quick Task" opens a DIFFERENT overlay — `quickTasks`, the sheet, which mounts `TaskDetailsPanel` **without** `writeSurfaceName` and so registers no handlers at all. The live mount is the tools grid → **Dupes** tab → **Tasks** tile. Verifying on the sheet would have shown zero write targets and looked exactly like a broken surface. (2) The floating Agent window's layout is restored SERVER-side, so a previous run's window — and its `conversation_id` — comes back even in a brand-new browser profile; clicking Run then re-launches that conversation and the backend rejects it with `Conversation already exists`, which presents as a run that produces **zero ask dialogs**. Close existing Agent windows before starting a run. Both cost real runs here.
