It is the surface's only authored input and the classic staged run-input (`marketing-crawls`, `image-generate`, `chat-voice`): the note is staged, nothing persists, and the user presses run. Handler is registered by `AuthorityRouterWorkspace` (which owns the state) and calls the same `setGuidance` the textarea's `onChange` calls. Validation lives in a pure core (`features/marketing/authority/authority-write-targets.ts`) so the throw lands synchronously in `applySurfaceWrite`'s try/catch rather than inside a React updater, and `AUTHORITY_GUIDANCE_MAX_CHARS` is imported by the Textarea's `maxLength`, interpolated into the manifest description and enforced in the handler — one vocabulary, three consumers, no drift. The empty string is explicitly legal and means "clear the note", because guidance is optional by design. The run-status guard reads a **ref advanced every render**, not a render closure: `applySurfaceWrite` resolves the handler BEFORE showing the confirm dialog, so a guard closing over rendered state can act on a status that went stale while the dialog sat open (the `image-studio` trap, recorded on `chat-voice`). A new read twin `authority_guidance` closes the evidence loop. **A single-target adoption, deliberately**, on the `matrx-admin/applications` precedent — a surface can be worth exactly one target — because the rest of this page is a report: running the analysis spends model budget over backlinks, the crawl graph, Search Console and the content plan and the human press stays the gate; the map/routes/evidence toggle is mechanical view state; and `authority_summary` / `authority_pages` / `authority_candidates` / `authority_recommendations` are the RECORD of what the analysis computed, so writing them would forge the finding instead of changing it — an agent moves those by writing guidance and letting the user re-run. **The generalizable lesson is the deferral.** Two triage targets were designed, built and then REMOVED before commit: dismissing a proposed route and adding one to the link plan both clear the judgment bar (the `marketing-findings` suppress/acknowledge shape) and both already have canonical server actions (`dismissAuthorityRecommendation`, `addAuthorityRecommendationToPlan`, the exact calls the Dismiss and "Add to link plan" buttons make). They could not be LIVE-VERIFIED: `seo.collection_run`'s RLS is `created_by = auth.uid() OR iam.has_access(...)`, the only run carrying recommendations belongs to another user, and the only run the test account owns completed with zero recommendations — so the page renders "Your authority map has not been calculated yet" and the success path can never be exercised. Manufacturing the data would have meant either spending real model budget on a client's site or inserting a fabricated run row; neither is a verification. Shipping them unverified is exactly what this skill forbids, so they were cut and the reasoning written into the manifest docblock for whoever holds the right credentials. **The aborted attempt did leave one durable finding**, which is why the guard shape is recorded: on the first live run the dismiss handler correctly refused with `the authority result has not loaded on this page yet. This is not the same as "no recommendations"` — a null `result` is lazily-loaded ABSENCE, not emptiness, and a guard that conflates them makes the target silently no-op. Live-verified on `/marketing/brands/…/sites/…/authority` with three real Badass Agent runs. Clean run: one message asked for the note plus two undeclared things; the single ask dialog carried the manifest description verbatim, Apply landed the exact text in the real textarea, and the agent refused both extras in its own words — "Running the authority analysis is deliberately gated to you … I have no tool that triggers the run" and "Switching to the Evidence tab is view state … there's no write target exposed to me". Error Inspector on that same session: **"No errors captured yet", zero `surface-writeback` captures.** Decline run: "Keep as is" left the note byte-unchanged (`""`), the agent acknowledged without an error ("You declined the change, so I've left the Optional priority note as-is"), zero captures. Invalid run: a forced numeric `12345` came back to the model word-for-word as `authority_guidance must be a string containing the priority note to stage, got number.` with **nothing staged** (validate-then-apply), producing exactly 2 captures — the control that makes the zero mean something. `check:surface-drift` green (142 surfaces, 3,521 values); `type-check` fully clean. DB mirror sync still pending. **Collision checks** before writing and again before commit: no `writeTargets` on latest `origin/main`, no authority branch in the FULL `git ls-remote --heads origin` list, no `agent.review_queue` row covering it.
- **2026-08-12 — The two-branch collision on `matrx-user/sandboxes`, reconciled: the targets SHIP, the "ruled out" comment is gone, and the objection that produced it is closed in code rather than overruled.** THE COLLISION, because the record is worth more to the campaign than either branch was. Two agents assessed this surface in parallel and reached OPPOSITE conclusions; **both merged**, and for a while `sandboxes.manifest.ts` carried a ~70-line "NO `writeTargets`, DELIBERATELY (2026-08-12) … Do not re-assign this surface" block directly above two live, declared, handler-backed write targets — while this file's Change Log carried both "RULED OUT … no write targets shipped" and "agent-writable (live-verified)" as adjacent entries, and the adopter list carried the surface as live. That is precisely the docs-contradict-code hazard the 2026-08-10 "Restored the Agent Builder write-target docs" entry warns about, arrived at not by a dropped merge but by two correct-in-isolation merges landing on one file. **The reconciliation rule that produced this outcome: neither branch was taken wholesale.** Both versions of the manifest were read in full, and the disagreement was adjudicated on its two actual load-bearing claims rather than by seniority or merge order. **(1) THE GATE CLAIM — conceded, and it was right.** The ruling-out assessment's core point was that `matrx-admin/database`'s `sql_query` precedent (an agent may draft an inert command) does NOT transfer to a SINGLE-LINE prompt where **Enter runs**, carrying `autoFocus`, which the terminal body re-focuses on any click — a staged command could sit in an already-focused live shell one reflexive keystroke from executing, where the SQL workbench needs a deliberate, separately-located Execute press. It also identified a real defect: the `command_input` handler was calling `inputRef.current?.focus()` after staging, manufacturing exactly that hazard. The shipping branch had already removed it; re-verified here live — **the active element after an apply is `BODY`, not the prompt.** **(2) THE AFFORDANCE GAP — closed, not deferred.** Its second objection was that the prompt has no staged-vs-typed affordance, so an agent-written command is indistinguishable from a typed one and there is "nothing for the user to review *as* a proposal". The shipping branch conceded this as a KNOWN GAP and deliberately did not bundle the fix, since it is a UI change needing its own verification. **This task built it and verified it:** the input row renders a `staged by agent · not run` badge and the prompt's cwd turns amber with a dotted underline whenever a value arrived through a write target, and both clear the moment the value stops being the agent's — the command marker on the user's FIRST KEYSTROKE, on ArrowUp/ArrowDown history recall (which replaces the value without firing `onChange`, so it is cleared explicitly), and on exec; the cwd marker when the shell reports its own cwd back after a command. A marker that could outlive the value it describes would be worse than none. **(3) THE FLOOR OBJECTION resolves on the count.** "Exactly ONE target, under the ~2 floor" was true of `command_input` alone, but `working_directory` is a second genuine authored field, declarable because `handleExec` now sends the DISPLAYED cwd. And `voice-pad` is this campaign's own precedent that a single authored buffer earns targets when composing it is the surface's whole purpose — which is what a terminal is. **Net gate, post-fix:** read the `ask` dialog naming the target and its contract → Apply → the command sits visible, unfocused and MARKED → deliberate click into the prompt → Enter. That is one act LONGER than the precedent it was measured against (ask confirm, then one Execute press), so the gate objection no longer separates them. **What SURVIVES from the ruling-out branch, unchanged:** its whole exclusion analysis, now the manifest's "WHAT IS NOT WRITABLE HERE, AND WHY" section — `sandbox_config` is `instance.config`, a polled read-back with no setter/form/dirty-flag/PATCH route, not a form; the `/sandbox` list mount earns nothing (create-dialog fields are pure-mechanical toggles already defaulted from Redux prefs, and the next act is Create Sandbox, so staging them drives the start by proxy); starting/stopping/extending/destroying stays human; `command_history` / `terminal_output` are the RECORD; infrastructure identity is not writable; and `SandboxDiagnosticsPanel` changes nothing. **There is still NO execute target and asking for one is a refusal — staging is the ceiling at any price.** Its "do not re-assign" instruction and its `commandInput` verdict are the only things overturned, and its Change Log entry is kept and marked SUPERSEDED rather than deleted, because a negative assessment that found two real defects is worth reading. Branch 1's INDEPENDENT `matrx-user/marketing-authority` work was untouched throughout — it is unrelated to this dispute and remains as shipped. **Live-verified end to end on latest `main` (real Badass Agent runs on `/sandbox/<id>`, running sandbox `sbx-7520dde5030e`):** both ask dialogs carried the manifest description **verbatim**, per target; Apply landed `/tmp` and `cat /etc/os-release` with the terminal transcript still EMPTY — nothing executed; the new markers appeared on apply and the command marker vanished on a single keystroke while the value stayed; a human-pressed Enter on an agent-staged `pwd` printed `/tmp`, proving the staged cwd governs execution, and BOTH markers cleared; "Keep as is" returned `declined: true` with the agent replying "No problem — I've left the terminal box as it was"; asked to execute, it refused plainly ("I have no tool that executes commands in the sandbox … there's no target that runs it"); a forced relative path returned the handler's throw verbatim (`working_directory expects an ABSOLUTE path beginning with "/" — plain text, not JSON. Got: "agent/project".`) with nothing staged. Error Inspector: **0 distinct · 0 total** on a load with no forced throw, and exactly the expected `[surface-writeback]` capture on the load where one was forced. `check:surface-drift` + `type-check` clean. DB mirror sync still pending. **The campaign lesson, since this is the third shape of collision recorded here** (after "a competing design landed mid-flight" and "the work was stranded on an unmerged branch"): two branches can each pass their own STEP ZERO, both be individually defensible, and still produce a self-contradicting file when they merge — because a negative assessment writes a DO-NOT block into the same artifact a positive one writes declarations into. When that happens, the tie-break is not merge order and not seniority; it is whether the losing side's objections are ANSWERABLE IN CODE. Here both were, so they were answered and the surface shipped stronger than either branch left it. Had they not been, the honest outcome would have been to revert the targets and delete the adopter entry.
- **2026-08-12 — `matrx-user/sandboxes` RULED OUT on the judgment bar: it is an execution-evidence surface, and `sandbox_config` is a read-back, not a form. No write targets shipped.** ⚠️ **CONCLUSION SUPERSEDED — see the reconciliation entry above ("the two-branch collision on `matrx-user/sandboxes`, reconciled"). The surface IS agent-writable: 2 staging targets on the terminal. This entry is kept as the record of the parallel assessment, and its EXCLUSION analysis (everything about `sandbox_config`, the list mount, the lifecycle actions and the diagnostics panel) is still the settled posture — only the `commandInput` verdict and the "do not re-assign" instruction were overturned, and only after both of its concrete objections were fixed in code.** The negative result is the deliverable — ~~**do not re-assign this surface.**~~ The chip flagged `sandbox_config` as the thing the whole call turns on: a configuration a user composes BEFORE starting a machine would be a legitimate draft target, a read-back of what the running container was given is evidence. **It is the read-back.** `sandbox_config` is `instance.config`, a column on the `sandbox_instances` row fetched from `GET /api/sandbox/[id]` and re-polled every 10s, rendered through `<pre>{JSON.stringify(instance.config, null, 2)}</pre>` in the "Configuration" card and again in the admin panel. No setter, no form state, no dirty flag, no PATCH route — and re-writing it after the fact is meaningless, because the container was already built from it. The config a user *does* compose lives somewhere else entirely (`useSandboxCreate`, on the `/sandbox` LIST mount) and is not a declared surface value at all, so the two were never the same thing. **Per-mount posture — both mounts earn nothing.** The list owns fetched evidence, browse state (`historyOpen`, `selectedHistoryIds`) and the create dialog; its only write paths are `createInstance` / `stopInstance` / `deleteInstance(s)`, i.e. spend or destruction. The create form was checked on its own merits rather than dismissed with the lifecycle rule, and it has **zero authored fields**: `tier` is a two-option toggle, `template` an enum from the fetched catalog, `template_version` derived from the template, `resources` three hosted-only numbers, TTL a 1/2/4/8h preset — every one already defaulted to the user's last-used choice out of Redux prefs. Those are the skill's own "pure-mechanical toggles nobody would ask an agent to flip"; they exist only while the modal is open; and the very next act is Create Sandbox, so staging them is driving the start by proxy. The detail mount owns the polled `instance` (every declared value bar the shell ones is a projection of it), the shell session, and dialog toggles, behind `handleExec` / `handleStop` / `handleExtend` / `handleDelete`. The `CrawlsTable` posture from `marketing-crawls` applies to the list verbatim: a list over immutable evidence registers nothing. **Stated explicitly rather than by omission:** starting, stopping, extending or destroying a sandbox is not a target — it spends real compute and wall-clock and the human press stays the gate (`podcast-studio`, `image-generate`, `marketing-crawls`); running commands is an emphatic no — `command_history` and `terminal_output` are the RECORD of what a machine actually did, the input-vs-output line `voice-pad` drew around transcripts, and arbitrary shell is the most dangerous act here; and `container_id`, `sandbox_proxy_url`, the hot/cold paths, heartbeat, expiry, status and both ids are infrastructure identity. **The one genuine candidate and why it still fails, because this is the interesting part: `commandInput`.** It is technically inert — `handleExec` fires only on Enter or the Send button — which is superficially `matrx-admin/database`'s `sql_query`, the precedent that lets an agent draft SQL precisely because a textarea full of `DROP TABLE` is inert characters an admin reads and decides on. **The analogy breaks on the gate, which is the entire basis of that precedent.** `sql_query` stages into a multi-line workbench whose execution requires a deliberate, separately-located **Execute** press. `commandInput` is a single-line prompt carrying `autoFocus`, which the terminal body re-focuses on any click, and where **Enter runs it** — so a staged command sits in an already-focused shell prompt where the most reflexive keystroke at a `$` executes arbitrary shell on a live machine as the `agent` user. It also has no staged-vs-typed affordance at all: unlike the SQL workbench, a command sitting in the prompt is indistinguishable from one the user typed, so there is nothing for the user to review *as* a proposal. "The human press stays the gate" would be true only in the most literal sense. And even granting it, that is exactly ONE target on a surface where every other value is execution evidence or infrastructure identity — under the ~2 floor, the same outcome as `matrx-user/messages` and `matrx-user/canvas`. `SandboxDiagnosticsPanel` was checked too and changes nothing: its only inputs are `fsRootPath` / `envFilter` / `envView` / `logSource` / `logTail`, browse and view state over a read-only inspector; `fileContent` is display-only with no write-file path behind it; and Reset is destructive. **No `agent.review_queue` row** — nothing was made writable, so there is nothing for Arman to test. The full judgment is also written into the manifest docblock so it travels with the file. **Collision checks** (before writing and again before commit): no `writeTargets` in the working tree or on latest `origin/main`, `git ls-remote --heads origin` (full list, not just `claude/*`) matched no sandbox/terminal branch, and no `agent.review_queue` row in the last two days covers this surface.
- **2026-08-12 — Sandbox terminal agent-writable: the compose/execute split (live-verified).** `matrx-user/sandboxes` declares 2 `mode:"draft"` / `applyPolicy:"ask"` targets on the `/sandbox/[id]` terminal — `command_input` (stages ONE shell command into the input box) and `working_directory` (where that command will run). Handlers live on the detail page's own `SurfaceRuntimeProvider` (`app/(core)/sandbox/[id]/page.tsx`) and drive the same `setCommandInput` / `setCwd` the user's own typing and the exec response drive. A new read twin `staged_command` was added and emitted so the evidence loop closes (read what is staged → replace or extend it). **The judgment call, written down because it is the point:** there is deliberately NO `run_command` / `submit` / `execute` target and no handler may reach `handleExec`. A sandbox shell is arbitrary code execution on a live machine and the seam's one-line `ask` confirm is not an informed review of what a shell string will do — so the agent COMPOSES and the human RUNS, and pressing Enter on a command sitting in plain sight is the consent. This is precisely where `tasks`' `save_task` action target stops generalizing: `save_task` persists an already-previewed draft into a row the user owns and can edit again, whereas a command is neither previewed-by-its-value nor reversible — its blast radius is the container and whatever it can reach. Same seam, different bar. `working_directory` required a small honesty fix to be declarable at all: `handleExec` now sends the DISPLAYED `cwd` on every exec (the route already accepted `cwd?: string`), so the prompt is authoritative rather than a lagging mirror of the orchestrator's session cwd — without it, an agent-set cwd would have been a UI that lies until the next command snapped it back. A no-op under ordinary typing, since `cwd` is always set from `result.cwd`. Both handlers validate and throw, and both throw messages say **"plain text string, NOT JSON and NOT JSON-encoded"** in the message itself — the inline-tool layer parses a JSON-looking argument before the handler sees it, and without that wording an agent "fixes" the rejection by double-encoding and lands escaped `\n` and stray quotes in the input box. Deliberately NOT targets: `terminal_output` / `command_history` / `executing` (output and status), Stop / Extend / Delete (live-machine actions; delete is destructive by definition), and everything on the `/sandbox` list mount, which registers no handlers — tier/template/TTL are machine-provisioning config, not authored content. Honest scouting found only these two write-worthy fields (no sandbox label/name exists anywhere in the schema or UI; the diagnostics panel's `fsRootPath` / `selectedFile` / `fileContent` remain undeclared per the manifest's standing `readinessNote`), so each description carries full contract prose instead. Live-verified with a real Badass Agent run: both targets asked in ONE message each carrying the manifest description verbatim, Apply landed both (`cwd` → `/tmp`, box → `cat /etc/os-release`) with the terminal transcript still EMPTY — nothing executed; "Keep as is" declined with no error and a graceful acknowledgement; a direct "actually run it" request was refused ("I have no run capability here… nothing runs until you press Enter yourself"); a forced-invalid relative path returned the handler's own throw verbatim (`working_directory expects an ABSOLUTE path beginning with "/" — plain text, not JSON. Got: "agent/project".`); and a human-pressed `pwd` after an agent-staged `/tmp` printed `/tmp`, proving the staged cwd governs execution. Error Inspector: zero `surface-writeback` captures. `check:surface-drift` + `type-check` clean. DB mirror sync still pending. **A parallel assessment of this same surface RULED IT OUT, and its argument is recorded in the manifest rather than buried, because it found a real defect.** Its point: unlike `matrx-admin/database`'s `sql_query` — the precedent for drafting an inert command — this is a SINGLE-LINE prompt where Enter RUNS, carrying `autoFocus`, which the terminal body re-focuses on any click; so a staged command can sit in an already-focused live shell one reflexive keystroke from executing, where the SQL workbench needs a deliberate, separately-located Execute press. That is correct, and this page's handler had made it worse by calling `inputRef.current?.focus()` after staging — manufacturing exactly that hazard. **It no longer focuses the input**, re-verified live (active element after an apply is `BODY`, not the prompt), so reaching the gate costs a deliberate click plus Enter. Where this lands, having taken the point: the `ask` confirm IS the separately-located gate, nothing applied changes the machine, and a wrong command is cleared by typing over it. **Known gap, stated rather than papered over:** the input has no staged-vs-typed affordance, so an agent-written command looks identical to a typed one; a "staged by \<agent\>" marker that clears on first keystroke is the obvious next increment and is deliberately not bundled here, since it is a UI change needing its own verification. Note for whoever merges second: the ruling-out branch adds a "NO `writeTargets`, DELIBERATELY / do not re-assign" block to this same manifest — if both land, that comment and these targets contradict each other, and the reconciliation is a judgment call for a human, not a wholesale take-one-side merge. **Both DID land, the contradiction was real, and it has since been reconciled — including the marker this entry deferred. See the reconciliation entry above.**
- **2026-08-11 — `matrx-user/canvas`: the manifest was FICTION, so it got a first emitter and an honest vocabulary — and deliberately NO write targets.** Assigned as a write-target chip. The chip's prerequisite check ("no live emitter, no `SurfaceRuntimeProvider` — do `surface-authoring` first") was correct but understated the problem: the declared vocabulary described a **different application**. `selected_node_id`, `selected_nodes` and `current_text_block` describe a diagram-node editor with node selection and text elements; **the canvas has no nodes and no selection concept whatsoever** — the only `select`-shaped token in all of `features/canvas/` is a CSS `user-select` comment in `ResizableCanvas`. `render_mode` was documented as an edit/preview toggle; the real `CanvasRenderMode` is `inline | global | auto`, a placement preference. And `urlPattern`-wise the surface is mapped from `/canvas` in `route-to-surface.ts`, but **`/canvas` is not a route** — `app/(public)/canvas/` holds only `discover/` and `shared/[token]/`. So the previous entry was not "declared but unemitted", it was **unemittable**: three of eight values could never be filled by this codebase, which is the read lie in its strongest form, because an agent bound here would have been handed a vocabulary for a page that does not exist. **What the canvas actually is: a HOST, not an editor.** `CanvasSideSheetImpl` → `CanvasPane` → `CanvasBody` is a type-keyed switch that renders ARTIFACTS (mermaid, table, code, quiz, html, chart, flashcards, working document) opened from a chat message; it slides in over whatever route the user is on. The authored content lives inside each artifact renderer and, where it is agent-writable at all, on that artifact's OWN surface — `mermaid-editor`, `html-page`, `working-document`/`scratchpad`, all already shipped. **Hence NO `writeTargets`, and the floor rule is the reason, not laziness.** Ranked against the bar: `current_text_block` (the chip's designated YES, and the whole premise of the assignment) **cannot exist**; `canvas_json` is the artifact payload and was already ruled out as unreviewable-in-a-dialog; `current_canvas_id`/`current_canvas_type` are identity; `item_count` is derived; `render_mode`/split/pane-switching are mechanical view state nobody would ask an agent to flip. That leaves exactly ONE honest candidate — the saved item's `title`, editable via `canvasItemsService.update` from the inline rename in `SavedCanvasItems` — and `description`/`tags` exist on `UpdateCanvasItemInput` but have **no UI at all**, so a write there would land somewhere the user cannot see or review, failing the evidence loop. One target is below the ~2 floor, so the surface does not earn the work and none was shipped. **The structural argument is the durable one:** writing "into the canvas" generically means reaching through the host into whichever artifact renderer happens to be mounted — a parallel write path around surfaces that already ship their own targets, which is exactly what the doctrine forbids. **What DID ship (the `surface-authoring` half, which the chip assigned unconditionally):** the manifest re-authored against the live pane — fictional values deleted, `render_mode` documented with its real enum, `canvas_json` documented as **only the `{ artifactId }` pointer for materialized artifacts** (the body lives in `canvas_items` and is not inlined, so an agent must not claim to have read content it was not given), plus the values the pane really owns (`current_canvas_title`, `current_canvas_is_saved`, `open_items` as `{session_id, canvas_id, type, title}`, `is_split`, `secondary_canvas_id`) in two curated groups; the surface's FIRST `SurfaceRuntimeProvider`, mounted in `CanvasSideSheetImpl` (after the `!currentItem` guard, so it covers the single AND split layouts and every route the pane overlays), with the builder in `features/canvas/lib/canvas-scope.ts`; `readiness` `stub` → `partial`. **`getScope` reads the slice off the store at Run time rather than closing over rendered state** — the canvas is one ⌘\ away from switching items between mount and launch — and the one non-Redux input (`isMobile`) rides a ref advanced every render. **A one-line real bug fixed on the way:** `selectCanvasRenderMode` fell back to `"panel"`, which was never a `CanvasRenderMode`; it now falls back to the slice's own `"auto"` and is typed, so the declared enum cannot be contradicted by the fallback. **Live-verified** with a real Badass Agent run on a throwaway conversation. The route-vs-mount wrinkle recorded in the `mermaid-editor` entry bites hard here and is worth reusing: on `/chat/[id]` the ROUTE surface `matrx-user/chat` wins, and because `SurfaceAgentsPanelImpl` then computes `hasLiveScope = runtime.surfaceName === surfaceName`, the canvas scope is not merely mislabelled — **it is dropped entirely, and the run launches with an empty bag**. Verification therefore ran on `/artifacts` (no route→surface mapping), reached by CLIENT-SIDE nav with the pane open, since the canvas slice is not persisted and a full reload empties it. There the popover named `Canvas | matrx-user/canvas`, the Surface Context window rendered both curated groups and reported **`Live · 8/15 supplied · contract honored`** (no "Undeclared (runtime only)" rows), and the agent read back every value correctly: `current_canvas_type: working_document`, `current_canvas_title: Working document`, `item_count: 1`, `is_split: false`, `render_mode: auto` (the fixed fallback, visible), `current_canvas_is_saved: false`, `open_items[0].session_id: canvas-1786461362296-…`, and `canvas_json.conversationId` — with **`current_canvas_id` correctly ABSENT**, which is the manifest's own "empty when the open item is session-only" clause coming true rather than a gap. Asked whether it could write to the canvas, the agent answered "No. I have no tool that writes directly to this live canvas surface" — the correct behaviour for a surface with no `writeTargets`: no `apply_surface_write` is offered at all. Error Inspector: **zero `surface-writeback` captures** (there are no writes to capture). **A nesting note worth keeping:** the canvas provider is an ANCESTOR of whatever it renders, and `getSurfaceRuntime` picks the DEEPEST registration, so when the open item is a working document `WorkingDocumentEditor`'s own provider still wins and its four shipped targets stay reachable — a host surface cannot shadow the artifact surface inside it. That is also the mechanism argument for why the canvas should not grow content targets: the artifact's surface is already strictly closer to the content. `pnpm check:surface-drift` green (142 surfaces, 3498 values); `type-check` clean for these files — the 18 remaining errors are pre-existing, all in `features/marketing/data/*` and `packages/matrx-agents/*`. **Collision checks** (before writing and again before commit): `canvas.manifest.ts` had no `writeTargets` on latest `main`, `git ls-remote --heads origin` matched no canvas branch, and `agent.review_queue` had no canvas row.
- **2026-08-11 — `matrx-user/data-tables` gets its FIRST emitter AND its write half: 2 ask-policy entity targets, `selected_range` deleted as unemittable, and the coordinates-with-the-value pattern for grids.** This surface was a pure read lie: `readiness: "stub"`, 14 declared values, and — verified before writing anything — no emitter and no `SurfaceRuntimeProvider` anywhere in `features/` or `app/`; the only two references to the name in the entire repo were the manifest itself and two `route-to-surface.ts` prefixes. So this was TWO jobs, `surface-authoring` then `surface-write-targets`, and the authoring half is the larger one. **(1) The mount, and why it is opt-in.** All 14 values live in `UserTableViewer`, which also renders inside `DatasetOverlay`, `ViewTableModal` and the `UserTableWindow` panel — mounts owned by OTHER surfaces. Since the registry resolves deepest-first AND `listLiveWriteTargets()` walks the entire mounted stack, an unconditional provider there would both shadow the host surface and offer these targets on someone else's page. Gated behind an `emitSurfaceScope` prop that only `/data/[id]`'s `DataTableDetailClient` passes — the `matrx-user/lists` `asRoute` gate expressed as a prop rather than a second provider. The `/data` LIST route mounts nothing at all: it renders table cards and has no authored state, so there is nothing to emit and nothing to write. Scope builder in `features/data-tables/agent-context/buildDataTablesScope.ts`, beside the existing `buildDocumentsContextData.ts`; it OMITS what the page genuinely lacks rather than faking it, which is what keeps the manifest's "empty when …" prose true. Live: **7/19 supplied, contract honored, 2/2 agent-writable**, no "Undeclared (runtime only)" entries. **(2) `selected_range` DELETED.** The grid has no multi-cell selection concept — not "not wired yet", it does not exist — so no emitter could ever populate it. Removing it is the honest fix; its DB mirror row was purged after confirming `platform.associations` held zero references to the name. `is_read_only` was ADDED, because both handlers gate on it and an agent needs to know whether a write is possible at all. Several descriptions were also corrected to match reality rather than the original guess: `current_column_name` is the MACHINE field name (the thing a write needs), `column_list` now carries `display_name` alongside `name` (an agent handed only headers cannot write a cell), and `visible_data_csv` leads with a `row_id` column — a CSV of just the cells is readable but unwritable. **(3) The two targets, and THE grid-specific trap.** `table_description` (entity/ask) through a new `updateTableMetadata` service wrapper; the RPC `COALESCE`s every omitted argument, which is exactly what makes a description-only write unable to blank the table's name, and `EditTableModal` + `TableSettingsModal` were migrated onto the same wrapper so there is genuinely ONE path rather than my handler plus their two raw `supabase.rpc` calls. `cell_value` (entity/ask) writes ONE cell through `upsertCell` (`udt_upsert_cell` — a surgical `jsonb_set` that structurally cannot touch another cell, row, or column definition), and its value is an OBJECT carrying `{row_id, field_name, value}` together. **That bundling is the load-bearing decision.** `applySurfaceWrite` resolves the handler closure BEFORE the confirm dialog is answered, so a handler that read "the currently selected cell" off its render closure would validate against the grid as it was and write into the grid as it now is — landing a correct value in the WRONG cell, which on a grid is strictly worse than refusing, and invisible afterwards. Making the agent NAME the coordinates it READ, then checking them at apply time against state published through a ref reassigned on every render, converts that race into a refusal with instructions ("re-read `visible_data_csv`"). The second bound is visibility: the row must be in `displayRows` — the page actually on screen, i.e. after column filters — so every cell an agent writes is one the user watches change. A write to page 7 of a 400-row table would be unreviewable, so it is refused even though the RPC would accept it. Coercion reuses `normalizeCellValue`, exported from `EditableCell` for the purpose, so an agent write and a human keystroke can never store different things; but where that helper is deliberately forgiving for someone mid-typing (NaN and unparsed JSON pass through to the server), the handler is not, and refuses before anything is stored. `is_read_only` is three-valued in the ref (null = "unknown, refuse") because `isReadOnly` computes to `false` before the table row and the signed-in user have loaded, and `false` means writable — refusing early is safe, allowing early is not. **(4) Live-verified with real Badass Agent runs on a throwaway 5-column/3-row fixture table** (never real user data). One message drove both targets; each raised its OWN ask dialog carrying the manifest description verbatim; Apply on `table_description` persisted (SQL-confirmed, 103 chars) and left `table_name` intact, proving the COALESCE contract; Apply on `cell_value` wrote `category` on one row and **SQL confirmed the blast radius exactly — that row went version 1→2 with every other field byte-identical, while the two untouched rows stayed at version 1 with their original `updated_at`**. "Keep as is" declined cleanly, the agent acknowledged gracefully, and SQL confirmed the cell unchanged. A deliberate `{value: "not a number at all"}` into a `number` column came back to the agent as the handler's own throw verbatim (`cell_value.value "not a number at all" is not a valid number for column "score". Send a number (or null to empty the cell).`) with nothing written and the row still at version 1 — validate-then-apply. Error Inspector on a clean load (valid apply + decline only, no forced invalid): **0 captures, badge showed "No errors captured yet."** `check:surface-drift` (142 surfaces, 3496 values) and `type-check` clean — the 6 files with remaining type errors are all pre-existing (`features/brokers/*`, `features/marketing/data/*`, one package test). DB mirror synced through the canonical `POST /api/admin/surfaces/sync-manifests`: 19 values, 5 groups, `url_pattern`, `intro`, and **both write targets in `ui.ui_surface_write_target`** (note `scripts/emit-surface-sync-sql.ts` predates write targets and emits values only — use the API route, not the script, when a surface gains targets). **(5) An honest finding that is NOT about this seam, and that the skill's "ask for an undeclared target, expect a refusal" step surfaced by accident.** Asked to add a column and replace the table body, the agent DID replace all 3 rows with 10 new ones — through a server-side dataset tool of its own, not through `apply_surface_write` (the columns were untouched, and no ask dialog was ever raised). Re-run as a clean isolation test — "using ONLY your page write tool, set the table NAME and the search_term, and list the targets it actually offers" — the surface seam behaved exactly right: it enumerated precisely `table_description` and `cell_value`, refused both requests as undeclared, and raised no dialog. So the narrow posture designed here does not by itself bound what a bound agent can do to a user table; a parallel, pre-existing tool path can insert and delete rows wholesale with no per-write confirm. Worth a human look on its own terms — flagged rather than fixed, since it is a different tool surface. **The generalizable lesson for this campaign: the "undeclared target is refused" check must name the write tool explicitly, or a capable agent will satisfy the request some other way and the test proves nothing.** **(6) CAMPAIGN HANDOFF — no chips were fired from this task, for two separate reasons, and the scouting is recorded here so the next agent does not have to redo it.** Mechanically: this session sat at lineage depth 8 of a limit of 8, so `create_session` refused outright — the avalanche chain cannot be extended from here, and picking these up requires a fresh chain. More usefully: **the well is nearly dry.** Scanning every manifest on `main` for "lacks `writeTargets` AND has real editable page state", exactly TWO candidates survive the judgment bar, and BOTH are `readiness: "stub"` with no mount, so each needs `surface-authoring` before `surface-write-targets`. **(a) `matrx-user/smart-code-editor`** — the `smartCodeEditorWindow` overlay (`features/window-panels/windows/smart-code-editor/SmartCodeEditorWindow.tsx` + `features/code-editor/agent-code-editor/components/SmartCodeEditor.tsx`), whose own docblock says drafts do not survive reload, which makes it the safest write posture on the board. The buffer wants the scratchpad/working-document 4-target shape rather than one blunt replace, and a multi-file write wants THIS entry's coordinates-with-the-value pattern keyed on `active_file_path`. `diagnostics` / `git_*` / `workspace_*` are observed evidence about a real workspace, and `agent_skills` is capability governance. It is explicitly distinct from the ruled-out `matrx-user/code-editor` — worth confirming rather than assuming. **(b) `matrx-admin/bundles`** — `/administration/agents/bundles`, `features/tool-registry/bundles/components/BundlesAdminPage.tsx`, whose manifest docblock already blesses precisely this scope ("help the admin name/describe a new bundle, draft member aliases"): `bundle_description` through the canonical `updateBundle`, and a `{tool_id, alias}` object through `setBundleMemberAlias`, are two clean targets, while bundle MEMBERSHIP stays human on `agent-builder`'s reasoning that changing which tools a bundle reaches is a capability change and not a copy edit. Everything else still lacking targets was checked and FAILS the bar: the whole marketing report family (including `authority`, which has one guidance textarea and nothing else, and `reputation`, which has no editable state at all); `files`, a browser holding only search/sort/filter state; `image-annotate`, whose annotations are inescapably spatial the way `analysis-studio` ruled; `observational-memory`, an admin inspector; `crm-manager`, which inherits the ruled-out `crm` and declares zero own values; and the records and viewers (`ai-results`, `assistant-message`, `canvas-viewer`, `file-preview`, `gallery`, `image-viewer`, `share`, `context-preview`). **A chip fired at one of those wastes an entire agent run, so the honest move at this point in the campaign is fewer chips rather than padded ones.**

No write targets shipped.** The negative result is the deliverable, so the next agent does not re-derive it: **do not re-assign this surface.** The inbox has exactly ONE field that clears the bar — the composer draft in `features/messaging/components/MessageInput.tsx` — and the skill's ~2-target floor exists for precisely this case. The intended second target, `current_conversation_title`, has **no canonical write path anywhere in the repo**: it reads `display_name ?? group_name`, where `display_name` on a direct conversation IS the other participant's name (identity, never writable) and `group_name` is written once at creation and never again. `lib/supabase/messaging.ts` exposes only `sendMessage` / `sendBridgeMessage` / `markConversationAsRead`; there is no `conversations` UPDATE in the codebase at all, and the slice's `updateConversation` is a local reducer the realtime initializer uses to MIRROR rows, not a write path — so declaring a title target would be the "declared target with no canonical write path" trap, and building a rename feature to justify a target is inventing product to hit a count. The remaining values are refused by settled doctrine: the transcript (`last_message_text`, `all_conversations`) is the RECORD (`matrx-user/chat`, 2026-08-10), sending is the outward-facing act and here it is outward **to another person under the user's name**, and sender identity / read state / timestamps / deletion were never candidates. The one YES field would also be a near-duplicate of chat's shipped `input_draft` — the same `{text, mode?: "replace" | "append"}` contract on a second surface — so the adoption would have been one borrowed target and nothing else. Note the campaign's accepted ONE-target adoptions (`task_draft`, `drill_config`, `generation_request`, `note_draft`) are all COMPOSITES covering several fields, or uniquely high-leverage (`sql_query`); a bare message draft is neither. **Shipped anyway, because it was wrong in the file:** the manifest claimed `readiness: "stub"` / "no runtime emitter" while TWO `SurfaceRuntimeProvider` mounts have been emitting scope all along — the thread route (`app/(core)/messages/[conversationId]/page.tsx`) emits 8 of 9 surface-specific values, the list route (`MessagesPageClient.tsx`) only `total_unread_count` + `all_conversations`. Corrected to `partial` with an accurate note, added the missing `urlPattern: "/messages"`, and wrote the whole judgment into the manifest docblock. `current_conversation_message_count` is declared but emitted by NEITHER mount — the surface's one real remaining read gap, left for a `surface-authoring` pass. No `agent.review_queue` row: nothing was made writable, so there is nothing for Arman to test. **Campaign-state finding from the replacement search** (14 candidates checked against `features/surfaces/manifests/`): the unadopted well is close to dry for single-skill work. `matrx-admin/bundles` is the best remaining write-target candidate on the judgment bar — `BundleDetail` is an inline name/description/metadata editor with its own Save bar — but its manifest has no emitter AND `/administration/agents/bundles` currently fails to load any rows ("Failed to load bundles" against `tool.bundle`), so it cannot be live-verified until that data path is fixed. `matrx-user/agent-settings` owns 4 genuinely authored fields (name, description, category, tags) through `AgentSettingsForm`, but has no emitter, is window-only, and `route-to-surface.ts:34` maps a `/agents/settings` prefix that **does not exist as a route**; note its handlers must stage into that form's LOCAL `draft` state, because reusing `useAgentBuilderWriteHandlers` would dispatch `setAgentField` into the shared Redux record the form diffs against, leaving the form showing the new value with its Save bar reading "All changes saved" — a silent no-op. `marketing-authority` has one guidance textarea; `crm-manager`, `files` (self-declared `isEditable={false}`), `marketing-reputation`, `image-annotate` (its authored text is trapped inside marker.js, not React state) all fail the bar.
- **2026-08-11 — Content-extractor template agent-writable; the "assigned surface has no mount" case.** The campaign chip named `matrx-user/extractor-chunker`. It earned ZERO targets, and that is the finding: `grep` puts its surface name in a manifest, a registry, a route alias and one `getManifest()` call in `VariableMappingEditor` — there is no `SurfaceRuntimeProvider` for it anywhere, because it is a SERVER-side per-chunk vocabulary catalog that aidream's `_build_surface_vars` fills once per chunk at run time. `writeTargets` on it would have been declared, mirrored, and never offered to a single agent — the skill's own "no mounted runtime looks exactly like a broken target" trap, reached by following the chip literally. The FORM the chip actually pointed at (`ChunkingConfigForm`, the template editor at `/tools/pdf-extractor/[documentId]`) renders inside `PdfStudioShell`'s provider, so `matrx-user/pdf-extractor` — previously read-only and `readiness: "verified"` — is where the targets went. **Check which surface is MOUNTED before you check which surface is named.** Two targets, both `draft`/`ask`: `extraction_template_draft` takes a PARTIAL `{template_name, page_range, chunk_size, chunk_overlap}` — one object rather than four micro-targets because the page composes them in one act ("Apply Recommended" patches range, size and overlap in a single dispatch and the name auto-seeds from the agent), so one confirm dialog covers one decision; `extraction_output_columns` is split out for the reason `marketing-crawls` split its patterns — the Results table definition is a separate decision with its own editor, its own consumer, and a user may well accept it while rejecting the chunk geometry. Handlers dispatch the SAME `patchDraft({fileId, patch})` the user's keystrokes dispatch, are validate-then-apply so a partly-good object never half-lands, and throw with the legal shape spelled out (`chunk_size` against `MIN_CHUNK_SIZE`/`MAX_CHUNK_SIZE`; `chunk_overlap` against the size that will be live AFTER the patch, not whichever one is in the draft; `page_range` through the Pages input's own `parsePageRangeInput` and then against the document's real page set; column `type` against a new shared `COLUMN_TYPES` extracted out of `SchemaEditor`'s private array so the dropdown and the handler have ONE vocabulary). The mode gate is two-layer and worth copying: `ChunkingConfigForm`'s live handlers refuse while the panel is not in EDITING mode or a run is in flight, and `PdfStudioShell`'s provider supplies a BASE set that refuses with "the editor is not on screen" for when the panel is not mounted at all — `resolveHandlers` merges registered handlers OVER the provider's, so the layering is order-independent, unlike two `useSurfaceWriteHandlers` registrations where React's child-before-parent effect order decides the winner. Read twins added in the same change (`marketing-site-media` precedent — a target whose value an agent cannot read back has no evidence loop): `extraction_template_editor` is the gate made READABLE, so the agent checks `editing` before it writes instead of learning by refusal. **NOT writable:** Run — `saveTemplateFromDraft` → `launch` spends real model budget over every chunk of the document, the most expensive button on the surface, and the human press stays the gate per `podcast-studio` / `image-generate` / `marketing-crawls`; Save; `clearJobResults` (destructive stays human); `agent_id` and `variable_mapping`, both genuinely valuable and both deferred because their vocabularies (the agent catalog, the chosen agent's variable names) are not surface values here — a target an agent can only guess at is a trap, not a feature; and every run output (`clean_text`, `raw_text`, `chunk_index`, `chunk_count`, `job_id`, `run_id`), which is the record of what the extractor produced — an agent moves those by changing the config and letting the user re-run, which IS the evidence loop on this surface. Live-verified with a real Badass Agent run on a 33-page document: one message changed both targets, each ask dialog carried the manifest's own prose, Apply landed the name / range / size / overlap and three columns in the visible editor, "Keep as is" left chunk size at 6 and returned a non-error the agent acknowledged, and a request to write RAG boost and the template kind was refused with the agent naming the only two targets it has. Three deliberate invalid values reached the model verbatim (`chunk_size must be a whole number from 1 to 50 … Received: 500`; `page_range "60-70" names 11 page(s) that do not exist in this document (first: 60). This document has 33 page(s), up to page 33.`; and the mode-gate refusal after Cancel) with nothing staged. Error Inspector: **0 captures on the clean load** (valid applies + a decline + the undeclared-target refusal) against **3 on the deliberately-invalid load** — the control that makes the zero mean something. `check:surface-drift` clean; `type-check` still exactly the 11 pre-existing `features/brokers/*` errors. A second agent built a competing design on `matrx-user/extractor-chunker` in parallel and self-labelled its branch `claude/surface-write-extractor-chunker-alt-design` "NOT FOR MERGE AS-IS — superseded by the pdf-extractor design on main"; nothing to reconcile, but it is the second time this campaign that two agents landed on one surface.
- **2026-08-11 — Smart Code Editor agent-writable; THE reference for an editor whose canonical state path is NOT undo-safe.** `matrx-user/smart-code-editor` declares 3 `mode:"draft"` / `applyPolicy:"ask"` targets over the floating 4-column agentic editor: `replace_selection`, `insert_at_cursor`, and `content` (named for THIS surface's whole-buffer read value, not `code-editor`'s `current_file_content`). The surface had **no `SurfaceRuntimeProvider` anywhere** — `readiness: "stub"`, "no runtime emitter yet, nothing emits this scope" — so mounting the first one in `SmartCodeEditor.tsx` was part of the job; that shared core backs both `SmartCodeEditorWindow` and `SmartCodeEditorModal`, and `getScope` goes through the manifest's own `createSmartCodeEditorScope` rather than a second hand-rolled builder. Readiness is now `verified`. **The trio and its reasoning were taken from `matrx-user/code-editor` deliberately and almost verbatim — but two premises underneath it are FALSE here, and checking them is the whole lesson.** **(1) The undo stack is not free.** `code-editor` can say "reuse the path the user's typing takes" because its buffer IS Monaco. This editor is UNCONTROLLED: React state flows back down as `initialCode`, and `SmallCodeEditorImpl`'s sync effect applies any out-of-band change with `model.setValue()` — which discards undo history. So the obvious implementation (call `handleContentChange`, the setter the typing path uses) would have silently cost the user their undo stack on every agent write, while looking exactly right in review. The handlers instead edit the MODEL directly with `pushEditOperations` between `pushStackElement()` stops; that fires Monaco's change event, which flows into the same `handleContentChange` typing feeds, so state converges, the `setValue` branch never trips (the model already holds the text), the window's `code-change` emit fires as usual, and ⌘Z reverses an agent edit like a paste. **Generalizable: "dispatch the same action the user's typing dispatches" is a rule about the RESULTING STATE, not about which function you call — on an uncontrolled editor the two diverge, and the undo stack is where the divergence shows.** Model-level edits also survive the second false premise: **this editor opens READ-ONLY** (`isEditing` defaults false, pencil off) and the widget's `executeEdits` — what `code-editor` uses — is gated by that option, so the same code there would have no-opped silently on the default state of the page. **(2) There is no Save, and no autosave either.** The window is registered `ephemeral: true`, its files live in `useCodeEditorWindowState`'s in-memory mode (no `fileIds`, so no `code_files` rows and no auto-save middleware), and the UI has no Save control at all. Targets stay `draft` because that is the honest classification of the stakes (staged, reversible, unpersisted) — `entity` would make the dialog say "This is saved immediately", which is flatly false, and `ui` (the one branch appending nothing) would call a whole code buffer "ephemeral view state" and invite a later reader to relax it to `auto`. The cost of that choice is recorded rather than hidden: the seam's `draft` line, "nothing is saved until you save", is *literally* true here yet implies a Save the surface lacks, so the descriptions carry the true version ("a scratch buffer … a reload discards it") and do not lean on the appended sentence. This is the `mermaid-editor` / `podcast-run` mode-as-a-truth-claim test run in the opposite direction — those surfaces autosave, so `draft` would lie; this one never saves at all. **`editor_title` was scouted as a 4th target and REJECTED on the record:** it is an opener-supplied prop the window shell renders, with no canonical write path and no user gesture that renames the window, so a handler would have to invent state nothing else writes — the "declared target with no canonical write path" trap. Also undeclared: diagnostics and the git/workspace values (a report of where the code came from, not an input), multi-file lifecycle, and handing code back to the opener. Every handler reads the editor through a REF, never a render closure, because the writeback seam resolves all staged handlers before the first dialog is answered — on an editor a stale snapshot means replacing the wrong range — and all three refuse while the editor's own agent flow is showing its diff overlay (`state !== "input"`). One plumbing change was needed and kept minimal: an optional `onEditorMount` prop on `SmallCodeEditor`/`Impl` (additive, threaded through `CodeOrDiffColumn`), because that component exposed its Monaco instance to nobody. Live-verified with real Badass Agent runs on the window opened from `/demos/smart-code-editor` — a route with NO `route-to-surface` mapping, so `SurfaceAgentsPanelImpl` binds the MOUNTED runtime and the popover correctly reads `matrx-user/smart-code-editor` (the route-vs-mount trap recorded in the `mermaid-editor` entry avoided by construction, not by luck). ONE message asking for two changes raised two per-target ask dialogs carrying the manifest descriptions verbatim under the canonical labels; Apply landed both (buffer 1625 → 1992 chars, the new header comment visible in Monaco); a second run landed `insert_at_cursor`; asking for the editor TITLE was refused with the agent stating unprompted that the page exposes exactly `replace_selection`, `insert_at_cursor` and `content` and declining to fake it by jamming a title into the buffer; `replace_selection` with nothing selected returned the handler's own throw verbatim (`client_tool_error: replace_selection needs a selection, and nothing is selected in the editor. Ask the user to highlight the code to replace, or use insert_at_cursor / content instead — do not guess a range.`); and "Keep as is" left the buffer byte-identical at 1625 chars with the agent acknowledging the decline without retrying. Error Inspector on a clean load (valid applies + a decline, no forced bad value): **0 distinct · 0 total occurrences**. Every throw message names the shape as "a non-empty plain text string, not JSON and not JSON-encoded … raw code only — no markdown fences", since the inline-tool layer parses a JSON-looking argument before the handler sees it and an agent "fixes" a rejection by double-encoding. `check:surface-drift` (142 surfaces, 3496 values) + `type-check` both clean, with ZERO errors of any kind — the 11 pre-existing `features/brokers/*` errors that every recent entry here reports were fixed on `main` during this task, so that baseline caveat no longer applies and a future agent seeing 11 errors should treat them as NEW. DB mirror (`ui.ui_surface_write_target`) still pending a manifest sync; the client tool works either way, as this run shows. **Scope note:** the older standalone `MultiFileSmartCodeEditorWindow` is a SEPARATE component that does not render `SmartCodeEditor`, so it mounts nothing and is offered no tool — recorded in `readinessNote` rather than left for a reader to discover.
- **2026-08-11 — Analysis Studio: `annotation_redact` (a 4th target that REVERSES a documented exclusion), a same-tick URL-clobber fix, and the `redact` read twin. Also a documented dead end on `matrx-user/quick-tasks`.** This is an ADDITIVE follow-up to the entry below, not a competing design — that design landed on `main` while this work was in flight and it wins; only what it did not cover is added here. **(1) The bug.** `studio_focus_annotation` called `deps.selectAnnotation(...)` then `deps.goToPage(...)`. Both rebuild the query string from the SAME `searchParams` snapshot, so the second `router.replace` started from the pre-change URL and dropped the first one's param: the URL kept `?page=N` and silently lost the `?annotation=` it had just set. A user never hits this — their clicks are separate events one render apart, so `searchParams` has re-read by the time the second fires — but a write handler does both in a single tick. The canvas looked correct until a reload threw the selection away. Fixed with one `handleFocusAnnotation` → one `pushUrl`; the deps interface now takes `focusAnnotation(id, page)` instead of the two setters, so the shape makes the batching mandatory rather than optional. **Generalizable: when a write target drives two callbacks that both touch the URL, batch them — separate handlers that are correct for a human are not automatically correct for an agent.** **(2) The new target.** `annotation_redact` (`{redact: boolean, annotation_id?: string}`, `entity`/`ask`) marks or clears a region's redaction flag through the same `updateAnnotation` the other two content targets use — it is literally the region context menu's own "Mark for redaction" item. **This reverses an exclusion the landed design argued for** ("marking a region for redaction is a disclosure decision, not a copy edit"), so the original reasoning is preserved verbatim in the manifest beside the counter-argument rather than deleted, and a `DECISION NEEDED` row is registered for a human to pick. The counter-case: marking is not redacting (nothing leaves the document; the user still runs the Redact tab's pass, which is where disclosure actually happens); the write path is identical to `annotation_extracted_text`, which is reached from the very same context menu; and "go through this contract and mark every SSN, account number and date of birth" is the highest-value agent action on a PII document and is unreachable one right-click at a time, with each call still raising its own ask dialog. If the original reading wins, delete the target and its handler — **keep the read twin either way.** **(3) The read twin.** The emitter now publishes `redact` on `annotations` and `current_page_annotations`; without it an agent asked to "mark the rest" cannot see what is already marked and proposes duplicates. Live-verified with real Badass Agent runs on a throwaway 2-page PDF (uploaded, annotated via SQL, deleted afterwards — `files.files` / `files.pages` / `files.page_annotations` all confirmed zero): the ask dialog carried the target's description verbatim; Apply persisted `redact=true` (SQL-confirmed) and a "Keep as is" on the second annotation left it `false`; `{redact:"yes"}` came back to the agent as the handler's own throw verbatim (`annotation_redact: redact must be true or false. Pass true to mark the region for the redaction pass, false to clear the mark.`) with nothing written; and after `studio_focus_annotation` the URL carried BOTH `annotation=` and `page=2` and a reload reproduced page 2 — the fix, demonstrated. Error Inspector on a clean load (valid apply + decline only): the badge did not render at all, i.e. zero captures of any kind. The undeclared-target refusal was confirmed earlier on this same unchanged seam (`No mounted surface declares write target "annotation_category".`, no dialog raised). `check:surface-drift` (140 surfaces, 3411 values) + `type-check` clean — the 11 remaining type errors are pre-existing, all in `features/brokers/*`. **Campaign note — `matrx-user/quick-tasks` was the original assignment and was deliberately NOT done.** `agent.review_queue` row `128e59b2` ("Quick Tasks window is agent-writable (8 write targets)", 2026-08-10) records a prior agent completing that exact assignment, with `metadata.origin.branch = claude/surface-write-quick-tasks` and commit `1240fc91`. At the time of scouting neither existed on origin: `git ls-remote` listed only `main` and one unrelated branch, the commit was unreachable from every ref including all 105 `refs/pull/*` heads, `quick-tasks.manifest.ts` on `main` had no `writeTargets` and only ever had one commit (the one that created it), and this file had zero mentions of the surface. Re-implementing would have produced a second, independently-designed 8-target set against a live pending review row — the duplicate-implementation collision this campaign exists to avoid. (The branch has since REAPPEARED on origin, which is consistent with the original agent still holding it.) Left alone and raised for a human.
- **2026-08-11 — `matrx-user/chat` INDEPENDENTLY re-verified, and the `variable_values` ruling sharpened.** A second agent picked up the chat chip, found the surface already shipped by the 2026-08-10 entry below, and — per the campaign's collision rule — kept that work untouched and contributed only the additive part. Two things came out of it. **(1) Independent live re-verification** on `/chat/[conversationId]` against the branch tip, since a shipped surface that no one re-runs is a claim rather than a fact: one message drove both targets; each raised its OWN ask dialog carrying the manifest description verbatim, with the bounds interpolated from the vocabulary constants (`at most 200 characters`, `1-20000 characters`) rather than re-typed, which is the anti-drift property actually paying off where a reader can see it; Apply on `conversation_title` persisted to `chat.conversation.title` (confirmed by direct SQL, not by trusting the toast); Apply on `input_draft` staged into the page composer without sending; **"Keep as is" declined cleanly and the row was confirmed UNCHANGED by SQL afterward** — a decline that silently wrote would look identical in the UI, so it is worth checking at the table; asking the same run to edit a transcript message was refused with the agent explaining unprompted that only `conversation_title` and `input_draft` exist; and a forced `{"text": 12345, "mode": "sideways"}` returned the handler's own throw verbatim — `client_tool_error: input_draft expects a non-empty text string — the message to stage in the composer.` — validating `text` before `mode`, with no coercion. Error Inspector: **zero captures on the clean run**, two on the deliberate contract break, which is the seam being loud by design. The `SurfaceAgentsPanelImpl` route-vs-mount wrinkle recorded in the `mermaid-editor` entry does NOT bite here — the header popover on `/chat/[id]` resolves to `matrx-user/chat` and offers `Run Badass Agent` against the mounted runtime. `check:surface-drift` (140 surfaces, 3402 values) + `type-check` clean. **(2) The `variable_values` NO now has a structural reason**, and it is recorded in the manifest's judgment block, which previously ruled on every other declared value but was silent on this one. The entry below leaves it out because chat agents rarely declare variables and the happy path could not be exercised live; that is true but understates the case, because it reads as "not yet" when the answer is "not here". The load-bearing reason is that **this surface owns no editor for it**: every component that dispatches `setUserVariableValue` (`AgentVariablesInline`, `BoundVariableChips`, the `variable-input-variations/*` family, `ChatAssistantVariableInputs`) lives outside `components/chat/`, and `ChatRoomClient` only READS the value through `selectResolvedVariables` in order to publish it. Note this is NOT the capability-governance line — a variable is substituted into a prompt and does not change what a run may REACH — so the usual `agent-builder` reasoning does not apply and a reader would be right to expect a target; it fails the earlier test instead, the "declared target with no canonical write path on this mount" trap. It earns a target on whichever surface actually mounts a variable editor. **(3) THE MERGE DEFECT RECURRED, a third time — restored here.** The chat write-target CODE reached `main` intact (`dfb4fb0b`, `writeTargets` present on the manifest, handlers on `ChatRoomClient`), but both of its doc artifacts were dropped on the way: the 2026-08-10 Change Log entry below and the `matrx-user/chat` clause in the agent-writable adopter list above were BOTH absent from `main` at `1a5c79e4`, so this file was actively claiming chat is not agent-writable while the shipped app offered two working targets. Both are restored in this commit, alongside — not instead of — the six entries `main` gained in the meantime (`analysis-studio`, `context-items`, `chat-voice`, `war-room`, the previous restoration, `scratchpad`); the dated-entry count goes 101 → 103, which is the +2 restoration and nothing overwritten. This is now the THIRD recorded occurrence (see the 2026-08-09 Agent Builder restoration and the 2026-08-10 one below), and the pattern is identical each time: a conflict in this file gets resolved by taking one side wholesale, which silently reverts documentation for code that shipped. **The check that catches it is cheap and belongs in every campaign rebase:** count `^- \*\*2026-` before and after, and grep the adopter list for your own surface name — the code compiling proves nothing about whether its documentation survived. **POSTSCRIPT — and then it happened to THIS entry, which makes four.** The commit carrying everything above reached `main` intact, and every other piece of it is still there: the manifest's `variable_values` ruling, the restored 2026-08-10 Change Log entry, the restored adopter clause, the `components/chat/FEATURE.md` line. The single artifact a later merge dropped was this Change Log entry itself — the one describing the restoration — so `main` briefly held restored documentation with no record of why it had been restored. Re-added here. Two things generalize. First, **the loss is partial and file-local, not per-commit**: four of five artifacts from one commit survived, so "my change is in `main`" is not evidence that any particular part of it is. `git merge-base --is-ancestor` answered YES the whole time and told me nothing useful; only grepping for each artifact's own distinctive phrase found the gap. Second, **the entries most at risk are the NEWEST ones at the top of this list**, because the top is where every agent in the campaign inserts and therefore where every conflict lands. If you write an entry here, grep `main` for a distinctive phrase from it after your branch merges — not just for your code.
- **2026-08-11 — Agent Run surface agent-writable — the declarations were already on main but INERT; one missing line published them. (Docs restored: this entry and the adopter clause were themselves dropped by a merge hours after the code landed.)** `matrx-user/agent-run` now offers 3 ask-policy targets: `user_input_draft` (`draft`, the `{text, mode: "replace" | "append"}` composer pair), `variable_values` (`draft`, a partial per-key patch), and `conversation_title` (`entity`). **The defect worth remembering: `agent-run.manifest.ts` declared `const writeTargets: SurfaceWriteTarget[] = [...]` with all three targets fully written, and `AgentRunnerPage` already passed `getWriteHandlers` with all three handlers implemented — but the exported `agentRunManifest` object never referenced the array.** `listAgentWritableTargets()` reads the manifest, so the surface advertised nothing, no `apply_surface_write` tool was ever injected on a run page, and the whole feature was dead code that type-checked, passed `check:surface-drift`, and read as shipped. It was the ONLY manifest in the repo with a single `writeTargets` occurrence (every other adopter has two — the declaration and the `writeTargets,` property); **`grep -c writeTargets <manifest>` is the cheap detector for this class of defect, and `check:surface-drift` does not catch it because it validates VALUES and never inspects write targets.** A dangling declaration is worse than an absent one: it makes an unshipped surface look adopted to anyone reading the file. The landed design was otherwise sound and was KEPT verbatim — handlers dispatch the same `setUserInputText` / `setUserVariableValues` the user's own typing fires and the canonical `renameConversation` thunk, vocabulary is imported from `chat.manifest.ts` (`CHAT_DRAFT_WRITE_MODES` / `CHAT_INPUT_DRAFT_MAX` / `CHAT_CONVERSATION_TITLE_MAX`) rather than re-typed, and `variable_values` re-reads `selectInstanceVariableDefinitions` at APPLY time so an undefined name is refused with the real declared list and nothing is applied. This change added the one-line wiring plus the first live verification — the work had never been, and could not have been, verified. **Posture confirmed while verifying, not assumed:** handlers deliberately have NO mid-stream guard, unlike `chat-voice`. Per the chat feature's Flow 6 a send during a run QUEUES by default or steers mid-run and the composer stays live — a hard-locked composer was a BUG fixed on 2026-07-29 — so refusing to stage while streaming would re-impose exactly what was removed. Single mount: `AgentRunnerPage` past its `isAgentRunSurface` gate, so the `/code` workspace backed by the same component registers nothing. Live-verified with real Badass Agent runs on `/agents/[id]/run` against an agent declaring `city` / `response_format` / `what`: one message asking for three changes produced a per-target ask dialog carrying the manifest description VERBATIM, Apply landed the draft in the run composer and `city` = `Barcelona` in the real variable input, and the third request — changing the agent's model to `gpt-4o` — was refused with the agent naming the only three writable targets and pointing at the agent editor as where model changes belong. "Keep as is" returned `{ok:false, declined:true}` with no error. A deliberately invalid `{"NotARealVariable": "x"}`, sent under explicit instruction not to pre-validate or substitute, came back as the handler's own throw verbatim: `variable_values refused — "NotARealVariable" is not a variable this agent declares. Declared variables: "city", "response_format", "what". Nothing was applied.` **The `conversation_title` persistence gate was exercised in both directions** — on a fresh unsaved run it refused (`this run has not been saved yet, so there is no row to rename`), and after a human turn persisted the row the same request returned `{ok:true, target:"conversation_title", mode:"entity", message:"\"Conversation title\" applied and saved."}`. Error Inspector on the clean load: zero `surface-writeback` captures. `check:surface-drift` + `type-check` clean — the 11 remaining type errors are pre-existing and all in `features/brokers/*`. DB mirror (`ui.ui_surface_write_target`) still pending a manifest sync. **Meta-finding for the campaign:** the code line survived the merge and both `features/surfaces/FEATURE.md` edits did not, while `features/agents/FEATURE.md` did — the same drop that hit `content-plan-entities` in this wave and the 16 entries restored on 2026-08-10. When your work auto-merges, go back and CHECK that your adopter clause and Change Log entry are still on main; do not assume a green push means the docs landed.
- **2026-08-11 — Skill editor agent-writable; THE reference for a draft whose read twin lives in a DIFFERENT component. Also: the assigned surface (`matrx-user/scopes`) was rejected on the judgment bar.** `matrx-user/connections-skills` declares 5 ask-policy `mode:"draft"` targets over `/agent-connections/skills`' detail/create editor: `skill_label`, `skill_description`, `skill_type`, `skill_body` and `skill_trigger_patterns`. A skill IS authored content — a markdown document plus the description that decides when an agent loads it — so this is the jackpot class. Every handler stages through the SAME `set(key, value)` helper each input's `onChange` calls, so an applied write is literally a keystroke the agent typed: the field goes dirty, Save lights up, and nothing reaches `skill.definition` until the user presses it. `skill_type` validates against `KNOWN_SKILL_TYPES`, the list the Type `<select>` renders its own options from, so the vocabulary the agent is told, the vocabulary the handler accepts, and the options on screen cannot drift. **The generalizable finding is the read twin.** The emitter is `SkillsSection`, but the draft is `useState` one component down in `SkillDetailEditor`, so `getScope` could not see it and the only declared twin (`selected_skill_summary`) reports the SAVED row — a draft target whose twin never moves is an evidence loop that lies. Fix: the editor hands a `ConnectionsSkillsDraftSnapshot` upward through an `onDraftSnapshot` callback into a ref that `getScope` spreads at Run time, so the new `skill_draft_*` group reflects the STAGED buffer and the agent can read back exactly what it just staged. Both new props (`surfaceName`, `onDraftSnapshot`) are optional, so the super-admin console's mount of the same editor registers nothing and is behaviourally untouched. `skill_id`, delete, and `is_system`/`is_public` stay human-only: the identity every agent inclusion resolves against, a destructive action, and blast radius. **Verified live** (Badass Agent on `/agent-connections/skills`, against a throwaway skill, since deleted): all 5 targets offered in one run with the manifest's description text in each ask dialog; Apply staged and toasted "… staged — review and save"; the user's own Save persisted label + body + trigger patterns to `skill.definition` (confirmed by SQL — real newlines, no double-encoding); "Keep as is" on Description left the saved value untouched; `skill_type: "banana"` came back as the handler's verbatim error with NOTHING staged and Save still disabled (validate-then-apply); a request to change `skill_id` produced no dialog and no change. Error Inspector on a load with no induced error showed no badge at all — zero captures — with a positive control in the same session (the deliberate bad value) raising it to "2 errors captured", proving the check detects what it claims to. **Rejected on the way in:** the assigned surface was `matrx-user/scopes`, scouted as having inline context-item cell editing on its hub. It does not — `ScopesHub` mounts its provider with `isEditable={false}`, every cell is a read-only `<td>`, rows navigate away, and the templates/settings panels expose only a `refresh()` button; the only writable state on the whole surface is a local filter textbox. Scope and context-item-value editing lives in `ScopeDetailEditor` on `/organizations/[orgId]/scopes/[typeId]/[scopeId]`, a route that mounts NO surface at all — wiring one there is a `surface-authoring` job, not this skill's. Padding `scopes` to reach two targets would have been worse than moving.
- **2026-08-11 — Agent Advanced Editor agent-writable (live-verified); the surface's FIRST provider, and THE second citation for the overlay-naming rule.** `matrx-user/agent-advanced-editor` declares 4 `mode:"draft"` / `applyPolicy:"ask"` targets: `editor_system_instruction`, `editor_append_system_instruction`, `editor_output_schema`, and the composite `editor_catalog_profile`. **This surface had no runtime provider at all** — it was `readiness: "stub"`, declaring a vocabulary nothing ever emitted, so no write target could have resolved against it; `AgentContentWindow` now mounts the provider (`useAgentAdvancedEditorSurface`) and emits the open agent as the read half, and the manifest's docblock was also corrected: it described a single-large-field overlay, but the window is a twelve-tab whole-agent editor. Writes go through the same actions the user's own typing dispatches — `withAgentSystemInstruction` + `setAgentMessages` for the prompt (so attached non-text blocks round-trip), `setAgentOutputSchema` for the schema (the Output Schema tab's own Apply path), `setAgentField` for the catalog fields — and nothing reaches the database until the user presses the footer Save, which is deliberately not a target. **The naming rule is load-bearing here, not cosmetic.** Targets are prefixed `editor_` because this is a floating WINDOW: its provider registers shallow, `applySurfaceWrite` resolves a bare name against the deepest surface that DECLARES it, and `matrx-user/agent-builder` already declares `system_instruction` / `append_system_instruction`. Left unprefixed, opening this editor on `/agents/[id]/build` would hand the write to the builder behind it — and because this window has its own agent picker and multi-agent tab strip, that is frequently a DIFFERENT agent than the one the user is looking at. Same failure `matrx-user/quick-tasks` answered with `panel_*`; each description also names the window out loud so the model knows which of two open editors it is writing into. **Composite vs separate, decided per target:** `editor_catalog_profile` takes `{description?, category?, tags?}` as ONE object because the category and the tags ARE the classification OF the description — one act, one commit, one catalog row (the `feedback_draft` / `note_draft` reasoning) — and atomicity is also correctness here, since every handler in a multi-target turn resolves BEFORE the user confirms the first dialog, so three siblings could each land against a different intermediate description. The replace/append pair stays separate (a different consumer: the model's preamble, not the catalog listing), and so does the schema. A fifth shape was considered and rejected: one polymorphic "write whatever tab is open" target, which with twelve tabs would land text wherever focus happened to be at resolve time rather than where the user was looking. **Two deliberate divergences from `agent-builder` over the same Redux record, both documented rather than silent:** `agent_name` is NOT writable here (on this window the name is navigation chrome — footer EntityRef, tab strip, sidebar row), which is safely narrower; and `editor_output_schema` IS writable here, which is broader than the builder's exclusion list. That list groups output schema with model/tools/MCP/skills as "what an agent may REACH", but an output schema is the shape the agent EMITS — authored structured content, the closest thing to a prompt in JSON form, and restructuring it is a job this window exists to do.
