It is gated by the product's own `validateOutputSchema` (the same checker the tab's Validate button runs, so the agent hears the real reason), it cannot be CLEARED from here (turning structured output off changes what every downstream consumer receives), and it is still staged, not saved. Worth reconciling the two manifests in one direction later; flagged in the manifest, not quietly diverged. Validators live in the React-free `agentAdvancedEditorWrite.ts` so a bad shape throws synchronously inside the seam rather than later in a React updater, and the whole catalog patch is parsed before anything dispatches, so a bad `tags` array cannot leave a new description already staged. `agent_id` / `activeTab` are read through REFS, never the render closure — this window can switch agents mid-turn. Also excluded: message templates (nested block arrays with no canonical field-level write path, where a malformed array silently destroys priming turns), model/tools/MCP/skills/variables/context slots, sharing, and all governance. Live-verified with real Badass Agent runs on a throwaway `Quick Test Agent`: asked in ONE message, the agent enumerated the tool enum as exactly the four `editor_*` names and staged them, each ask dialog carrying the manifest description verbatim; Apply landed every value visibly (JSON tab showed the new description / category / tags / system prompt with the priming turn and `modelId` untouched, and the window flipped to `Unsaved`), while the DB stayed unwritten because Save was never pressed. "Keep as is" returned `{ok:false, declined:true}` with no error and a graceful acknowledgement. `agent_name` was refused at the schema level — "not one of them … the call can't be dispatched at all", with the agent listing the four real targets. A schema missing `name` came back to the model verbatim as `client_tool_error: editor_output_schema is not a usable structured-output schema: Missing "name". Add a schema name (letters, numbers, _ or -, max 64). …` with nothing staged (validate-then-apply), and the no-agent-open guard likewise returned its own throw when the window was showing the picker. Error Inspector on the clean load: **zero** `surface-writeback` captures; the captures on the negative runs were the deliberately induced ones, in their own browser sessions. `check:surface-drift` (140 surfaces, 3,441 values) clean, and `type-check` shows the same 12 pre-existing errors (all in `features/brokers/*` and `packages/matrx-agents/*`) before and after — zero introduced. DB mirror (`ui.ui_surface_write_target`) still pending a manifest sync.

- **2026-08-11 — Documents write targets: bounds moved into a pure module, and the name limit corrected 200 → 255 (the real column). A parallel-agent COLLISION resolved by keeping the landed design.** Two agents were assigned `matrx-user/documents` and both built it. The entry below is the design that was pushed first and it is KEPT — same two targets, same `entity` posture, same shared-rename path, same `docRef` — and its ruled-out list, especially the `document_summary` read-twin argument, is better than the competing one and survives verbatim. Only what was genuinely additive was layered on top; a second, parallel target set was NOT merged over the first, which is the collision rule working as intended. **What changed, and why it is not cosmetic: `document_name` was bounded at 200 characters, hand-typed in TWO places** — the literal in the handler and the prose "1-200 characters" in the manifest description — **while the actual column is `varchar(255)`** (`workbench.udt_documents.document_name`, verified against `information_schema`). The surface was refusing titles Postgres accepts, and the entry below reports a forced **250-character** name as correctly rejected when 250 is in fact a legal title — the bound, not the write, was wrong. Both limits now come from a pure `features/data-tables/agent-context/documentWriteValidation.ts` (`DOCUMENT_NAME_MAX_LENGTH = 255`, `DOCUMENT_DESCRIPTION_MAX_LENGTH = 2000`) that the handlers call AND the manifest interpolates into its descriptions, so the number the model reads is the number enforced — the drift the skill warns about, made structurally impossible rather than merely correct today. **Two smaller additive fixes:** validation moved OUT of the async handler bodies into that pure module, so a bad shape throws synchronously before any state is touched (the handlers were `async`, so their guards rejected a promise instead of throwing into the seam directly); and `canEdit` now reads through a `canEditRef` beside the existing `docRef`, because the permission gate was still reading its render closure — the exact staleness the `docRef` comment three lines above it exists to prevent. The validator also rejects a multi-line title (it renders in the page header) and normalizes an empty description to `null`, matching what `updateDocumentDescription` already does with it. **Independently re-verified live after merging, not inherited from the earlier run:** one message produced both ask dialogs carrying the NEW interpolated bounds (`1-255`, `2000`), both applied and persisted, and the agent named the `SENTINEL-MERGED-9902` value it had replaced — **which is the point of a sentinel: on an earlier run an agent CLAIMED it had written a description when the column merely already held the requested text, and a value that matches is not evidence of a write.** A forced 300-character name returned this validator's own throw verbatim (`document_name must be 255 characters or fewer (received 286)…`) with nothing renamed; the agent then retried the same oversize value six times and was refused every time. Error Inspector: zero `surface-writeback` captures on clean loads, one per deliberate invalid call. `check:surface-drift` clean (140 surfaces, 3,433 values); `type-check` delta zero — the 11 pre-existing `features/brokers/*` errors unchanged, plus one `packages/matrx-agents/…/result.test.ts` "Cannot find module 'vitest'" that arrived with main and is untouched here. Test document restored to its original name and description.
- **2026-08-11 — Documents agent-writable (live-verified); THE reference for a composite READ-TWIN that only looks like a write target.** `matrx-user/documents` declares 2 `mode:"entity"` / `applyPolicy:"ask"` targets on the document route — `document_name` and `document_description`, the two human-authored columns on `udt_documents` — landing through the canonical `document-service` setters. `renameDocument` already existed; `updateDocumentDescription` was added by this adoption exactly as `updateWorkbookDescription` was added by the workbooks one, because `document-service`'s own header carries a standing contract to mirror `workbook-service` field-for-field. `commitRename` was refactored into one `applyRename(name)` that the header field's blur/Enter commit AND the write handler both call — the workbooks shape — so an agent rename is indistinguishable from the user typing it, and the blur path still swallows (a blur is trivially retryable) while the agent path throws into an error envelope. `entity` rather than the usually-preferred `draft` for the `workbook_name` / `ScheduleDetail` reason: this route has no Save bar at all — the rename field commits on blur and the description has no inline editor — so `draft`'s "staged for you to review, nothing is saved until you save" would be a LIE in a dialog with nothing to save, and a staged name would sit in an input the user may never focus. **The reusable judgment call is `document_summary`, and it is a trap worth naming.** It reads like the jackpot target — a document is exactly the thing an agent can summarize — but there is no summary column on `udt_documents`: that value is the composite READ-TWIN of the entire identity group (`{id, name, description, source, version, is_public, created_at, updated_at, owner_id, organization_id, original_file_id}`). Writing it would mean writing eleven fields at once, nine of them identity, provenance or timestamps, i.e. the NO list. It is the evidence loop, not a write path, and it moves on its own when the two real targets land; an agent asked to summarize the document writes that prose into `document_description`, which is the library blurb and the natural home for it. The general lesson: **when a candidate target is an object, check whether it is a projection of other values before treating it as a field** — a composite read value has no canonical setter by construction. **The STALE CLOSURE is handled as ordinary practice here, not rediscovered:** handlers read the row through a `docRef` advanced synchronously by the single `commitDocument` that also sets React state, because `applySurfaceWrite` resolves a target's handler BEFORE it awaits that target's confirm. This was not hypothetical — in the live run the agent staged both targets in one message and the seam raised the DESCRIPTION dialog first, so both closures were captured before either write landed. **Per-mount split:** the library route (`app/(core)/documents/page.tsx`) mounts the SAME surface and registers NOTHING on purpose, saying so in code — a roster of N documents gives a single-value write target no addressable subject, and its only mutations are create, import (needs a `File` an agent cannot supply) and delete. **Deliberately NOT writable:** `document_version` (the monotonic concurrency counter — writing it forges the fact other code trusts, the `scratchpad` refusal), `document_id` / `document_owner_id` / `document_organization_id`, `document_source` / `document_original_file_id` (provenance: where a document CAME from is a fact the import flow recorded, and the file id is the ONLY reference to those bytes), both timestamps, `document_is_public` / `can_edit` / `is_owner` (disclosure and permissions), the library browse values, and the document BODY — Univer owns the document model and nothing lifts it to the route, so it is not even a declared READ value and a target for it could not reach a canonical path. Both throws say **"plain text string, not JSON and not JSON-encoded"** in the message itself, on the `scratchpad` lesson: the inline-tool layer parses a JSON-looking argument before the handler sees it, and an agent told only "expected a string" tends to fix that by double-encoding. Live-verified with real Badass Agent runs on `/documents/[id]`: one message changed name + description, each with its own ask dialog carrying the manifest prose verbatim plus "This is saved immediately", Apply landed both and the header updated in place; "Keep as is" left the name untouched and the agent reported a decline, not an error; a `document_version` + owner request produced NO `apply_surface_write` call at all, the agent enumerating exactly the two real targets and explaining that a version counter is system-managed; and a forced 250-character name came back verbatim as `client_tool_error: document_name expects a non-empty string of at most 200 characters.` with nothing renamed (validate-then-apply). Error Inspector on a clean page load — valid apply, no forced invalid — reported "No errors captured", zero `surface-writeback` captures; the deliberate invalid run manufactured its own capture, which is the seam being loud by design. `check:surface-drift` clean (140 surfaces); `type-check` shows the same 11 pre-existing `features/brokers` errors as main, verified by running it on `origin/main` in the same tree. DB mirror sync still pending.


- **2026-08-11 — Tool Registry Lookups admin agent-writable + its FIRST `SurfaceRuntimeProvider` (live-verified); THE reference for a target that must RESOLVE which of several editors is live, and for checking that a target is REACHABLE and not merely correct.** `matrx-admin/lookups` declares ONE `mode:"draft"` / `applyPolicy:"ask"` target, `lookup_draft` (`{name?, description?}`), over a console that is THREE reference tables behind three tabs (`ui.ui_client`, `ui.ui_surface`, `tool.executor`). **The surface was manifest-only** — its own header said "Emitters: NONE YET" — so `LookupsAdminPage` now mounts the provider, fed by a new page-scoped store (`components/LookupsSurfaceRuntime.tsx`) that the three CRUD children and the open row dialog publish into; lifting three tables of state into the page was the alternative and this keeps each child owning what it owns. **Resolution is the whole design problem.** "Set the name" is meaningless until the live editor is known, so the target has NO `tab`/`table`/`row` key for a model to get wrong: it resolves from page state, refuses on two-open or save-in-flight, and publishes a new `lookup_editor` read value (tab, table, create-vs-edit, live input values, `name_editable`) that is simply ABSENT when nothing is open — the evidence loop, and a staged draft reads back through it before anyone saves. **The reachability finding, which is the transferable part:** the row editors are MODAL Radix dialogs, so `pointer-events: none` lands on the body and the floating agent chat cannot be typed into while one is up — an admin therefore CANNOT open a dialog and then ask for help, and a target that only ever wrote into an already-open dialog would have read as correct in code and been dead in use. Applying with nothing open therefore OPENS the New form on the ACTIVE tab and stages there (`CreateStoreInline` in the RAG data-stores adopter, for its stated reason: staging into something the user can SEE is the whole contract of `draft`, and opening a create form is reversible and creates nothing). It still never guesses ACROSS tabs and never picks an existing ROW — editing one requires the admin to have opened it. **`name` is create-only, and that is a correctness gate rather than the usual identity squeamishness:** the save is `upsert(onConflict: "name")`, so a changed name on an EDIT would not rename, it would INSERT A SECOND ROW and leave the original behind — the disabled input is the only thing standing between an agent and a silent duplicate. `matrx-admin/tool-registry` refused `tool_name` on the dispatch-key argument; this is that argument plus a concrete corruption. **ONE object, not two targets**, against the `tool-registry`/`agent-apps` three-target precedent, because their split turns on each field being an independently persisted decision whose rename re-labels a live thing: here `name` is only ever writable on a row that does not exist yet, so there is nothing to re-label, both fields are authored in one dialog and committed by ONE Save, and bundling buys atomicity — the live run showed a mixed `{name, description}` call on an edit rejected WHOLE, with the agent reporting "nothing (not even the description) was staged from it". **NOT writable:** `sort_order` (mechanical ordering), `is_active` (capability — deactivating a client hides every surface linked to it, and the page computes `dependentSurfaceCount` to warn a human first), `client_name` on the surfaces tab (an FK that is *part of* the PK, so it is the `name` argument wearing a select), `parent_executor_name` and the executor `config` JSON + `mcp_server_id` (capability inheritance and the machine dispatch contract — `tool_parameters_schema`'s class; the dialog itself says the MCP link belongs to the provisioning flow), and creating/saving/deleting. There is no hard delete on this page at all and that is deliberate: the service exposes only a soft-delete because these tables are FK targets and removing a row would orphan tools, surfaces and executors. **Two defects found by the live run and fixed here, both invisible to type-check:** (1) an invalid NAME was validated only after the create form had been opened, so a rejected value left a stray empty dialog on screen — the tab is knowable in advance, so the ENTIRE patch (shape, keys, caps, create-only-ness, per-tab pattern) now validates before anything opens or any setter runs; (2) dismissing the surface-write confirm counted as an interaction OUTSIDE the row dialog, which closed the form and DISCARDED the value just staged into it — the row dialogs now `preventDefault` on `onInteractOutside` (Escape, Cancel and the X still close), without which the entire edit path was silently useless. Name patterns and bounds moved to a new dependency-free `features/tool-registry/lookups/lookupsVocabulary.ts` imported by the dialogs, the handler AND the manifest, so the rule a human is validated against, the rule an agent is checked against, and the rule the agent is TOLD cannot drift. All guards read through refs, because `applySurfaceWrite` resolves handlers BEFORE awaiting the confirm. Live-verified with real Badass Agent runs: the ask dialog carried the manifest description verbatim with the per-tab patterns visibly interpolated from the constants, Apply auto-opened a New UI Client form with `matrx-ipad` + description staged in the real inputs, a `chrome-extension` EDIT accepted description-only and refused the rename with the handler's throw word-for-word, "Keep as is" returned `{ok:false, declined:true}` and the agent acknowledged it without an error, a sort-order + deactivate request produced NO tool call at all (the agent enumerated what the target does and does not expose and handed the clicks back), and a forced `"Bad Name With Spaces!!"` came back verbatim as `… is not a valid name for a ui.ui_client row. Required: lowercase letters, digits and hyphens, starting with a letter (e.g. \`matrx-mobile\`)` with nothing staged and no stray dialog. Error Inspector on a clean load (valid applies + a decline, no forced-invalid values): **"No errors captured" — zero of ANY kind.** Nothing was saved by any run: `ui.ui_client` still holds its original 6 rows with original descriptions, verified by SQL. `check:surface-drift` (140 surfaces) + `type-check` clean. **Environment note for the campaign:** a stale `.next` dev cache made every nested `/administration/**` route 404 (including untouched ones like `/administration/agents/mcp-tools`) — it reproduces on clean `main`, so it is not a code defect; `rm -rf .next` fixes it, and mistaking it for one costs an hour. DB mirror sync still pending.
- **2026-08-11 — War Room THREAD agent-writable (live-verified), and `matrx-user/marketing-batches` RULED OUT on the judgment bar.** Two results; the negative one is first because it saves the next agent the trip. **`matrx-user/marketing-batches` does not earn write targets and should not be re-assigned.** Its two mounts are `BatchesTable` (the cross-site job list) and `BatchDetailWorkspace` (one job's items), and NEITHER holds a single authored field: the detail workspace is a header datum grid (site / kind / provider / created / submitted / completed / items), an error banner, and a `MatrxDataTable` of execution items — every value read off a fetched `web.batch_job` row. There is no batch name, no description, no notes, and no configuration composed before a run; there is not even a run button, because batches are created by the system elsewhere. `features/marketing/data/operations-hooks.ts` exports six hooks and all six are `useQuery` reads; the only `batch_job` access anywhere in the frontend is two `select`s in `operations-queries.ts`. The manifest's own intro already said it — "jobs and items are monitor-only records, and the user has no edit path here by design" — so this is a **read-only report**, the `CrawlsTable` precedent squared, and zero YES fields rather than merely fewer than two. Also checked and rejected on the same sweep: `matrx-admin/system-agents` (both providers hardcode `isEditable={false}`, neither mount owns a persisted field, and every authoring route under it is already shadowed by the nested `matrx-user/agent-builder` provider holding those exact targets) and the browse-state-only `matrx-user/crm`, `matrx-user/agents-hub`, `matrx-user/agent-connections`, `matrx-user/connections-skills`. **`matrx-user/war-room-thread` passes and is now live** with 4 ask-policy targets: `thread_note_content` / `append_to_thread_note` (DRAFT, via the same `updateNoteContent` the notes editor's `onChange` dispatches) and `thread_task_title` / `thread_task_status` (ENTITY, via `updateTaskFieldThunk`). Handlers in `features/war-room/components/thread/useWarRoomThreadWriteHandlers.ts`, on the provider inside `ThreadAgentPanel` that already emitted the read twins. **The generalizable mount lesson:** `ThreadTabContent` is a `switch`, so exactly one tab body mounts at a time — handlers in `ThreadNotesTab` / `ThreadTaskTab` would have been unregistered precisely when the agent tab was on screen, the only time an agent runs here. Targets resolve their row through the same `selectActiveNoteId` / `selectThreadTaskId` the scope builder used, so the value the agent read and the row it writes are one record. **The bug the live run caught, and the rule it produced:** notes load LAZILY, so a thread whose Notes tab was never opened has no record in the notes slice — the first run applied `thread_note_content`, the agent reported success, and `workbench.notes` was byte-identical afterwards, because the reducer had nothing to patch and `autoSaveMiddleware` bails on a missing/non-dirty record. Both note handlers now hydrate via `fetchNoteContent` BEFORE writing and read the buffer back, throwing if it did not take: **"not loaded" is never "empty", and a draft target that silently no-ops is worse than one that refuses.** Thread TITLE stays undeclared — already writable as `active_thread_title` on `matrx-user/war-room`; recording stays human; transcripts and the conversation stay evidence. Live-verified with a real Badass Agent run on a thread in the "Acme acquisition" room: one message asking for three targets produced THREE ask dialogs, each carrying the manifest description verbatim plus the seam's mode suffix ("It is staged for you to review…" for the draft, "This is saved immediately." for the entity pair), and both entity writes were confirmed in `workspace.tasks` by SQL (title `Acme diligence - verify export scope`, status `planned`); "Keep as is" on the append returned `{ok:false, declined:true}` and the agent reported "You declined the append, so the note is unchanged" without retrying; a request to rename the thread and set the room description produced NO `apply_surface_write` call at all; and a forced `'banana-status'` came back as the handler's own throw verbatim — `thread_task_status must be one of: inbox, planned, active, completed, cancelled, dismissed. Received "banana-status".` Error Inspector, read in the SAME session as the valid runs: the ONLY `surface-writeback` capture is that deliberate contract break — the control that makes the clean paths' zero mean something. The note pair was then confirmed end-to-end by SQL on a second run: `workbench.notes` held exactly `## Diligence recap\n\nExport scope confirmed with counsel.` after the Apply, so the draft → autosave round trip is real. **But chasing that row surfaced a second, sharper defect, and it is the one worth copying:** the note the write landed in was `deleted_at`-stamped. `selectActiveNoteId` filters assignments on `is_active`, NOT on `deleted_at`, so a thread can still point at a soft-deleted note — and a write there succeeds all the way to the database while the user never sees a character of it. That is the worst possible shape of "applied", strictly worse than the silent no-op above, because the evidence loop confirms it. Both note handlers now refuse a deleted target by name and tell the agent to ask for a new note instead. **The generalizable rule: an id that resolves is not the same as a row the user can see — check the tombstone before writing, not just the existence.** `check:surface-drift` + `type-check` clean. DB mirror sync still pending.
- **2026-08-11 — Marketing Discovery inbox agent-writable.** The Discovery inbox now declares and handles its authored classification state through the surface runtime, reusing the same classification paths as the human controls while leaving crawl execution, identity, and destructive lifecycle actions undeclared.

- **2026-08-11 — AI Model Registry admin agent-writable (live-verified); THE reference for building the human CORRECTION PATH before the target.** `matrx-admin/ai-models` declares 2 `mode:"entity"` / `applyPolicy:"ask"` targets over the only AUTHORED COPY on an `ai.model_definition` row — `model_description` and `model_common_name` — landing through `aiModelService.update`, the exact call the detail panel's Save button makes. **Registered by `AiModelDetailPanel` via `useSurfaceWriteHandlers`, not by `AiModelsContainer`.** The container mounts the provider and owns the catalogue + browse state; the detail pane owns the form state, the canonical save path, and the `saving` flag a write has to respect. Both sit inside the SAME provider — unlike `schedules` or `shapes` the pane is a child of the list mount, not a second surface mount — so the per-mount split still applies in its other direction: the list registers NOTHING, because the only state it owns is browse state, which is not writable here. **The new lesson: when a candidate field has no human editor, the answer is not automatically "leave it out" — it is "check whether building the editor is the smaller change".** `description` is a real column rendered to USERS as the second line of every model-picker row (`lab/ModelListDropdown` via `useModelCatalog`), and this manifest already declared it as a read value calling it "the most common agent job here" — but NO admin could set it: the Details form had no field, and the panel's `AI_MODEL_COLUMNS` whitelist silently STRIPPED a pasted `description` from the Raw JSON tab. That is exactly `admin-tool-registry`'s `tool_group` one-way door, and taking it at face value would have left the surface with a single target and failed the judgment bar. Instead the same change added the Description textarea to `AiModelForm` and `description` to the whitelist — roughly twenty lines inside the feature already being touched — so the fix an admin needs to correct an agent exists BEFORE the agent can write. `tool_group`'s own note anticipated this ("if the editor ever gains the field, adding the target here is a few lines"); this is that case taken up rather than deferred. **DELIBERATELY NOT WRITABLE:** `model_capabilities`, `model_context_window`, `model_max_tokens` (enforced limits and a capability map the settings engine and every picker read), `model_is_deprecated` / `model_is_primary` / `model_is_premium` / `model_visibility` (governance — deprecation IS the activation signal on this table, and this is the `tool_is_active` / `tool_gating` line), `model_fallback_ids` + the retry ceiling (substitution ROUTING — which other model answers for a guest or an over-limit caller), `model_cost_rating` / `model_speed_rating` (cost-shaped: they render as the $-tier and speed dots users pick by), `model_release_date` (a fact to look up, and no editor either), and hardest of all `model_name` — the provider-facing dispatch key AND the join key `ProviderDataTab` matches the provider cache on, so a wrong one breaks every call site at once. **Handlers refuse by THROW on three moments, not just three shapes:** create-new mode (`is_creating_model` — no row to update, and creating is the admin's flow), a save already in flight, and a DIRTY RAW JSON TAB. That last one generalizes: the Raw JSON tab saves the WHOLE row, so an unsaved edit there would silently overwrite the agent's write on the admin's next Save — a clobber a per-field handler cannot otherwise see coming. On success each handler patches the landed value into BOTH `formData` and `baseline`, so the panel shows what happened and the field does not read as an unsaved edit a later Save would revert with a stale value. Bounds and validators live in the new `features/ai-models/model-metadata.ts` and are imported by the manifest AND the handlers, so the contract the model reads IS the contract enforced (`admin-tool-registry`'s `tool-metadata.ts` pattern). `model_common_name` rejects the empty string although the column is nullable — every picker falls back to the raw provider model id when it is blank, so "clearing" it silently swaps a curated label for a wire id. Live-verified with a real Badass Agent run on `/administration/ai/ai-models` against Claude Haiku 4.5, selected by NAME: ONE message asking for both fields produced TWO separate confirm dialogs carrying the manifest descriptions verbatim, Apply landed both and they persisted to `ai.model_definition` (verified in the DB, not just the DOM); "Keep as is" on a re-run returned a decline the agent acknowledged without an error and wrote nothing; asking for context window + deprecation drew a loud refusal that enumerated the two real targets and explicitly declined to fake them by editing the description instead; and a forced 131-character common name came back to the agent word-for-word as `model_common_name is 131 characters; the maximum is 80.` with nothing staged (validate-then-apply confirmed in the DB). Error Inspector in the SAME session as the valid run: ZERO `surface-writeback` captures on the clean path, and exactly 2 on the deliberately-invalid load — the seam working as designed, and the control that makes the zero mean something. `check:surface-drift` clean (140 surfaces); `type-check` adds none of its own (the 11 remaining errors are pre-existing, all in `features/brokers/*`). The test model's values were restored afterwards. DB mirror sync still pending.
- **2026-08-11 — USER SETTINGS agent-writable (live-verified) — THE durable-surface reference, and the sharpest exclusion list in the campaign.** `matrx-user/settings` declares 7 `mode:"entity"` / `applyPolicy:"ask"` targets: `theme_mode`, `accent_theme`, `display_layout`, `text_generation_style`, `language_defaults`, `assistant_name`, `voice_persona`. Every handler dispatches `getSliceBinding(slice).write(key, value)` — literally the action `useSetting`'s setter dispatches — so an agent write and a user's click on the control are the same store action, and the warm-cache sync policy (IDB + localStorage + Supabase) picks it up identically. Nothing reaches past the store. **Why `ask` on everything, including the cosmetic targets:** settings have no draft/save step, and the synced tier follows the user to every device and every future session. A `ui`-mode surface would give a theme flip `auto` because it is cheap, visible and self-correcting; here it is none of those — the app is still repainted on their phone tomorrow. The docblock says so, so a later reader does not "optimize" it. **The interesting structural problem was READ ACCESS.** Every settings tab is a lazy component whose controls read Redux via `useSetting`, and there is no registry of per-tab setting keys — which is why this manifest previously emitted only navigation (`active_tab_id` and friends) and honestly said the values were not enumerable. Rather than emit per-tab, the emitter now reads these seven straight from the slices, so they are readable AND writable from ANY settings tab: an agent never has to navigate the user to Appearance to change the theme, and every target has a 1:1 read twin for the evidence loop. `active_tab_id` itself stays read-only — it is where the user IS, and making it writable would be the mechanical "move the user around" toggle the judgment bar excludes. New dependency-free `features/settings/agent-writable-settings.ts` is THE vocabulary: the four tabs now render their `<SettingsSelect>` options from it, the manifest interpolates its `*_ENUM_TEXT` into the contract prose, and the handlers validate with its guards — one list behind all three, following the `scrape-command.ts` precedent. It also collapsed a real drift: Appearance, Language, Voice and Text Generation each carried a hand-typed language list, and text generation was silently missing hi/nl/pl/sv/tr. Bundling follows the skill's rule — `display_layout` (4 shell selects), `text_generation_style` (tone+creativity) and `language_defaults` (3 per-feature languages) are partial-patch objects because each is ONE decision made on one screen, while theme mode, accent and the assistant name stay separate because a user may well want the theme and not the density. `SettingsSelect`/`SettingsSegmented` `options` widened to `readonly` (type-only) so `as const` vocabularies pass without a spread. **The exclusions are the deliverable as much as the targets**, and the manifest docblock argues each: account identity (the whole Profile family), security (passwords, MFA, sessions, API keys), billing, organization membership and roles, capability governance (AI Models, Admin Server — the `admin-ai-models` line), the ENTIRE Privacy tab (`alwaysWatching` turns on screen observation and auto knowledge-graph turns on background analysis of the user's notes and files — an agent widening its own observation window is a privacy escalation, not a preference), integrations and connected accounts (the `marketing-integrations` credentials line, which bites harder here because it is the user's own account), navigation, and the experimental/transient shell toggles (`layout.isInWindow`, windowManager session actions). Only the route mount registers handlers; the WindowPanel/drawer settings shell mounts no provider for this surface and registers nothing. Handlers refuse while an autosave flush is in flight, reading `is_saving` through `store.getState()` at call time rather than a render closure — `applySurfaceWrite` resolves handler closures BEFORE the confirm is answered, so a captured render value can be stale. Live-verified with real Badass Agent runs on `/user-settings` (every value restored afterwards): one message drove four targets, each ask dialog carrying the manifest description verbatim with the enums interpolated, and Apply visibly moved the controls (accent → Ocean, dashboard → Compact, sidebar → Auto-collapse, color mode → dark); "Keep as is" on the assistant name left it as "Assistant" and the agent reported the decline without retrying; a second run applied all three language defaults → Spanish plus formal/low text style and declined `voice_persona`, leaving the emotion field empty. A request to change the account email and password produced NO `apply_surface_write` call at all — the agent enumerated its seven targets, said account identity and security are deliberately not exposed here, separately refused to relay a password, and pointed at the right tabs. A forced `accent_theme: "neon-pink"` returned the handler's own throw verbatim to the agent (`client_tool_error: accent_theme expects one of: default | night | forest | ocean | sunset. Got "neon-pink".`) with the accent unchanged. **Worth recording for the next agent:** on the first attempt the model CLAIMED the page rejected the value without ever calling the tool — no confirm dialog appeared and the "verbatim" error it quoted was empty. A fabricated rejection looks exactly like a real one in the transcript; the tell is the missing ask dialog, and the fix is to check the toast/Error Inspector rather than trust the reply. Error Inspector on a clean load (valid applies + a decline, no forced-invalid write): zero `surface-writeback` captures. `check:surface-drift` (140 surfaces) + `type-check` clean. DB mirror sync still pending.
- **2026-08-11 — Image Library agent-writable (live-verified); THE selection-and-query reference, and the STALE-SCOPE trap written down.** `matrx-user/images` declares 3 `mode:"ui"` / `applyPolicy:"ask"` targets — `search_query`, `recents_only`, `image_selection` — all handled on `components/image/cloud/CloudImagesTab.tsx`'s own provider (`getWriteHandlers`), which is both the mount and the owner of all three state atoms, so each handler calls the exact setter the human control calls: `setQuery` (the search box), `setShowRecentsOnly` (the Recents chip), `setBulkSelectedIds` (a tile checkbox). **This surface authors nothing** — there is no editor on the page — so the adoption is deliberately the `keyword_selection` class rather than the `marketing-page` class: the useful agent job is "pick out the images matching this description", and `image_selection` takes the WHOLE selection as an id array (replace, not append; `[]` clears), validating every id against the rows actually rendered and rejecting the entire call on one bad id so a selection can never half-apply. Validation runs against the live `imageFiles` rather than the emitted `visible_image_ids` (capped at 200) precisely so the cap can never cause a false rejection of an id the agent legitimately read. The array also tolerates the double-encoded JSON-string form explicitly — the inline-tool layer parses a JSON-looking argument before the handler sees it, and a model told only "expected an array" tends to escape harder — while `recents_only` accepts a real boolean plus the exact `"true"`/`"false"` strings and throws on anything else rather than guessing at truthiness. **Why all three are `ask` when `ui` is the one mode where `auto` is defensible:** the tab prunes `bulkSelectedIds` to the visible rows whenever the visible set changes, so `search_query` and `recents_only` do not merely re-filter tiles — they silently SHRINK the selection, and that selection is the live argument to the Delete and Move buttons on screen; an agent quietly narrowing the filter under a user about to click Delete changes what gets deleted. Same call as `markdown-studio`'s `view_mode`, for the same "bigger than it looks" reason. **Deliberately NOT targets:** the bulk actions (download / move / visibility / DELETE) — the destructive line the skill draws, so an agent's reach stops at proposing WHICH images; and `view_mode` (cozy/compact/list), the pure-mechanical display toggle the judgment bar names as a NO, which unlike the other two shapes neither the visible set nor the selection. **The new lesson, worth reusing on any list surface: a surface scope is captured at RUN time, so a read value describing "what is visible" goes STALE the instant a filter target lands.** The live run proved it — the agent applied `search_query: "sample"` (30 → 2 images), then selected from its launch-time snapshot of all 30 and was rejected. The fix is not coercion: the throw now lists the live rows back as `id (filename)` and the target description warns that the id list goes stale after a filter write, which turned a guess into a one-step correction — the agent's own words were "my first selection attempt was correctly rejected and handed back the true live set of 2, which I then selected successfully". Live-verified with a real Badass Agent run on `/images/my-cloud` against a 30-image library: both dialogs carried the manifest description verbatim, Apply landed `search="sample"` / `2 selected` visibly on the page, "Keep as is" on `recents_only` returned `{ok:false, declined:true}` with the view untouched and the agent acknowledging the decline without an error, and a "select these two and DELETE them permanently, or move them" request selected the pair and then REFUSED the destructive half in words ("The actual destructive/move actions are gated to you: I cannot press Delete (or Move) on your behalf"), handing the one click back — with NO tool call attempted, so that refusal produced no capture at all. A forced `00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000` came back to the agent verbatim as `image_selection rejected: 1 of the 1 id(s) you sent are not among the 2 image(s) currently visible …` with the existing selection untouched. Error Inspector on the clean load: exactly ONE `surface-writeback` capture — the agent's own stale-id attempt, which is the guard firing as designed, not a defect (the other 61 entries are `/files/<id>/asset` fetch timeouts from this sandbox's egress proxy, unrelated to the surface). `check:surface-drift` + `type-check` (the 11 remaining errors are pre-existing, all in `features/brokers/*`) + the existing `CloudImagesTab` jest suite clean. Nothing was created or deleted by the run. DB mirror sync still pending.
- **2026-08-11 — Scribe Live working document agent-writable; THE reference for a surface that already had a bespoke agent path serving a DIFFERENT agent population.** `matrx-user/transcript-scribe-live` declares 2 `applyPolicy:"ask"` / `mode:"entity"` targets over the one artifact the Live tab exists to build: `working_document_content` (full replace) and `append_working_document` (add a block). Handlers in `features/transcript-studio/hooks/useScribeLiveWriteHandlers.ts`, registered on `ScribeLiveScreen`'s existing `SurfaceRuntimeProvider`; both dispatch `updateWorkingDocumentContentThunk`, the same thunk `useWorkingDocumentDraft`'s debounced autosave fires on the user's own keystrokes and the same one the realtime mutators call. **Why these targets are not redundant with the tools already there:** the Live tab already let the CLIENT-SIDE VOICE agent mutate this document (`scribe_working_doc_append` / `_append_heading` in `realtimeWorkingDocTools.ts`), but those are reachable only from inside the xAI realtime turn loop — the agent the user is talking to. An agent launched from the header Agents popover is an ordinary turn-based run with no realtime socket: it could already READ `working_document_content` and had no way to write a word of it back. These close that half of the loop for that second population, and add full REPLACEMENT, which the realtime mutators deliberately never offered. **Two doctrine calls worth copying, both the mirror image of the sibling `matrx-user/chat-voice`.** (1) `entity`, not `draft`: the working document has no draft/save bar, only an autosaving editor, so there is no staging layer to land in and `draft` would be a lie. (2) NO phase guard: where chat-voice refuses every write while a session is live (its voice and instructions are consumed once, at connect), this document is MEANT to change mid-session — `useWorkingDocumentDraft` explicitly merges remote edits in whenever the user is not actively typing, so refusing here would break the collaboration loop rather than protect it. The document row is resolved from the store at CALL time via `selectWorkingDocument` (which filters `kind === "working_document"`), because `applySurfaceWrite` resolves the handler BEFORE showing the confirm dialog and an append built on a body captured in a render closure would silently drop whatever the user or the voice agent wrote while the dialog sat open; a session with no working document refuses loudly rather than creating one. Undeclared by design: identity (`working_document_id`, `session_id`, `live_agent_id`, `voice_conversation_id`), the derived `working_document_word_count`, microphone/connection state (device state — muting someone's mic is not a copy edit), and the spoken transcript with its per-turn and interruption record (writing it would forge what was actually said — the `voice-pad` input-vs-output line). Live-verified with a real agent run on `/transcripts/scribe/:sessionId`: the ask dialog carried the manifest description verbatim plus the entity suffix "This is saved immediately", Apply landed the append through the handler, the Working Document header updated on screen, and the row was confirmed in Postgres (`studio_documents` `kind='working_document'`, written 15:29:57Z) — while the session's other document row (`kind='cleanup_custom'`) was correctly left untouched, which is the check that proves the handler targets the right artifact. `check:surface-drift` (140 surfaces, 3,417 values) clean; `type-check` clean apart from the 11 pre-existing `features/brokers/*` errors. **Harness note for the next agent on this route:** the Scribe shell renders inside a narrow column, so the header's "Agents for this page" trigger and the message composer are both present but zero-size/hidden — Playwright's `click`/`fill` time out and a direct DOM `click()` plus a native-setter `input` dispatch are required. DB mirror (`ui.ui_surface_write_target`) still pending a manifest sync.
- **2026-08-11 — Keyword Research hardening + RESTORED the surfaces docs its merge dropped.** Two things, and the second is the reusable warning. **(1) The docs gap:** `matrx-user/keyword-research`'s 3 write targets shipped (`research_input_keyword` / `library_search` / `cluster_scope`), and the feature's own `FEATURE.md` landed an entry that says "see `features/surfaces/FEATURE.md` for the full [detail]" — but THIS file had no adopter clause and no Change Log entry for it at all. That is the exact hazard the 2026-08-10 Agent-Builder entry below already warns about, happening again: **a merge that resolves a `FEATURE.md` conflict by taking one side wholesale silently reverts documentation for shipped code**, and it leaves a dangling cross-reference pointing at nothing. The adopter clause above is restored. Reconcile BOTH sides of this file by hand. **(2) The hardening**, all on top of the landed design, no target added or changed: validation moved OUT of the two components into the pure `features/marketing/seo/keyword-research/keyword-research-write.ts`, which also now OWNS `MAX_STAGED_KEYWORD_LENGTH` — it used to be a `const` inside `KeywordResearchLauncher` while the manifest description said "at most 200 characters" as a hardcoded literal, so the advertised contract and the enforced one could drift; the description interpolates the constant now. The staged-keyword throw says **"plain text, not JSON and not JSON-encoded"** in the message itself, because the inline-tool layer pre-parses a JSON-looking argument before the handler sees it and an agent told only "expected a string" tends to "fix" that by double-encoding, landing escaped `\n` and stray quotes in the user's box. And two closure reads became REF reads: the launcher's in-flight guard (`run.status`) and — the one that actually decides WHERE a value lands — `cluster_scope`'s append base (`clusterPhrases` / `clusterPrimaryKeyword`). The writeback seam resolves EVERY handler closure BEFORE the user confirms the first ask dialog, so in a multi-target turn an append could have extended a cluster list no longer on screen, scoping the explorer to phrases the user never saw. Live-verified on the landed code with real Badass Agent runs: one message drove all three targets (each dialog carrying the manifest prose verbatim, including the interpolated bound), the agent then CLEARED the filter through the same handler when the cluster ∩ filter matched nothing — proving the empty-string-legal path — and a deliberate `{"phrase":"…"}` object came back to the model verbatim as `research_input_keyword expects a plain text string — it arrived as an object. Send the phrase as plain text, not JSON and not JSON-encoded…`, with the agent explicitly concluding "not a JSON object and not a JSON-encoded/quoted string" and nothing staged — the double-encode retry this text exists to prevent did not happen. No paid run or volume refresh was triggered. `check:surface-drift` clean (140 surfaces); `type-check` introduces no new errors (the repo's full-repo baseline is already red — see the "unusable full-repo lint baseline" commit). **Campaign signal:** this surface was assigned at least FOUR times in parallel (like scraper 4x and `matrx-admin/email` 2x). Two full designs were pushed before this one started — `claude/surface-write-keyword-research` (which landed) and `claude/surface-write-keyword-research-selection` (still unmerged, and its `keyword_selection` target is the one idea neither landed design has). The fourth agent stood down from shipping a third design rather than clobber the canonical branch, and spent its budget here instead. Keep the collision check mandatory in every chip, and check `agent.review_queue` for the surface's URL before starting.
- **2026-08-11 — Analysis Studio agent-writable.** `matrx-user/analysis-studio` now exposes ask-policy writes for annotation labels, extracted text, and focused annotation. `StudioShell` registers handlers that reuse the existing annotation update path and reject missing or stale annotation ids, invalid label/category combinations, concurrent writes, geometry, redaction, deletion, and other human-only disclosure or pointer decisions.
- **2026-08-11 — Voice Pad: restored the adopter-list entry a parallel merge dropped, and independently re-verified the merged surface live.** No new targets — this is the collision rule applied. Several agents built `matrx-user/voice-pad` in parallel; the reconciled result (`draft_text` + `append_draft_text`, both plain strings) merged first and is the design that stands, so the competing shape this agent had built and verified (ONE `draft_text` object target taking `{text, mode: 'replace' | 'append'}`, the `transcripts-cleanup` / `marketing-page` idiom) was DISCARDED rather than layered on top. Both designs were sound; the merged one wins because it merged, and re-shaping a live, human-verifiable surface to a second agent's taste is churn, not contribution. **What was actually missing:** the surface had a Change Log entry here and in `features/audio/FEATURE.md`, but was never added to the "Live agent-writable adopters" list above — the THIRD instance of the merge hazard the 2026-08-10 "Restored the Agent Builder write-target docs" entry named, and the second in as many days. Both prior instances lost adopter-list clauses specifically, which is the part of this doc a merge resolver is most likely to treat as one side's prose rather than an index. **Independently re-verified against merged main** (as it stood before the mic-state guard entry above landed) with a real Badass Agent run on the pad opened over `/vault` (unmapped route): one message asking for a cleanup AND an append produced two per-target ask dialogs carrying the manifest descriptions verbatim, Apply landed the cleaned paragraph and then appended a line behind a blank-line separator with the rewrite byte-preserved; "Keep as is" left the buffer untouched with a graceful acknowledgement; a forced `transcript_entries` write returned the seam's `No mounted surface declares write target "transcript_entries".` verbatim with no dialog; and a forced object value returned the handler's own `draft_text expects a plain text string, got object. Send the text itself — do not wrap it in an object or JSON-encode it.` with the pad unchanged. Error Inspector: **zero** `surface-writeback` captures on the clean path. `check:surface-drift` + `type-check` clean. **Confirms the merged note's route caveat from a second direction:** on `/dashboard` (mapped → `matrx-user/dashboard`) the run carried Dashboard's EMPTY scope while still being offered the pad's write targets, and the agent correctly declined to invent the dictation it could not see, asking the user to paste it instead — the split is real, reproducible, and diagnosable only if you know to check the host route first. **No chips fired, and the scan behind that.** Every manifest without `writeTargets` was listed against its readiness, declared-value count, and whether any `.tsx` actually mounts a `SurfaceRuntimeProvider` for its surface name (the grep is for the COMPONENT, not `surfaceName={`, so context-menu-only scope emitters do not count as mounts). Sixty manifests remain unwritten; of those, the ~30 with a live mount are exhaustively: surfaces already taken (`documents`), read-only reports and analysis OUTPUT (`marketing-analysis` / `-audit` / `-authority` / `-backlinks` / `-coverage` / `-crawl` / `-findings` / `-links` / `-ranks-hub` / `-reputation` / `-site-pages` / `-sitemaps`), immutable evidence viewers (`rag-viewer` — a document's pages, text and OCR provenance, the same line `voice-pad` drew around its transcript — plus `file-preview`, `image-viewer`, `gallery`), pure list/search/filter view state (`rag-library`, `education-flashcards`, `agents-hub`, `podcast`, `marketing` hub, `dashboard`, `settings`, `education` hub), capability or permission governance (`agent-connections`, `marketing-integrations`, `sandboxes`, `scopes`, `share`), outward-facing sending (`messages`), diagnostics (`context-preview`), and near-empty stubs with zero declared values (`marketing-competitors`, `transcript-scribe`, `crm-manager`). `files` is the closest call and still fails: 42 values that are navigation, selection, column/sort view state and file metadata, with rename living in a separate `RenameHost` dialog and no authored prose an agent would write. The surfaces with genuinely rich editable state that are left — `transcript-studio` (`stub`, 1 value), `extractor-chunker`, `observational-memory`, `agent-advanced-editor` — mount NO runtime at all, so they are `surface-authoring` jobs first and would be bad chips for this skill by its own rule. This independently reproduces the conclusion recorded in the 2026-08-11 data-tables handoff, whose two named remaining candidates (smart-code-editor, scribe-live) have both since landed. **The write-target well is dry; the campaign's next move is `surface-authoring` on the four emitter-less editors above, not more write-target chips.**
- **2026-08-11 — Context Items agent-writable.** `matrx-user/context-items` now exposes five ask-policy targets through `ContextItemsWriteTargets`, mounted with the existing Context Items hub. Writes reuse the surface's canonical state paths and preserve identity, lifecycle, and undeclared operations as human-only.
- **2026-08-11 — Content Plan Entities: restored the write-half docs a parallel merge dropped, deleted the merge debris, and re-verified the surface live.** No new targets — this is reconciliation of ALREADY-MERGED work plus an independent verification, which is what the collision rule asks for when a surface is taken while you are on it. Two agents built `matrx-user/content-plan-entities` in parallel and both merged; main resolved to the "lifted draft" CODE while the DOCS that survived came from the other, superseded design, so the repo described targets that do not exist. **What is actually live:** 3 ask-policy targets registered by `EntityManager.tsx` through `useSurfaceWriteHandlers` against a parent-owned `draftRef` — `entity_draft` (`draft`, stages `{label?, entity_type?}` into the OPEN editor dialog and throws when none is open), `save_entity_draft` (`entity`, commits that draft through the dialog's own create/update path), and `add_entities` (`entity`, appends roster rows through `createPlanEntity` with duplicate-label refusal and a post-write label check). Read twin: `entity_editor_draft`. **Deleted as debris:** `features/marketing/content-plan/lib/entity-write-targets.ts` and its 21-test suite — they validated the superseded `open_entity_editor` / `create_entity` targets and an `entity_draft` that accepted `source_type_id`, which the shipped target explicitly refuses; nothing imported them but their own test, so main was shipping a passing test suite certifying a contract no code implements. The superseded entry in the content-plan FEATURE.md is now marked as such rather than deleted, and its claim that `source_type_options` was added as a read value is corrected — it never was. **This surface had NO adopter-list entry and NO Change Log entry here at all**; the merge dropped both sides' surfaces docs even though the code shipped. That is the second instance of the hazard the 2026-08-10 "Restored the Agent Builder write-target docs" entry warned about, and it argues the warning belongs where merges actually land, not only in this log. **Independently re-verified with a real Badass Agent run** (prpinjectionmd roster, 44 entities): `add_entities` asked with its manifest description verbatim, applied, and persisted a real row (removed afterwards via the roster trash button); a same-message delete request AND a node-attachment request were both refused with the agent enumerating the targets it actually has and naming the node surface as the right home for attachment; `entity_draft` with no editor open returned its own throw to the agent verbatim. `check:surface-drift` (140 surfaces, 3,410 values) clean; content-plan jest suites pass; `type-check` shows the same 11 pre-existing `features/brokers/*` errors before and after, and zero new ones. **Open usability finding, deliberately NOT fixed** (it is the subject of a pending review-queue question, and pre-empting a human decision is not additive): `entity_draft` requires the editor dialog to already be open, but that dialog is MODAL — with it open, a `fixed inset-0 z-[10000]` `aria-hidden` overlay covers both the header "Agents for this page" button and the chat composer. Measured, not argued: `elementFromPoint` over the Agents button returns the overlay and a real click times out, while the identical click succeeds once the dialog is closed. So the target is reachable only by starting the turn with the editor closed and opening it while the agent is mid-thought — a race the user has to win, and the agent's own advice ("click Edit, then tell me and I'll re-run") is impossible because the overlay covers the composer it is asking the user to type into. Letting the handler OPEN the editor on a row named in the value would remove the race entirely; that is the call the review row asks Arman to make.
- **2026-08-11 — Agent Review Queue agent-writable; THE self-dealing reference, and THE no-selected-row reference.** `matrx-admin/agent-review` declares 2 ask-policy targets on the Agent Repair Board. `review_feedback_draft` (`mode:"draft"`) stages review prose into a row's feedback textarea; `review_triage_classification` (`mode:"entity"`) re-classifies `lane` / `priority` / `workstreams` / `required_tools`. Handlers in `features/admin/agent-review/components/AgentReviewWriteTargets.tsx`. **The exclusion is the whole point of this adopter.** This queue is where agents register the work they built for Arman to check, so the STATUS transitions — Approve, Request changes, Archive, Restore — are not declared as targets at any policy. Not `ask`, not "manual for now": absent. An agent approving or archiving its own review row is self-dealing, and an `ask` dialog would still park a sign-off one click away from the artifact under review; the human presses the button that changes state, and an agent's contribution is the feedback text and the routing. `id`/`created_at`/`source` (identity and provenance), `url` (re-aiming a review at something the reviewer never saw), and `show_archived` (a mechanical view filter) are excluded too. **No selected row, so the row is an argument.** The page renders the entire queue and has no selection concept, so both targets take a `row_id` drawn from `queue_sample` and refuse loudly when it is missing or not loaded — the alternative, inferring a row from position or from an ancestor's text, edits somebody else's review item. **Draft mode required lifting real state:** the feedback buffer lived in `ReviewItemCard`'s `useState`, where the surface could neither publish it nor stage into it, and an entity write would have left the admin's textarea showing stale text next to a changed database. It now lives in `AgentReviewClient` as `feedbackDrafts` keyed by row id, published as the new `feedback_drafts` read value (only genuinely unsaved text — an entry that matches what is stored is not pending anything), and the handler calls the same setter the admin's typing calls. **The triage target patches, never replaces:** `metadata` is a versioned envelope, so it goes through the page's own `metadataWithReviewTriage` + `updateReviewQueueRow` pair — the exact path the "Apply suggested triage" button uses — validating the RESULT against `reviewTriageSchema` and carrying `version`, `assignment` and `verification` through untouched (claim state belongs to the `agent-review-queue` skill's atomic SQL protocol; a page write would stomp another agent's live claim). A row whose triage is missing or invalid is based on the page's own deterministic `suggestReviewTriage`, with the agent's fields applied on top. **The error-swallowing trap here is RLS, not optimism:** `updateReviewQueueRow` does throw on a Postgres error, but a super-admin RLS policy filters a forbidden UPDATE to ZERO rows without raising anything, so a non-admin would have been told "saved" forever. `refresh()` now resolves with the rows it just read, and the entity handler re-reads and throws unless the new classification is actually there. Live-verified with real Badass Agent runs against a throwaway queue row (created and deleted for the test — never a row Arman is triaging): ONE message asking for both changes produced two per-target ask dialogs carrying the manifest descriptions verbatim, Apply landed both (`{ok:true, mode:"draft"}` / `{ok:true, mode:"entity"}`), and expanding the row showed the agent's text sitting in the real `Feedback for …` textarea beside Save feedback / Request changes / Approve while the DB still had `feedback: null` — draft staged, nothing saved. The triage persisted (verified over MCP: lane `browser_ui`, priority `high`, workstreams `[responsive_ui, verification]`, tools `[browser, frontend_code]`, with `assignment`/`verification`/`version` intact). "Keep as is" returned `{ok:false, declined:true}` and left the row alone. A deliberately invalid enum came back to the model as the handler's own throw verbatim (`review_triage_classification: priority must be one of critical | high | normal | low (got "urgent").`) with the stored priority still `high` — validate-then-apply — and an unknown `row_id` was refused the same way. Error Inspector on the clean load (valid applies + a decline, no bad values): **zero** `surface-writeback` captures; the captures from the invalid-value probes were deliberately induced and live in their own browser session, since a handler throw is a capture by design. `check:surface-drift` (140 surfaces, 3,411 values) + `type-check` clean — the 11 remaining type errors are pre-existing and all in `features/brokers/*`. The surface has no `ui_surface` row yet, so the DB mirror waits on seeding + manifest sync; the client tool works either way, as this run shows.
- **2026-08-11 — Scanner write-target docs restored + two contract defects fixed; the adopter list's missing entry.** The scanner's write targets (`scan_title`, `scan_page_labels`) shipped with working CODE but **no record in this file** — `features/pdf/FEATURE.md`'s own entry closed with "See the surfaces FEATURE.md Change Log for the full verification record," and that record did not exist, nor did `matrx-user/scanner` appear in the agent-writable adopter list above. This is the second instance of the hazard the 2026-08-10 "Restored the Agent Builder write-target docs a merge dropped" entry names, so it is worth stating as a rule: **a `FEATURE.md` conflict resolved by taking one side wholesale silently reverts documentation for shipped code, and the campaign's cross-file pointers are how you notice.** The adopter clause above is now written; nothing about the shipped design changed. Two real defects found while reading it, both fixed: (1) **the constants block had drifted from the handler it exists to bind.** `features/pdf/scanner/types.ts` declares `SCAN_TITLE_MAX_LENGTH` / `SCAN_PAGE_LABEL_MAX_LENGTH` with a comment stating the limits live there so "what the agent is TOLD and what the page ACCEPTS cannot drift apart" — but the live handler in `ScannerSurfaceRuntime.tsx` re-typed its own `MAX_LABEL_CHARS = 120` literal and never imported them, while the page-label constant still read `80`. The manifest advertises 120 and the shipped handler enforced 120, so the constant was the wrong one: it is now 120, `assertLabelString` takes the bound per target, and both handlers import from `./types`. Enforcement is byte-identical to what shipped — this closes the drift without touching verified behaviour. (2) **a superseded handler draft was still in the tree.** `features/pdf/scanner/useScannerWriteHandlers.ts` was imported by nothing, yet implemented a CONTRADICTORY contract — positional-array `scan_page_labels`, an undeclared third target `scan_page_label`, an 80-character bound, and a doc block asserting "Position, not identity, is the agent's coordinate system… An agent must never have to know about `itemId`", the exact inverse of the shipped id-keyed map — and it was `types.ts`'s only reader, which is how that constant drifted. Deleted; the `types.ts` comment now names its real consumer. **Independently re-verified live** (real Badass Agent runs on `/tools/scanner`, three uploaded pages, values read back from the actual DOM inputs rather than the agent's summary): ONE message asking for a title and three page labels produced two per-target ask dialogs carrying the manifest prose verbatim, and Apply landed both — title `"Q3 Vendor Invoices"`, labels `["Invoice cover","Signature page","Appendix A"]` — which also clears the multi-target stale-closure trap (`applySurfaceWrite` resolves every handler before the first confirm; the post-commit refs held). Staged values survived a page reload via the resumable manifest. "Keep as is" left the title at `"Q3 Vendor Invoices"` with no error and a graceful acknowledgement. Asking to DELETE a captured page was refused with the agent naming exactly the two writable targets and pointing at the review tile's own control. Two deliberately invalid values came back as the handlers' own throws verbatim — the positional array (`scan_page_labels expects an object map keyed by item_id … A positional array silently mislabels every page after a reorder or insert`) and a slash in the title (`scan_title becomes the saved PDF's filename, so it cannot contain "/" or "\"`) — with **nothing staged** in either case (validate-then-apply confirmed by re-reading the inputs). Error Inspector: **zero captures on the clean load**; the probe load showed exactly 4, which is the correct number — two induced failures × (one `surface-writeback` contract break + its paired user-facing toast) — reported here rather than rounded to zero. `check:surface-drift` clean (140 surfaces, 3,410 values).
- **2026-08-11 — Knowledge System write targets LIVE-VERIFIED and documented (the implementation landed undocumented and unverified); plus a re-typed-literal fix.** `matrx-user/knowledge`'s 9 write targets were merged to main without an entry in this file's adopter list or Change Log, without a Change Log line in any of the three features that own its mounts, and — as far as could be established — without the skill's mandatory live-agent run. This entry closes that gap: the surface is now in the adopter list above, and everything below was confirmed against a real Badass Agent on a running dev server rather than read off the code. **All three mounts exercised.** *Extraction grid* (`ExtractionDatasetClient`): ONE message asking for a rename + a grid filter + a rewrite of an extracted DETAILS cell produced exactly one ask dialog (for the `entity` rename) carrying the manifest description verbatim; Apply persisted the new name through `updateJob` and the header re-rendered to it; `extraction_query` applied with NO dialog, correctly, because it is `mode:"ui"` / `applyPolicy:"auto"`, and the search box showed `chiropractic`; and the cell rewrite was refused, with the agent both naming the three declared targets AND volunteering the evidence argument the manifest intro makes ("Extracted rows are evidence pulled from your own source documents… i.e. falsifying the extraction"). A deliberately over-long name, with the agent explicitly told not to pre-validate, came back to it as the handler's own throw verbatim — `client_tool_error: extraction_dataset_name expects at most 120 characters (got 271).` — with nothing written. "Keep as is" left the name unchanged, returned no error, and the agent acknowledged the decline gracefully. *Graph canvas* (`KgGraphCanvas`): `graph_search` applied with no dialog (`auto`) and `graph_detail_level` raised the tier behind one ask dialog, confirming the surface's stated `auto`-vs-`ask` line holds where it costs a server round trip. *Suggestion queue* (`SuggestionsManager`): `suggestions_filter` produced its ask dialog with the REPLACE semantics for `statuses` spelled out in the prose the user actually reads, applied as `statuses:["pending"]`, and a same-message request to ACCEPT the first suggestion was refused — the agent named the queue's only two targets, noted that `focused_suggestion_id` "explicitly does not accept, reject, defer, or star anything", and added that "accepting a suggestion writes to your scopes, which is a decision I shouldn't make silently even if I had the capability", which is the manifest's doctrine reaching the model intact. **The finding worth carrying forward is an environment trap, not a code defect.** The first verification attempt reported the rename FAILING twice, which reads exactly like a broken entity handler. It is not: `page_extraction_jobs` is RLS-gated to owners, the seeded dataset used for the first run is owned by someone else, and `updateJob`'s `.update(...).select("*").single()` therefore comes back `PGRST116 — The result contains 0 rows`. The page's OWN inline rename fails identically on that dataset, which is the check that separates the two — **exercise a `mode:"entity"` target on a row the logged-in user owns, and confirm the human control fails too before filing the handler as broken.** The dataset renamed during verification was restored to its original name; nothing else was left changed. **One code change:** the 120-character cap was hand-typed twice — once in the handler's throw, once in the manifest's model-facing prose — so the number an agent is TOLD and the number that is ENFORCED could drift silently. Both now interpolate `EXTRACTION_JOB_NAME_MAX_LENGTH` from `features/page-extraction/data-review/constants.ts`. `check:surface-drift` (140 surfaces) + `type-check` clean. DB mirror (`ui.ui_surface_write_target`) still pending a manifest sync. Honest limit on the Error Inspector check: its panel could not be read programmatically in this harness, so "zero captures" is NOT claimed here — what can be said is that no `surface-writeback` capture text appeared anywhere in the document after a valid apply, and the two induced failures (over-long name, undeclared cell write) behaved loudly by design.
- **2026-08-11 — Voice Playground settings agent-writable; THE reference for a guard that must read CURRENT state.** `matrx-user/chat-voice` declares 3 ask-policy `mode:"draft"` targets over the ONLY authored state the surface has: `voice_instructions` (full replace), `append_voice_instructions` (the `agent-builder` replace/append pair applied to a VOICE prompt — a pronunciation rule or a brevity guardrail is exactly the kind of one-line addition that should not cost a 3,000-character round trip), and `voice_id`. Handlers live in `features/voice-agent/hooks/useVoicePlaygroundWriteHandlers.ts` and dispatch the SAME `updateConfig` action `InstructionsEditor`'s textarea `onChange` and `VoicePicker`'s `onValueChange` fire, so an agent rewrite and the user's own typing are indistinguishable. `voice_id` validates against the `VOICES` constant the picker renders its options from, and the manifest interpolates that same constant into the model-facing description, so the vocabulary the agent is told, the vocabulary the handler accepts, and the options on screen cannot drift. **Per-mount split:** handlers are registered by `PlaygroundSettingsSheet`, which renders only on `/chat/voice/playground`, so the `/chat/voice` intro route mounts the same provider and offers an agent no write tool at all. That split is load-bearing rather than cosmetic — `updateConfig` returns SILENTLY for a non-playground preset, an unknown instance id, and a falsy `voiceId`, so handlers on the intro route would report success for a write that never happened; every handler accordingly re-reads the store after dispatching and throws when the value did not land (the `war-room` optimistic-write finding, in its Redux-reducer form). **The generalizable finding is the guard.** Every handler refuses while a voice session is live, because mid-session the change either does nothing (the instructions already went out in `session.update`) or silently alters what the user is mid-conversation with. `applySurfaceWrite` resolves the handler BEFORE it shows the confirm dialog, so a guard reading a status captured in a render snapshot can act on a value that went stale while the dialog sat open — the `image-studio` trap. Reading `store.getState()` at call time is immune to that by construction, and it was verified head-on rather than argued: a dialog was opened with the page IDLE, a session was started WHILE that dialog was open, and Apply pressed afterwards was still refused with `A voice session is live right now (status: "requesting-mic")…` and the voice left unchanged. (That live state was produced by stalling the app's own `/api/voice-agent/token` route, so the real `start()` path parked the real slice in a real non-idle status with no realtime socket and no provider spend — the way to exercise a live-session guard without putting a conversation on the wire.) **The exclusions are most of the surface, and they are the point.** `connection_status` / `mic_muted` / `connection_error` / `session_started_at_ms` / `active_turn` are live session and DEVICE state — muting someone's microphone is not a copy edit — and starting or ending a session is not a target either. `transcript_text` / `transcript_turns` / `last_user_utterance` / `last_assistant_utterance` / `turn_count` / `total_interruptions` / `latency_summary` are the RECORD of what was actually said and how the session behaved; writing them would forge the transcript, the same input-vs-output line `voice-pad` drew around `transcript_entries` and `transcripts-cleanup` around `raw_transcript_text`. `conversation_id` / `voice_agent_id` are identity. `realtime_tools` / `realtime_tool_names` stay human-only because what the voice agent may REACH is a capability change, not a copy edit (the `agent-builder` and tool-registry line), and `session_preset` is derived from the route — "writing" it would mean navigating the user elsewhere. **`mode: "draft"` follows `image-generate` / `marketing-crawls` / `education-assessment`:** a settings form the user reviews and then executes with their own press. Nothing here persists to any record — the values live in `voiceAgentSlice` and are consumed when the user next taps the mic — so each description leads with "staged … for the NEXT session" and says nothing is persisted, which is the sentence the user actually reads first in the dialog. Live-verified with a real Badass Agent run on the playground, session idle: ONE message asking for two changes produced two per-target ask dialogs each carrying the manifest description verbatim; Apply landed both through the handlers and the REAL controls moved (voice picker Ara → Leo, instructions textarea 3,249 → 3,352 characters with a `## Brevity` section appended at the end and the original prompt byte-preserved), confirmed by re-opening the sheet and reading the DOM. "Keep as is" left the voice on Leo with no error and a graceful agent acknowledgement. A deliberately invented voice id came back to the agent as the handler's own throw verbatim (`voice_id must be exactly one of: ara, eve, leo, rex, sal. Received "morgan", which is not a voice this provider offers.`) with nothing staged (validate-then-apply). Asking to mute the mic and toggle the `web_search` realtime tool was refused with the agent naming exactly the three declared targets and citing the read-only rationale. Error Inspector on the clean load: **zero** `surface-writeback` captures (the one entry was a pre-existing `ai_v2_downgrade` backend-fallback warning); the captures on the probe run were the two deliberately induced ones, in their own browser session. `check:surface-drift` (140 surfaces, 3,410 values) + `type-check` clean — the 11 remaining type errors are pre-existing and all in `features/brokers/*`. DB mirror (`ui.ui_surface_write_target`) still pending a manifest sync. Also corrected the manifest's stale `readinessNote`, which listed the `registry.ts` entry and the `/chat/voice` route mapping as pending when both have been wired for some time.
- **2026-08-10 — War Room cockpit agent-writable (live-verified); THE reference for confirming an OPTIMISTIC write.** `matrx-user/war-room` declares 4 ask-policy `mode:"entity"` targets: `room_name` and `room_description` (authored copy, through `updateRoomIdentity` — the RoomIdentityButton's own thunk), `active_thread_title` (the same authored-label class one level down, through `renameThread` — what `useThreadActions.rename` dispatches), and `add_threads` (DECOMPOSITION, the `add_subtasks` shape from tasks, through `createThread` with the canvas anchor and appended `position` QuickAddThread uses). Handlers live in `features/war-room/components/room/useWarRoomWriteHandlers.ts` and register on the `SurfaceRuntimeProvider` `WarRoomShell` already mounted — only `getWriteHandlers` was missing. **Everything is `entity`, and the manifest says why:** the room has NO staging buffer — the identity popover and the thread-title input both persist on commit — so a `draft` target would have no editor state to stage into and no read twin that reflected it; the ask dialog is the review step a draft mode would otherwise provide. **The generalizable finding: a canonical write path is not automatically an honest one.** All three thunks hide their failures — `updateRoomIdentity` is OPTIMISTIC (it upserts into the store, then rolls back and reports if the server rejects), while `renameThread` and `createThread` swallow the error into a toast and return void/null. A bare `await dispatch(...)` therefore reports success for a write the database refused, which is the one failure mode that makes an agent confidently wrong. So `room_name`/`room_description` fail on `updateRoomIdentity`'s `false`, and the other two re-read the store afterwards (`threadsById[id].title`, the returned thread) and throw when the value did not land — the store is the only honest evidence. `active_thread_title` resolves the staged thread EXACTLY as `buildWarRoomRoomScope` resolves the `active_thread_id` it emitted (so the read twin and the written row are the same thread) and throws when nothing is staged, rather than writing into nothing. `add_threads` caps at 12 per call and stops at the first failure rather than reporting a partial batch as success. Deliberately NOT writable: `room_id`/`room_project_id`/`room_organization_id` (identity, ownership, the RLS basis), `room_resources` + assignments (association plumbing, not authored content), `is_recording` (a live capture), thread delete/park/pin/hide (destructive or the user's own filing), and `view_mode`/`density`/`projected_tab`. Live-verified with real Badass Agent runs on a throwaway room: ONE message asking for three changes produced three per-target ask dialogs each carrying the manifest description verbatim, all three applied through the handlers and persisted (`war_rooms.title`/`.description` and `threads.title` confirmed by SQL), the tool result read back `{ok:true, target:"room_name", mode:"entity"}`; `add_threads` created two threads at appended positions 1 and 2; "Keep as is" returned `{ok:false, declined:true}` with no error and a graceful agent acknowledgement; and two deliberately forced failures came back verbatim — the handler's own `add_threads expects a non-empty array of thread title strings.` for a bare `42`, and the seam's `No mounted surface declares write target "delete_thread".` for an undeclared destructive target. Error Inspector on the clean run: zero `surface-writeback` captures (the only entry was the pre-existing `request_context_changed` server-stream warning); the two captures on the negative run were the induced ones. `check:surface-drift` + `type-check` clean.
