DB mirror (`ui.ui_surface_write_target`) still pending a manifest sync.
- **2026-08-10 — Restored 16 Change Log entries and 20 adopter-list clauses a merge dropped (again).** Rebasing the scratchpad work onto `70a59394` conflicted in this file, and the incoming side had lost content that shipped: the Change Log went from 94 dated entries to 78, and the agent-writable adopter list lost every clause from `matrx-admin/agent-apps` through `matrx-user/workbooks` — `code-editor`, `mermaid-editor`, `markdown-studio`, `markdown-editor`, `quick-note-save`, `tool-registry`, `applications`, `database`, `scraper`, `task-create`, `transcripts`, `transcripts-cleanup`, `education-tutor`, `education-fastfire`, `education-grade-work`, `marketing-site-media`, `agent-apps` and more. The CODE for all of it is intact on main; only the docs were reverted, so this file was claiming surfaces are not agent-writable when they are. Both sides were reconciled by hand — main's new `war-room` / `chat-voice` adopters and Voice Playground / War Room entries kept, the dropped ones restored, nothing overwritten. **This is the second recorded occurrence** (see the 2026-08-09 Agent Builder restoration): this file is a campaign-wide hotspot, and resolving a conflict in it by taking one side wholesale silently reverts documentation for shipped code. Reconcile both sides, and check the dated-entry count before and after.
- **2026-08-10 — Scratchpad + Working Document agent-writable (live-verified); THE "read-only from outside, writable from inside" case, and the STALE-CLOSURE reference.** `matrx-user/scratchpad` and `matrx-user/working-document` each declare the SAME 4 `applyPolicy:"ask"` / `mode:"entity"` targets — `document_content` (whole-body replace), `append_document_content`, `replace_selection`, `insert_at_cursor` — exported once as `CONVERSATION_DOCUMENT_WRITE_TARGETS` from `manifests/_conversation-document.manifest.ts`, the same file that already shares their value set. One export, not two copies, because ONE editor (`WorkingDocumentEditor`) renders both surfaces and ONE handler block services them; two lists would drift from that single implementation, and a target whose handler drifted away is a loud runtime defect by design. Each manifest still carries its own docblock arguing why the same targets are right from its own side. **The judgment call is the scratchpad, and it is the reusable one:** its manifest says the cloud agent only READS it, which sounds like a refusal. It isn't. That restriction is about the agent out in the chat, and it is enforced where it lives — the scratchpad is published to a conversation as a read-only `user_scratchpad` context entry, and the chat's `ctx_patch` path cannot write it. It says nothing about an agent the user runs from INSIDE the scratchpad, which is the only agent these targets are ever offered to, on the user's explicit request, behind a per-target confirm — exactly the case the surface was split off to serve ("clean it up, bullet it, make a table"). Inside either document it is just text, so the writable parts are identical. **Like `html-page` and `mermaid-editor`, this surface mounted NO `SurfaceRuntimeProvider`** — it only emitted scope through `UnifiedAgentContextMenu` at trigger time — so the provider was added around `WorkingDocumentEditor`, reusing the existing `getApplicationScope` (built on `useWorkingDocumentSurfaceScope`) as `getScope` rather than writing a second builder. **`mode:"entity"` is a truth claim, on the `mermaid-editor` precedent:** this editor has no Save button; `useWorkingDocument.onChange` — the SAME function every keystroke goes through — writes the canonical slice immediately and its 700ms debounce commits the durable row, and the panel chrome literally reads "Auto-saved". `draft`'s "staged — review and save" would have been a lie in a dialog with nothing to save. The dialog now reads "This is saved immediately", verified live. Recovery is stated as **version history, never ⌘Z**: unlike Monaco or the mermaid reducer this editor mounts no undo stack, and a programmatic React value change does not enter the textarea's native undo history — so no description claims one, and the `history.row_versions` trigger that captures every commit is named instead. **The safety gate is `has_conflict`, and it is real rather than defensive:** `useWorkingDocument`'s commit returns early while a refused-save conflict is unresolved, so a write staged over it would appear in the editor, never persist, and enlarge the very merge the user is about to reconcile — every handler refuses loudly with that reason (they also refuse a view-only sharee, whose durable writes RLS silently drops). Deliberately NOT targets: `document_version` (the optimistic-concurrency token — writing it would forge the check the safety story rests on), `conversation_id` / `conversation_context` / `active_scope_ids` (the conversation is a REFERENCE, not this surface's content), the binding/identity fields (re-pointing a document at another durable row has its own picker and merge dialog), every derived value (`is_dirty`, counts, `current_heading` — an agent moves them by writing text, which IS the evidence loop), `document_title` (the doc auto-names itself from content via `useAutoLabel` while unnamed, and a non-empty title means a human chose it), and `editor_mode` / `cursor_offset` (the human's pane and caret; `cursor_offset` is READ to aim `insert_at_cursor`, never written). **The new general lesson is the STALE CLOSURE.** `applySurfaceWrite` resolves a handler BEFORE awaiting the confirm, so when an agent stages several targets in one turn every closure is captured up front; a handler reading `draft` off its render closure would splice into a snapshot taken before the previous target landed — on a document editor, that means replacing the WRONG range. The live body is therefore read through a ref advanced SYNCHRONOUSLY as each write applies, not merely on render. The matching **ordering wrinkle is declared, not papered over**: `replace_selection` and `insert_at_cursor` take their offsets from the live DOM, so when the textarea's value has diverged from that ref — an earlier same-turn whole-document write React has not re-rendered yet — they refuse and tell the agent to re-read `selection` / `cursor_offset` and issue the edit as its own step, rather than cutting at offsets into superseded text. `insert_at_cursor` pads to a blank line only at its two join points, because a document-wide newline collapse would silently rewrite spacing the user chose elsewhere. All four throws say **"plain text string, not JSON and not JSON-encoded"** in the message itself: the inline-tool layer parses a JSON-looking argument before the handler sees it, and an agent told only "expected a string" tends to "fix" that by double-encoding — escaped `\n` and stray quotes are extremely visible in a user's own notes. Live-verified with a real Badass Agent run on the global scratchpad panel: the ask dialog carried the manifest description verbatim plus "This is saved immediately", Apply restructured 279 chars of messy notes into headed sections in the visible editor, `replace_selection` on a highlighted line rewrote exactly that range with the rest of the document byte-identical, "Keep as is" left the body untouched and the agent reported the decline without an error, an `document_version` request produced NO tool call at all (the agent enumerated exactly the four real targets and explained the token is read-only), and a forced empty `document_content` returned the handler's own throw verbatim as `client_tool_error: document_content expects non-empty text … Emptying the document is a human gesture, not an agent one.` with no double-encode retry. Error Inspector on the clean path: zero `surface-writeback` captures (one unrelated `request_context_changed` stream warning). `check:surface-drift` + `type-check` clean. DB mirror sync still pending. **Getting in matters, same as `mermaid-editor`:** these surfaces have no `urlPattern`, and `SurfaceAgentsPanelImpl` prefers the ROUTE surface whenever it disagrees with the mounted runtime — so on `/chat` the popover offers chat agents and these targets are invisible. The panel's own runtime wins on routes with no route→surface mapping (`/artifacts` was used here), which is where the header popover shows "Scratchpad".
- **2026-08-10 — The live chat route agent-writable (live-verified) — THE record-vs-draft reference.** `matrx-user/chat` declares 2 ask-policy targets: `input_draft` (`mode:"draft"`) taking `{text, mode?: "replace" | "append"}` and landing through the SAME `setUserInputText` action `AgentTextarea` and `NewChatLandingInput` dispatch on every keystroke — so an agent-staged draft joins the undo stack, the sacred draft-protection rules and the send flow exactly like typed text — and `conversation_title` (`mode:"entity"`) through the canonical `renameConversation` thunk that every conversation list already uses, never a raw supabase update. Handlers sit on `ChatRoomClient`'s own provider, the surface's ONLY `SurfaceRuntimeProvider`, which backs all three routes (`/chat/new` through `ChatNewClient`'s `landingContent`, `/chat/a/[agentId]`, `/chat/[conversationId]`); the other three files referencing this surface name (`RunSettingsEditor`, `RunControlsTabPanel`, `NewChatLandingInput`) turned out to be v3 context-menu wiring that mounts no runtime, so there was no second mount to give a posture to. **The judgment call this surface exists to record: chat is a RECORD wrapped around ONE authored field.** The composer holds the message the user has not sent yet — the textbook "authored content an agent drafts better/faster" case, and fully reversible because the user still presses send. The transcript is the opposite: `all_messages`, `last_user_message`, `current_message_text` and `full_conversation_text` are what was actually said by whom, and an agent rewriting them does not edit a draft, it fabricates history. The route HAS a user-driven "Edit & resubmit" path; it stays a human affordance precisely because the record's value is that a human wrote it. **SENDING is not a target** either: the send is the outward-facing act that spends a turn, bills a model and — through the three inbox modes — can steer or interrupt a run already in flight, so the human press stays the gate exactly as it does for `podcast-studio` and `image-generate`. Also undeclared: the run configuration (`added_tools` / `added_skills` / `model` / `sandbox_binding`), on `agent-builder`'s reasoning that changing what an agent may REACH is a capability change, not a copy edit; `attached_resources`, whose read half publishes lean refs to what is ALREADY attached and never the user's library, so an agent could only guess UUIDs (the `education-fastfire` set-picker line); switching the bound agent, since `initial_agent_id` is read-only provenance by this feature's own model; and `working_document` / `scratchpad`, lean refs whose bodies belong to their own surfaces. `variable_values` was considered and left out: its per-variable shapes come from a snapshot of custom components and scope bindings, chat agents rarely declare any, and a target whose happy path could not be exercised live is not one to ship. **The one non-obvious guard** is on the title: a fresh chat holds a CLIENT-MINTED conversation id and has no `chat.conversation` row until its first turn commits, so an UPDATE would match zero rows without erroring while every optimistic mirror cheerfully showed the new name — the handler gates on the same `waitForConversationPersisted` predicate URL promotion uses (`timeoutMs: 0`, i.e. a single probe rather than that path's 3-minute poll) and refuses out loud. New exports on the manifest — `CHAT_DRAFT_WRITE_MODES` / `isChatDraftWriteMode` / `CHAT_INPUT_DRAFT_MAX` / `CHAT_CONVERSATION_TITLE_MAX` — are the ONE vocabulary: the manifest interpolates them into the descriptions an agent reads and the handlers validate against the same constants, so the advertised contract and the enforced contract cannot drift. Live-verified with real Badass Agent runs on `/chat/[id]` and `/chat/new`: one message drove both targets, each raising its own ask dialog carrying the manifest description verbatim; Apply renamed the conversation in the history sidebar and appended into the composer, and **the specific hazard of this surface was checked explicitly — the staged text landed in the CONVERSATION's composer (`textarea[0]`, "Follow-up question:\nHow many people live in Paris?") while the agent-run message box (`textarea[1]`, same placeholder) stayed empty**; on `/chat/new` the draft landed in the hero "Ask anything" composer, proving the `landingContent` mount is covered by the same target. "Keep as is" returned `{ok:false, declined:true}` with the title unchanged and the agent acknowledging the decline without retrying; asking it to rewrite the last user message AND to send the composer produced `No mounted surface declares write target "last_user_message"` / `"send_message"`, with the agent explaining unprompted that sending stays a human action; a forced `input_draft` of a bare string came back as the handler's own throw verbatim — `input_draft expects an object: { "text": string, "mode"?: "replace" | "append" }.` — with the composer byte-identical afterward (no coercion); and on a brand-new chat the title call returned `conversation_title refused — this conversation has not been saved yet, so there is no row to rename. Its row is created when the first turn completes.` Error Inspector: the successful runs produced ZERO `surface-writeback` captures (only the known pre-existing `request_context_changed` stream warnings); every capture seen came from a deliberate contract break above, which is the seam working. `check:surface-drift` + `type-check` clean. DB mirror sync still pending.
- **2026-08-10 — Agent Apps ADMIN console agent-writable (live-verified) — the first admin surface, and the clean `mode:"draft"` reference.** `matrx-admin/agent-apps` declares 3 ask-policy draft targets, all on the CATEGORIES editor: `category_name`, `category_description`, `category_icon`. Handlers live on the categories page's existing `SurfaceRuntimeProvider` (`getWriteHandlers`) and go through the SAME `handleEditChange(field, value)` setter the admin's own typing calls, so a staged value and a typed one are indistinguishable, the "Unsaved Changes" badge and Discard button arm identically, and `handleSave` → `updateAgentAppCategory` (the canonical service) remains the ONLY path to the database — no raw supabase anywhere in the write path. **Why this surface earns the draft label the campaign has mostly had to settle `entity` for:** `category_has_unsaved_changes` is emitted from the very `hasUnsaved` flag the handlers flip. `selected_category` was NOT, though — it was emitted from the SAVED row while the pane rendered `editData`, so it disagreed with the form the moment anything was typed (a pre-existing read lie, human typing included). It now emits from the live `editData` buffer, with the value description saying so and naming `category_has_unsaved_changes` as the divergence flag against `categories_list`. That makes the evidence loop real: an agent reads back exactly what it staged, before anyone saves. **Judgment on an admin console:** almost nothing here earns a target. `is_featured` / `is_verified` / publish status, rate limits and unblocks, error resolution, executions and analytics are trust and abuse controls, not copy — all left undeclared, and the intro now says so explicitly so an agent proposes them in words. Even inside the category pane, `id` (identity, and the input is disabled), `sort_order` (mechanical ordering the admin does with the list's own arrows), creation and deletion (deleting orphans every app assigned to the category) stay human. Name/description/icon are three targets rather than one object on purpose: renaming re-labels the category everywhere it is offered to users while a description or icon edit does not, so each gets its own confirm and can be declined independently. `category_icon` validates against the real registry via `isRegisteredOrLucideIconName` — an invented icon name is rejected rather than saved as a broken glyph. Live-verified with a real Badass Agent run on a throwaway category: one message → an ask dialog per target carrying the manifest description verbatim, Apply staged into the real inputs with Discard/Save armed, the ADMIN's Save then persisted to `platform.categories` (name, description and `PenTool` icon confirmed by SQL), "Keep as is" returned `{ok:false, declined:true}` with `is_error:false` and the agent acknowledged the decline without retrying, a deliberately invalid `category_icon: "NotARealIconName123"` came back as the handler's own throw verbatim with nothing staged, and a request to feature/verify apps plus set sort order produced NO `apply_surface_write` call at all — the agent explained the tool exposes only the three category fields. Error Inspector: zero `surface-writeback` captures (only the known pre-existing `request_context_changed` stream warnings). `check:surface-drift` + `type-check` clean. DB mirror sync still pending. **Found in passing, NOT fixed here (out of scope):** `createAgentAppCategory` never sets `created_by`, so `platform.categories`' `std_insert` RLS check (`created_by = auth.uid()`) rejects every category the console tries to create — the Add dialog 403s for any admin. The throwaway used for verification had to be seeded out-of-band; the update path this change rides on is unaffected.
- **2026-08-10 — Applications hub agent-writable (live-verified) — and `matrx-admin/ai-models` RULED OUT on the judgment bar.** Two results, because the negative one saves the next agent the trip. **`matrx-admin/ai-models` does not earn write targets and should not be re-assigned.** Its detail panel's complete editable set is `AiModelFormData` — `name` (the wire identifier), `common_name`, `provider_id`, `context_window`, `max_tokens`, `is_deprecated` / `is_primary` / `is_premium` (the manifest itself notes deprecation IS the activation signal), `cost_rating` / `speed_rating` (these render the user-facing $-tier and speed dots in every model picker), and the four fallback/retry routing fields — plus `capabilities` (JSON Fields), Controls and Constraints (`ai.api.rules` ⊕ `ai.offering.override`), and a Raw JSON tab that is just all of the above at once. Every one is vendor identity, capability governance, commercially load-bearing tiering, or request routing — the exact line the `agent-builder` adopter drew. That is **zero** YES fields, not merely fewer than two. The one plausible authoring field, `description`, is a real `ai.model_definition` column that the manifest emits and even advertises as "the most common agent job here" — but it has **no editor anywhere in the panel**, and it is absent from `AI_MODEL_COLUMNS`, the whitelist the Raw JSON tab strips against, so the only generic write path would silently drop it. Making that surface writable is a `surface-authoring` job (build the editor first), not this skill's. Of the three named fallbacks, `matrx-admin/tool-registry` (one `is_active` switch on `ToolViewPage`) and `matrx-user/crm-manager` (an inheriting overlay with `values: []` over a list + scope filter) fail the same bar. **`matrx-admin/applications` passes and is now live**: ONE `mode:"draft"` / `applyPolicy:"ask"` target, `app_notice`, staging the operator broadcast (`AppConfigV1.notice`) every installed client shows once. Value is an object REPLACING the whole notice — `{level, title, body, url?}`, with `level` validated against the canonical `NOTICE_LEVELS` imported from `features/admin/applications/config/schema.ts` (the same const the editor's Select renders from and the manifest description interpolates, so the three cannot drift), `title`/`body` required and non-empty because a broadcast is authored whole, `url` optional and checked through the schema's own `httpsUrlSchema`. Validation lives in a pure core (`config/notice-write-targets.ts`, 16 unit tests) kept OUT of the React updater so the throw lands synchronously in the writeback seam; the handler is registered from `AppConfigEditor` via `useSurfaceWriteHandlers` because that component owns the draft, and it preserves the forward-compat `extras` keys the schema promises to round-trip. New read twin `config_editor_notice` is emitted by `AppConfigClient` from the SAVED row, not the unsaved draft — an agent asking what to replace wants what is live in the fleet. **The generalizable lesson is the per-mount NO**: this hub governs shipped CLIENTS, so the three server URLs and `min_supported_app_version` (they change what every installed client talks to, or force upgrades), `flags`, `credential_maintenance`, the app slug and schema version, catalog artifact pinning, and the read-only Installations and History tabs all get no target — a surface can be worth exactly one target and still be worth doing. Live-verified with a real Badass Agent run: the ask dialog carried the manifest description verbatim, Apply staged all four fields into the real editor (the notice inputs did not exist before the write — the notice was disabled — and read back off the DOM afterward with the agent's own copy), a same-message request to set `min_supported_app_version` was refused with the agent explaining `app_notice` is the only target exposed, "Keep as is" left the draft untouched and the agent acknowledged the decline without an error, and a forced `{"level":"urgent"}` came back to the agent verbatim as `app_notice: level must be one of info | warning | critical. Received "urgent".` Error Inspector: zero `surface-writeback` captures on the valid paths (the deliberate invalid call produced exactly one, which is the designed loud behavior). Nothing was saved — the run staged into an existing row's draft only, no throwaway records. `check:surface-drift` + `type-check` + `jest` clean. DB mirror sync still pending.
- **2026-08-10 — Database admin console agent-writable; THE reference for the drafting/execution line on a surface where execution is the danger.** `matrx-admin/database` declares ONE `mode:"draft"` / `applyPolicy:"ask"` target, `sql_query` — a full replace of the workbench editor buffer. Handler on `app/(admin)/administration/database/components/enhanced-sql-editor.tsx` (which already mounted the provider; only `getWriteHandlers` was missing) calls the SAME `setSqlQuery` the textarea's `onChange`, the template cards, and the history rows call, so staged SQL and typed SQL are indistinguishable. **Why this is safe on a database console, stated plainly because the instinct is to refuse outright:** the target replaces TEXT IN A TEXTAREA. Nothing connects, nothing runs, nothing is invalidated. That is why staging DDL/DML is explicitly allowed and the target description says so out loud — `DROP TABLE …` sitting in the editor is inert characters the admin reads and decides on, identical in risk to the same characters in a chat reply. The risk of this surface is EXECUTION, and execution has no write path: **Execute, Cancel, `use_cache` and Clear Cache are deliberately not targets** (cache behaviour decides whether the next run touches the live database at all — an agent flipping it changes what executing MEANS without changing anything visible in the SQL), and neither are the result/history/timing values, which are the RECORD of what the database actually did. The handler also refuses while `loading`, so results that land always belong to the SQL the admin is looking at. **`replacement_pairs` considered and REJECTED on the judgment bar, not on risk:** the Template Variables pairs do nothing until the admin presses "Apply Replacements to Query", which rewrites `sql_query` — so an agent writing pairs stages a stage, a strictly worse path to what `sql_query` already does in one visible step, and it has no read twin to declare `updatesValue` against. **Per-mount split:** the `DatabaseHubLanding` mount at `/administration/database` registers NO handlers on purpose — a static link grid over a build-time config module, with no editable state an agent could author. Validation lives in a pure core (`features/administration/lib/sql-editor-write-targets.ts`, 16 unit tests) so the throw lands synchronously in `applySurfaceWrite`'s try/catch rather than inside a React updater; it rejects non-strings, markdown-fenced values, empty/whitespace, and anything over `SQL_QUERY_WRITE_MAX_CHARS` (which the manifest description interpolates rather than re-typing). Also corrected a stale manifest doc comment that pointed the workbench at `/administration/database/database-admin` — the emitter is at `/administration/database/sql-queries`; the `database-admin` dashboard has its own editor and mounts no provider. Live-verified with a real Badass Agent run: the ask dialog carried the manifest description verbatim, Apply staged a genuine 11-line `pg_total_relation_size` query into the visible textarea with **nothing executed** (no results panel, no history row), "turn off Use Cache" and "actually RUN it" were both refused with the agent explaining that `sql_query` is the only target it has, "Keep as is" returned `{ok:false, declined:true}` and left a hand-typed query byte-identical, and three forced-invalid calls came back as the handler's own throws quoted verbatim (`sql_query must be a string containing the SQL to stage, got number.`; `sql_query must be raw SQL, not a markdown code block. …`; `No mounted surface declares write target "use_cache".`). The only `surface-writeback` captures in the Error Inspector were those three deliberate contract breaks — the successful Apply produced none. `check:surface-drift` + `type-check` clean. DB mirror sync still pending.

Handlers live in `ToolViewPage.tsx`'s `getWriteHandlers` and go through the new `features/tool-call-visualization/admin/mcp-tools/tool-definition.service.ts` → `PUT /api/admin/tools/[id]`, which is `requireAdmin()`-gated and writes with the service-role client because `tool.definition` is SELECT-only under RLS — there is no direct-write shortcut to be tempted by, and the page's own Active switch was refactored onto the same wrapper so there is ONE door. **Why `entity` and not the preferred `draft`:** the detail route is server-rendered read-only props with no editor state to stage into (editing has its own route, `/[toolId]/edit`, which does not mount this surface), so inventing a shadow draft would have meant a staged value no Save bar knew about; each handler validates, writes, then `router.refresh()`es so the read twin the agent sees next is what actually landed. **Per-mount split:** `McpToolsManager` mounts the same surface for the catalogue and registers NONE — its only local state is browse state (search / category / source / status / tag filters, column filters, sort), it holds no open record for a confirm dialog to name, and the detail page already owns the canonical door. **Deliberately human-only, and this is the point of an admin adopter:** `tool_is_active`, `tool_admin_only`, `tool_gating`, `tool_exemptions`, `tool_visibility`, `tool_tier` — changing what a tool may REACH or who may reach it is a capability change, not a copy edit (the `agent-builder` call, and it bites harder here: one flipped flag re-arms a tool for every agent on the platform); plus `tool_name` (the dispatch key — there is no slug column, so a rename silently breaks every call site), the parameter/output schemas and annotations (a wrong schema breaks every call of the tool; annotations are the destructive/read-only hints other agents trust), `tool_version`/`tool_semver`, and everything `mcp_server`-adjacent — the neighbourhood the manifest's existing SECURITY note keeps this surface out of. `tool_group` is excluded for a subtler reason worth reusing: it is a fair filing label, but NO human editor exposes it (absent from both `ToolCreatePage` and `ToolEditPage`), so an entity write there is a one-way door the admin cannot correct in the UI. New `admin/mcp-tools/tool-metadata.ts` is the canonical bounds module — `TOOL_DESCRIPTION_MAX_CHARS` / `TOOL_CATEGORY_MAX_CHARS` / `TOOL_TAG_MAX_CHARS` / `TOOL_TAGS_MAX_COUNT` plus throwing validators — interpolated into the manifest descriptions AND called by the handlers, so the contract the model reads is the contract enforced. The tag validator rejects an embedded comma because the admin's tag editor is one comma-separated input: a comma inside a tag would split it the next time a human edits the tool. Live-verified with a real Badass Agent run on `describe_demo` (values restored afterwards): one message → three ask dialogs each carrying the manifest description verbatim, Apply persisted the rewritten description and `["demos","replay","qa"]`, "Keep as is" on the category returned a clean decline the agent reported as declined with the category unchanged, a "deactivate this tool / set visibility public" request produced NO tool call at all (the agent enumerated the three targets it had and explained the refusal), and two forced-invalid calls came back as the handlers' own throws verbatim — `tool_tags entry "replay, qa" contains a comma; …` and `tool_category is 96 characters; the maximum is 60.` — with nothing written. Error Inspector on the run's page load: exactly ONE `surface-writeback` capture, the deliberate over-length break, and no others. `check:surface-drift` + `type-check` clean. DB mirror sync pending.
- **2026-08-10 — Agent Apps: the ENTITY targets now work on every sub-route (additive follow-up to the entry below).** Two agents reached this surface at once; the first landed the 5 targets, and this pass keeps that work verbatim and adds only the mount it did not wire. `app_category` and `app_tags` are `mode:"entity"` — they persist through `saveAppField` and need only the open row, not the Settings tab's pickers — so `AgentAppSurfaceRuntime` (the `/agent-apps/[id]` layout, mounted on EVERY sub-route) now registers them too, from shared validators in `features/agent-apps/route/agent-app-entity-writes.ts` that both mounts import so the contract cannot drift between them. **The generalizable rule this settles:** on a multi-mount surface, a DRAFT target's reach is bounded by where its input is mounted (a draft with nowhere to land is a write that goes nowhere), but an ENTITY target's reach is bounded only by where the row is available — scoping one to the tab that happens to render its picker is an artifact of the DOM, not a safety property. - **2026-08-10 — Code editor agent-writable (live-verified); the undo stack IS the safety net.** `matrx-user/code-editor` declares 3 `mode:"draft"` / `applyPolicy:"ask"` targets over the Monaco buffer: `replace_selection`, `insert_at_cursor`, `current_file_content`. Handlers live on the provider `features/code/agent-context/CodeWorkspaceContextMenu.tsx` already mounted and reuse its existing `handleTextReplace` / `handleTextInsertAfter` / `handleTextInsertBefore` — the very `executeEdits` paths the right-click AI actions use — so an agent edit lands on Monaco's undo stack exactly like a paste. **That is the generalizable bit for editor surfaces:** `draft` normally means "staged in React state until Save", but on a Monaco surface the buffer IS the draft, and the honest equivalent of reversibility is the editor's own undo history. So `current_file_content` performs ONE `executeEdits` over the full model range rather than `model.setValue()`, which would silently throw the user's undo history away — the difference between an agent rewrite you can ⌘Z and one you cannot. The full range is derived from `getLineCount()` / `getLineContent()`, which the repo's narrowed `MonacoModel` type already declares, instead of widening that shared type for one call. `replace_selection` REFUSES (throws) when nothing is selected rather than inventing a range — the target that touches least is the one that must be most certain about where it lands. Deliberately NOT writable: saving to disk, file create/rename/delete, tab lifecycle, filesystem/workspace switching, and the diagnostics (a report, not an input); persisting stays the human gesture the whole safety story rests on. Live-verified with real Badass Agent runs on `/code`: one message asking for all three produced three ask dialogs, each quoting the manifest description verbatim, all applied into the live buffer; "Keep as is" left the file byte-identical and the agent reported the decline without retrying; a save/rename/close/switch-filesystem request was refused with the agent enumerating the three real targets and correctly quoting "nothing is written to disk … the user still saves"; and calling `replace_selection` with no selection returned the handler's own throw verbatim. Alongside it the surface finally emits its LIVE state: the core publishes a `task_draft` snapshot into a ref the window's `getScope` reads, so the write target has a real read twin (`updatesValue`) instead of only the opener's prefill — closing the manifest's standing "not yet threaded up" gap. Both halves are gated on the `surfaceDraftRef` prop the WINDOW passes, so the `/tasks/new` mount of the same core never services the surface even when the window opens on top of it — the per-mount posture of schedules/education-assessment, expressed through a prop rather than two providers. **What an overlay surface means for writes, verified not assumed:** when the window closes nothing registers the surface, so `listAgentWritableTargets()` stops offering the tool and a call made anyway returns the loud `No mounted surface declares write target "task_draft"` with an Error Inspector capture — never silence. Deliberately NOT writable: project and scopes (the surface emits the selected ids but no pick lists, so an agent has no way to name a legal value, and a target it cannot supply a legal value for is worse than none), the source link / `linkScope`, and Create task itself. - **2026-08-10 — Transcripts viewer agent-writable (live-verified); THE reference for picking `draft` vs `entity` by whether the read twin can SEE the buffer.** `matrx-user/transcripts` declares 4 ask-policy targets on `TranscriptViewer.tsx`'s existing provider (it mounted `getScope` but no `getWriteHandlers`; the surface renders on `/transcripts/processor`, not on the `/transcripts` list table). `transcript_title`, `transcript_description` and `transcript_speaker_label` are `mode:"entity"` through the canonical `updateTranscript` thunk → `transcriptsService` — the same path the header's Edit → Save and the per-segment edit dialog use, never raw supabase; `transcript_body` is `mode:"draft"` into the inline editor's own `setEditContent`, opening the editor if closed so the staged text lands under the existing Save/Cancel bar. **The mode split is the generalizable finding.** This surface has TWO staging buffers and only one is observable: `getEditorApplicationScope` overrides the `content` value with the LIVE textarea while `isEditingContent` is true, so a staged body is real evidence for both the user and the next agent read — draft is honest there. Title and description read from the stored `activeTranscript` and never from `editTitle`/`editDescription`, which only render while `isEditingMetadata` is true, so a draft there would stage a value the read twin cannot report; those persist on Apply instead. Copying "draft is preferred" without checking the read twin would have produced two invisible writes. Confirmed for this surface and worth knowing campaign-wide: `isEditable` on `SurfaceRuntimeProvider` does NOT gate writes — it is carried onto the registry entry only, and `surface-writeback.ts` never reads it, so `isEditable={false}` mounts are still writable. Deliberately NOT writable: `tags` and `folder_name` (the service accepts both, but this viewer renders tags read-only and has no folder control — a target there would set a value the user can neither see staged nor correct in place, a write with no user twin; revisit if a tag editor lands), identity/provenance (`source_type`, audio/video paths, `is_draft`), deletion and Promote-to-Studio, and playback transport (mechanical). Live-verified with a real Badass Agent run on a throwaway transcript: one message drove both entity targets, each confirm dialog carried the manifest description verbatim, both persisted (verified by SQL, not just the DOM); the body draft staged a cleaned four-paragraph transcript with Save armed while `segments` in the DB stayed byte-identical; `transcript_speaker_label` renamed "Speaker 1" → "Dana Whitfield" in both its segments leaving the other speaker and all segment text untouched; "Keep as is" declined with no error and left the title unchanged; a forced-invalid call returned the handler's own throw verbatim (`transcript_speaker_label: no speaker "Speaker 9 Does Not Exist" in this transcript. Existing labels: "Dana Whitfield", "Speaker 2".`); and an undeclared ask (tags + folder) produced NO `apply_surface_write` call, the agent enumerating exactly the four targets it had. Error Inspector on a clean valid-write session: zero `surface-writeback` captures (only the pre-existing `request_context_changed` noise). `check:surface-drift` + `type-check` clean. **One thing the campaign should know:** in that undeclared-target test the tags and folder DID change in the database — Badass Agent reached the row with one of its OWN tools, outside the surface seam and outside the ask gate. The surface refused correctly; what a surface declines is not the same as what a globally-bound agent can reach, which matches the `image-generate` note about its own image tool. Not yet mirrored to `ui.ui_surface_write_target`.
- **2026-08-10 — Transcription cleanup pad agent-writable — and given its FIRST `SurfaceRuntimeProvider` (live-verified).** `matrx-user/transcripts-cleanup` declares 3 `applyPolicy:"ask"` targets: `cleaned_transcript_text` and `custom_output_text` as `mode:"draft"`, plus `session_title` as `mode:"entity"`. **The surface had no runtime emitter at all.** `CleanupPad` published scope only through `EditableContextMenu`/`ProTextarea` `getApplicationScope` — right-click launches worked, but nothing ever called `registerSurfaceRuntime`, so `getSurfaceRuntimeStack()` was empty on this route and a header-launched ("Agents for this page") run saw no live surface and could never have been offered `apply_surface_write`. The page mount now wraps its return in `<SurfaceRuntimeProvider>` reusing the EXISTING `buildScope` builder, so a header run and a context-menu run emit one identical payload. **Per-mount split** (the schedules/education pattern): only `variant="page"` registers. The `variant="embedded"` pads — War Room thread Audio tabs — register nothing, because a room renders tiles and several embedded pads can be mounted at once over DIFFERENT sessions, so deepest-wins resolution would land an agent write in whichever tile mounted last with no way for the user to tell which transcript it hit; the war room is also its own surface with its own bespoke HITL tools. - **2026-08-10 — Workbooks agent-writable; THE reference for riding a third-party editor's own command stream.** `matrx-user/workbooks` declares 3 ask-policy targets, all on the EDITOR route. `workbook_name` and `workbook_description` are `mode:"entity"` through the canonical `workbook-service` setters — `renameWorkbook` (already there) and a new `updateWorkbookDescription` sibling, so neither handler hand-rolls a `.from("udt_workbooks")` write; `commitRename` was refactored into one `applyRename(name)` that the header field's blur/Enter commit AND the write target both call, so an agent rename is indistinguishable from the user typing it. No parallel snapshot write was added, and none was needed; when a page embeds a third-party editor, the editor's command bus IS the canonical path. Its value is a PARTIAL map keyed by sheet id (`{"sheet-01": "Q3 Revenue"}`), fully validated before the FIRST `setName` — real ids, 1-31 chars, none of `: \ / ? * [ ]`, and uniqueness checked against the RESULT of applying the whole map so swapping two names is legal — because a rename map that half-applies then throws leaves a workbook nobody asked for. **Per-mount split:** the library route (`app/(core)/workbooks/page.tsx`) registers NOTHING on purpose and says so in code — it is a roster of N workbooks, a write target carries one value with no entity selector, and its only mutations are create, import (needs a `File` an agent cannot supply) and delete. **Deliberately NOT writable:** `workbook_snapshot` — bulk-replacing every cell of a user's spreadsheet is destructive, not authoring, and there is no canonical "overwrite the grid" gesture to route it through; also permissions/`is_public`, `workbook_id`/`workbook_source`, and every structural or destructive sheet edit. Live-verified with real Badass Agent runs: one message changed name + description + sheet name, each with its own ask dialog carrying the manifest prose verbatim; Apply landed all three and the sheet rename survived a full page reload (proving it rode the autosave into a persisted snapshot); "Keep as is" left the name untouched and the agent reported it as declined, not an error; "make this workbook public" produced no `apply_surface_write` call at all, the agent answering that only three targets are offered; and two forced-invalid calls came back verbatim as `client_tool_error: workbook_sheet_names: "Budget[2026]" contains a character sheet names cannot use (: \ / ? * [ ]).` with nothing renamed. Error Inspector: zero `surface-writeback` captures on the happy path (the only capture was the deliberate invalid call, which is the seam working as designed). `check:surface-drift` + `type-check` clean. The HTML target is the strongest case on the surface for the opposite of the usual reason: the body is Monaco, which swallows `contextmenu` before the v3 menu sees it (`features/html-pages/README.md` → "Known limitation"), so there is NO user-driven text-replace seam on the document — the declared target is the only path. `page_seo_metadata` rejects unknown keys by name, so an agent reaching for `is_indexable` or `canonical_url` through the object gets a loud refusal; `page_html_content` refuses fragments, code-fenced values, and anything without `<html>`/`<body>`, because a fragment would publish a broken page at `/p/{id}`. Zero `surface-writeback` captures. `check:surface-drift` + `type-check` clean.
- **2026-08-10 — Voice Pad agent-writable; THE narrow-but-earned adoption, and a second OBSERVED-EVIDENCE line.** `matrx-user/voice-pad` declares 2 ask-policy `mode:"draft"` targets over the pad's ONE authored field: `draft_text` (full replace) and `append_draft_text` (add to the end), the `agent-builder` replace/append pair applied to raw dictation. Handlers are on `VoicePad.tsx`'s existing nested provider and dispatch the same `setDraftText` the textarea fires on every keystroke. **This surface sits ON the judgment bar and is worth reading as a calibration case.** The mount owns exactly one editable buffer, which is normally below the ~2-YES-field floor; it earns targets anyway because cleanup IS the pad's purpose — dictation arrives with filler, no punctuation and homophone errors, and "tidy up what I just said" is the highest-value agent action on the whole surface. The append half is what makes the pair real rather than a token second target: dictation is incremental, so adding a closing thought must not require round-tripping the whole buffer, and appending when no draft exists yet carries the joined entries in first so nothing dictated is dropped. **The exclusion is the other half of the point:** `transcript_entries` and `live_transcript` are what the microphone actually heard, and an agent that could rewrite the raw transcript could launder invention into something the user reads back as a faithful record — the same line `scraper` drew around scraped content and `transcripts-cleanup` drew around `raw_transcript_text`. Keeping the entries untouched beside the rewrite is what makes the rewrite reviewable at all. `draft_text` refuses an empty string (clearing the pad is a human gesture, not a write-shaped delete); both refuse while the mic is live, because a completed dictation would land behind a draft the user can no longer see it in. Recording is a DEVICE action; clearing, removing entries and sending the pad onward stay human. Single mount by design — `VoicePadAdvanced` / `VoicePadEmbed` share `voicePadSlice` but mount no runtime, so they emit no read values and offer no tool. **Two campaign-useful findings from verifying this one.** (1) The pad hides its textarea until it has content, so with no microphone the only way to stage a "before" state is the component's own `onChange`; a write target is in fact the only other way text can enter an empty pad. (2) `SurfaceAgentsPanelImpl` prefers the ROUTE surface over a deeper floating runtime when the two disagree, so on a mapped route (`/dashboard`) the popover offers Dashboard's agents and the run carries Dashboard's scope — while `buildToolInjection` reads `listAgentWritableTargets()` off the live stack and still offers the PAD's targets. The result is a real split: tool present, page evidence absent, and the agent correctly refuses to invent the dictation it cannot see. Verify a floating-panel surface from a route with no mapping (`/reports` works) or you will misread this as a broken target. Live-verified with a real Badass Agent run: per-target ask dialogs carrying the manifest prose verbatim, Apply landing a genuinely cleaned paragraph and then appending "Next steps:" + three bullets without disturbing it, "Keep as is" leaving the pad byte-identical with a graceful acknowledgement and no error, a request to rewrite `transcript_entries` refused with the agent naming the only two writable targets and citing the read-only rationale from the manifest, and a deliberately empty value returning the handler's own throw verbatim (`append_draft_text expects a non-empty string. Clearing the pad is the user's own action, not a write.`). Zero `surface-writeback` captures on a clean load; `check:surface-drift` + `type-check` clean.
- **2026-08-10 — Image Generate agent-writable; THE reference for a composite draft target, and for refusing the expensive button.** `matrx-user/image-generate` (`/images/generate`) declares ONE ask-policy `mode:"draft"` target, `generation_request` — a partial `{prompt?, style?, image_size?, image_count?}` object. Handler lives in `app/(core)/images/generate/GenerateShellClient.tsx` (which already mounted the provider; only `getWriteHandlers` was missing) and calls the same `setPrompt` / `setStyle` / `setSize` / `setCount` the textarea, the input and the Selects call, so a staged request and a typed one are indistinguishable. **Why ONE target and not four:** these fields are one request the user composes in a single thought, `generation_request_summary` is literally their composite read twin, and every key being optional preserves per-field granularity (`{image_size}` alone leaves a typed prompt alone) while costing one confirm dialog instead of four. The trade is stated in the manifest: the user accepts or declines the object whole. **Generate is deliberately NOT a target** (the `podcast-studio` precedent — a run spends real money on image models, the human press is the gate); results, `is_generating`/`generation_enabled`, and anything about saved files/folders stay undeclared, and the handler THROWS rather than editing a request already in flight. The size vocabulary moved out of a re-typed union into `features/image-studio/constants/generation-options.ts`, which the Selects render from, the handler validates against, the manifest's contract prose interpolates, and `GenerateImageBody` now types — five copies collapsed to one. Live-verified with a real Badass Agent run: the ask dialog carried the manifest's own prose with the enum spelled out from the constant, Apply staged a genuinely good 900-character prompt + Landscape + "editorial illustration" into the visible form with Generate still enabled and unpressed and the results panel still empty, "Keep as is" returned `{ok:false, declined:true}` (the agent acknowledged and offered alternatives, no error) and left the form byte-identical, and "start the generation run" produced NO `apply_surface_write` call at all — the tool advertises only `generation_request`. Worth knowing for the campaign: Badass Agent answered that last one with its OWN image tool and volunteered that the result had not gone through the form's Generate — the surface seam held, but a globally-bound agent's own capabilities are a separate axis from what a surface exposes. Zero `surface-writeback` captures; `check:surface-drift` + `type-check` clean.
- **2026-08-10 — Agent Apps surface agent-writable; the mixed-mode-per-field reference.** `matrx-user/agent-apps` declares 5 ask-policy targets covering the entire storefront of a published mini-app: `app_name` / `app_tagline` / `app_description` as `mode:"draft"` and `app_category` / `app_tags` as `mode:"entity"`. The generalizable bit is that **mode is a property of the FIELD's UI, not of the surface**: on the Settings > Identity tab the three text fields are bound to local state with their own dirty marker and Save button (a real staging buffer → draft), while the category and tag pickers commit on change (no staging step → entity). Splitting them is what makes each target's description true. Handlers in `features/agent-apps/route/AgentAppSettingsContent.tsx` via `useSurfaceWriteHandlers` under the `[id]` layout's provider, routing through the same local setters the inputs use and the same `saveField` wrapper the pickers call (`saveAppField` thunk); `saveField` gained an opt-in `{rethrow}` so an agent write reports failure instead of only toasting it. Every handler throws on a bad shape and refuses when no app row has hydrated. Deliberately NOT writable: `slug` (the public `/p/[slug]` URL), publish status, `is_public`, agent/version binding, component code, shell config and rate limits — those change what the app IS or who can reach it. Because the read twins come from the Redux row rather than the inputs, each draft description states that the twin only moves once the user saves. Live-verified with real Badass Agent runs: all 5 targets confirmed in one message with the manifest's own prose in each dialog; drafts staged into the inputs with the Save button appearing and the DB untouched until the user pressed Save; category and tags persisted immediately (tags proved full-set replace by keeping an existing tag); "Keep as is" returned a clean decline the agent did not retry; a publish/visibility request was refused because those targets are not in the tool's enum; and a deliberately bad `app_tags` shape came back as the handler's own error text with nothing written. Zero unintended `surface-writeback` captures (the one capture observed was the intentional bad-shape probe). `check:surface-drift` + `type-check` clean.
- **2026-08-10 — CMS site workspace agent-writable; the campaign's clearest case of a target blocked by a MISSING EDITOR rather than by doctrine.** `matrx-user/cms-site` declares 4 ask-policy draft targets on its Settings tab: `site_global_css` on the route's own provider (staging into the `globalCss` state the textarea drives), and `site_theme_config` / `site_navigation` / `site_footer_config` registered from inside `SiteAdvancedSettings`'s three sections via `useSurfaceWriteHandlers` — the deep-child seam earning its keep, since lifting five `useState`s per section into the route to satisfy the provider prop is exactly the plumbing that gets skipped. No handler touches `CmsSiteService`; the human's per-section Save remains the only writer, which is what makes the targets legal under the CMS's `agent_write_policy` (`blocked | draft_only | full`) — **staging is not saving**, so they work even on a blocked site. `client_sites` has no draft twin, so that Save publishes site-wide at once: `ask` on all four, `auto` on none. **The transferable lesson is `site_meta_defaults`.** It is the single best agent-value field on the surface (site-wide title suffix / description / social image every page inherits) and it got NO target — not because writing it is wrong, but because WF-6 built editors for theme/nav/footer/contact/social and skipped it, leaving no draft state to stage into and no Save to review under. The only remaining path would be an immediate `updateSite({metaDefaults})`: an entity write that bypasses the human AND is a save, which the site's own policy forbids. **When the judgment bar says YES but there is no editor, the answer is "build the editor first", not "reach for entity mode"** — declaring it would have converted a UI gap into a policy violation. Second reusable find: strict unknown-key rejection collides with real rows, because `footer_config` carries keys the editor preserves but never edits (`order`), and those keys are IN the value the agent reads — so a refusal message has to name the fix ("send only the keys you are changing"), or the agent loops echoing back what it just read. Third: **a multi-target stage can be silently undone by the page's own save-then-refresh**. Each settings section saves and calls `refreshSite()`, and `SiteAdvancedSettings` remounts on the new `updated_at` "so drafts re-seed from truth" — so staging three targets and saving the cards one by one persisted only the first. Pre-existing (a human editing two cards loses the same work) but write targets make it routine, so the intro instructs agents to warn the user and re-stage after each save. **When a surface has several independently-saved cards, check what one card's save does to the others' drafts before declaring targets across all of them.** Live-verified with real Badass Agent runs on `dev-website`: per-target ask dialogs carrying each manifest description verbatim, Apply staging a full stylesheet plus a matching token palette plus a nav menu built from the site's actual page routes, "Keep as is" leaving the footer untouched with no error, the human Save persisting to `client_sites` (`global_css` 0 → 2749 chars), and an undeclared target refused. Zero `surface-writeback` captures; `check:surface-drift` + `type-check` clean.
- **2026-08-10 — Grade Work composer agent-writable; THE input-vs-output reference.** `matrx-user/education-grade-work` declares exactly 2 draft/`ask` targets — `problem_text` and `expected_answer` — wired on `GradeWorkSurface.tsx`'s own provider through the same `setProblem` / `setExpected` the two textareas call, so an agent stages the problem it just posed (and a full-credit rubric worth grading against) and the learner still attaches the photo and presses Grade. Two only, deliberately: that mount authors exactly two things plus a photo `File` no agent can produce, and a third was NOT invented to pad the count. The value of this adopter is the line it draws. Everything in the `grading` group — `grade_result`, `grade_explanation`, `grade_misconception`, `grade_steps`, `work_transcription`, `grading_status` — is grader OUTPUT and stays undeclared by doctrine: letting an agent write the grade a student received would forge the verdict this surface exists to earn. `photo_attached` is derived status, not an input. Starting the run is not a target either, unlike `tasks`' `save_task` — the grade is metered (`education.image_grade`) and COPPA-gated, so spending a minor's quota stays a human gesture. Handlers refuse to stage while a verdict is on screen or a run is in flight, because the composer is unmounted then and a silent `setState` would report "applied" for a value nobody can see. Both handlers say "plain text string, not JSON and not JSON-encoded" in the throw itself: the inline-tool layer parses a JSON-looking argument before the handler sees it, and an agent told only "expected a string" tends to "fix" that by double-encoding, landing escaped newlines and stray quotes in the learner's box. Live-verified with a real Badass Agent run: both targets confirmed in one message with the manifest's own prose per dialog, Apply landed both values in the boxes, "Keep as is" left the rubric untouched and returned a non-error the agent acknowledged, a deliberate object argument surfaced the handler's throw verbatim to the model with the textarea uncorrupted and no double-encode retry, and an ask to write the grade was refused with the agent naming the only two writable targets. Zero `surface-writeback` captures on a clean page load; `check:surface-drift` + `type-check` clean.
- **2026-08-10 — Restored the Agent Builder write-target docs a merge dropped.** The 2026-08-09 entry below and the adopter-list clause landed in `68b1c007` and were lost in a later merge resolution while the CODE stayed intact — the docs claimed the campaign's highest-leverage surface was not agent-writable when it is. Nothing changed in the surface itself. **A merge that resolves a `FEATURE.md` conflict by taking one side wholesale silently reverts documentation for shipped code** — reconcile both sides.
- **2026-08-09 — Agent Builder surface agent-writable (third adopter).** `matrx-user/agent-builder` declares 6 draft/`ask` targets — an agent authoring ANOTHER agent's system prompt. `system_instruction` (full replace) and `append_system_instruction` (adds a rule without re-sending the prompt) both go through the new `withAgentSystemInstruction` helper + `setAgentMessages` — the exact pair `SystemMessage.tsx`'s textarea dispatches, so a rewrite and the user's typing are indistinguishable and the system message's non-text blocks always round-trip; `SystemMessage.handleTextChange` was refactored onto the same helper so there is ONE rebuild rule. `agent_description` / `agent_name` / `agent_category` / `agent_tags` go through `setAgentField`. Handlers in `features/agents/hooks/useAgentBuilderWriteHandlers.ts`, registered on `AgentBuilderClient`'s provider (desktop + mobile) so they stay wired whichever panel is open; every handler validates and throws, and refuses on a version snapshot or a view-only agent rather than staging edits the user could never save. Deliberately NOT writable: model/tiers, tools, custom tools, MCP servers, skill config, Matrx actions, context slots, variable definitions, output schema, and all governance/visibility. Live-verified with a real Badass Agent run on the builder: ask dialog per target carrying the manifest description, Apply staged into the editor (header flipped "No unsaved changes" → "Save changes", read twins updated), "Keep as is" returned `{ok:false,declined:true}`, append landed at the end of an untouched prompt, and a model/visibility change was refused with an explanation. Zero `surface-writeback` captures. `check:surface-drift` + `type-check` clean.
- **2026-08-10 — Schedules detail route agent-writable; first surface with a per-MOUNT write posture.** The 2026-08-09 pass made the schedules EDITOR writable (6 `schedule_draft_*` targets on `ScheduleForm`); this completes the surface's other write-capable mount. `ScheduleDetail` (`/schedules/[id]`) now registers `schedule_title` and `schedule_description` as `mode:"entity"`, ask-policy, through the canonical `updateScheduledTask` thunk (invariant 1 intact — no new `.from('sch_*')`). The generalizable bit: a surface with several providers does NOT need one flat posture. One manifest, one target list, and `listAgentWritableTargets()` offering only where a handler is registered is enough for the editor to expose reversible drafts while the read-only detail route exposes just two immediately-persisted fields. The line drawn — and worth copying — is that the detail route gets ONLY fields that cannot change what the entity does or when it does it; prompt and trigger changes stay editor-only so the user reviews the whole schedule before it is armed, and `enabled`/auth-mode/agent-binding/limits remain undeclared everywhere. Live-verified with a real Badass Agent run: both entity targets confirmed per-target with the manifest's own prose, applied, and still correct after a full page reload; an undeclared target (pause) was refused with the agent explaining only two targets exist; zero `surface-writeback` captures.
- **2026-08-10 — Shape Studio agent-writable (live-verified), split per mount.** `matrx-user/shapes` declares 6 ask-policy **draft** targets and NO entity targets — every one stages into the exact state the user's own typing feeds, and the user still presses Start / Save details / Render. The interesting part is the per-mount split: six components mount this surface, and only the three that own authored state register handlers — `NewShapeClient` (`new_shape_intent`, `new_shape_sample`), `ShapeOwnerEditor` (`shape_details_label`, `shape_details_title_key`, `shape_details_loading_component`, registered by name from inside `ShapePreviewTab`'s provider and only rendered for the kind's owner), `ShapeTestTab` (`test_draft_instance`, which SEEDS `KindInputForm` rather than setting the rendered instance, so the form's ajv structural leg still gates what Save can see). `ShapesListClient`, `ShapeInstancesTab`, and `ShapeSurfaceRuntime` register nothing, so the list, Instances, and Schema routes offer an agent no tool at all. Ruled out on the judgment bar: the `kind` SLUG (identity — every `__kind` payload and instance references it), `visibility` (internal→public publishes into the shared library), activation (a dual-gate verdict, not a field), and example/instance CRUD + re-pinning (version-bumping and destructive). Live-verified with Badass Agent across all three mounts: per-target ask dialogs carrying the manifest prose, applies landing in the real editors, "Keep as is" returning `declined:true` with no error, undeclared writes (visibility, slug rename) refused loudly, and an out-of-vocabulary `title_key` hitting the handler's throw — the agent read the error back verbatim and enumerated the accepted set. Zero `surface-writeback` captures. One contract bug found and fixed by that run: the tool layer parses a JSON-looking argument before the handler sees it, so a string-only `new_shape_sample` was unreachable for JSON samples and taught the agent to double-encode (escaped `\n` in the textarea); the target now takes the object/array itself and pretty-prints it, with a guard that throws on a double-encoded string. DB mirror (`ui.ui_surface_write_target`) still pending a manifest sync.
- **2026-08-09 — Marketing brand cockpit agent-writable (live-verified).** `matrx-user/marketing-brand` declares 2 ask-policy entity targets (`brand_profile` — merge-patch of the full editorial profile, `brand_identity` — industry/description copy), both through `updateBrand` with the version guard; handlers in `features/marketing/components/brands/MarketingBrandWriteTargets.tsx`, pure validation/merge core in `features/marketing/lib/brand-write-targets.ts` (unit-tested). Verified with a real agent run: per-target ask dialogs, applies persisted (v12→v16 on the test brand), decline path clean, undeclared brand-name write refused, zero writeback captures. Mirrored to `ui.ui_surface_write_target`. Confirmed business facts/assets/properties deliberately have NO write path — the surface doctrine keeps confirmed truth human-owned (candidates flow through the discovery review inbox).
- **2026-08-08 — Tasks surface agent-writable (second adopter) + `surface-write-targets` skill.** `matrx-user/tasks` declares 8 ask-policy targets (title/description/status/priority/due date/labels drafts via `patchTaskEdit`; `add_subtasks`/`save_task` entity); handlers in `TaskEditorBody.tsx`; live-verified (4 targets in one run — drafts staged + subtasks persisted + save). - **2026-08-08 — Surface auto-adoption at launch.** `launchAgentExecution` now adopts the mounted `<SurfaceRuntimeProvider>` (deepest wins, via `getSurfaceRuntime()`) when the caller passes NEITHER `runtime.surfaceName` NOR `runtime.applicationScope` — name AND live scope together, so every one of the ~33 surface-blind direct launch call sites picks up binding layers, value mappings, write policies, and document evidence on any page with a provider (127 mounts) with zero per-site wiring. Explicit caller values always win; a scope-only launch is untouched; the route-prefix guess (`detectActiveSurface`) is deliberately NOT used here (a name without a mounted runtime has no scope and would fabricate one — it remains the tool-injection fallback only, `build-tool-injection.ts`). A throwing adopted `getScope()` logs loudly and launches surface-named but scope-less. Verified: type gate + A/B against clean main (identical behavior on the dev session whose chat send was already stalled by HMR churn); live confirmation of an adopted scope on a real launch still owed.

- **2026-07-24 — Agent↔surface binding restored: catalog entity types now honor the registry's declared visibility.** aidream `0227_enforce_entity_acl_on_all_association_writes.sql` (applied 2026-07-23 19:57 UTC) made `assoc_add` require `iam.has_access(target,'viewer')` on non-conveying edges. `ui.ui_surface` is an ownerless platform catalog — no `visibility` / `created_by` / `organization_id` column — so `platform.entity_row_access_attrs()` resolved it to `personal` with a NULL owner and NULL org, which **no** user can ever satisfy: every bind failed with `42501 assoc_add: viewer access to target required` (last successful bind was 08:11 that morning). `platform.entity_types` already declared `surface.default_visibility = 'public'`; the resolver was ignoring it. Admins can now see authored vs resolved manifest provenance/evidence and edit handler-validated global namespace config without leaving the surface. Follow-up polish keeps both headers thin with icon-only actions, canonical Copy for AI, friendly display labels, and no raw slash-delimited surface key in user chrome.
- **2026-07-17 — Document Evidence System surface contract.**
  `SurfaceManifest.evidenceSources` now declares when a surface already knows a
  `processed_document_id`. `launchAgentExecution` resolves those declarations
  into the canonical source-only `request.context` shape before any
  agent↔surface mappings, so document context and the automatic
  `document_content` / `document_search` / `knowledge_search` / `verify` toolset
  no longer depend on an agent author wiring an ID. PDF Extractor declares the
  source once; extractor-chunker / analysis-studio / scanner inherit it.

(2) `matrx-user/transcript-scribe` manifest registered (`assistant` role, default = the seeded audio assistant; `dictionary` + `session_defaults` namespaces; deliberately no `inheritsFrom` — the transcripts viewer vocabulary isn't emitted by the live studio); DB synced + verified live (5 values, 1 role, url_pattern). `userPreferences.transcription.scribeAssistantAgentId` DELETED end-to-end (zero live rows held a value — no data migration needed); `resolveDefaultAssistantAgentId` reads the surface role, the Scribe settings picker writes user-tier `ui_surface_agent_pref`. - **2026-07-13** — **Adversarial review of the cutover: tier scoping + role-authoritative classification.** (1) `agent.menu_surface` gained a tier-role WHERE (`menu_surface_tier_scoping.sql`) — the owner-rights view was leaking (and `fetchSurfaceBindingLayers` was APPLYING) other users' user-tier and foreign orgs' org-tier bindings; now global→everyone, user→self, org/project/task→org members (probed live as authenticated + anon). (2) Global-tier binding writes gated to super admins by `trg_guard_global_surface_binding` (`global_surface_binding_write_guard.sql`; probed: normal user 42501, user-tier bind + super-admin global bind OK). (3) FE tier classification now parses `role` (`tierScopeFromRole`) instead of column null-ness — `assoc_add` stamps an access org on every edge, so column-based classification turned global binds into org layers and made them undeletable (recomputed wrong role); `surface-bound-agents` bucketing fixed the same way. Data parity re-verified live: 35/35 graveyard rows have their tier-role edge.
- **2026-07-12** — **Bindings replaced by canonical associations + surface inheritance v1.** (1) Condemned `agent_surface` junction DELETED end-to-end: rows migrated count-verified into `platform.associations` (tier-encoded `role`), table → `graveyard`, `agent-surface-bindings.service.ts` + its beacons removed by deletion, `create_shortcut_from_agent_surface` / `agx_usage_scan_core` repointed, `menu_surface` exposes `role`, drift-scan/remediate/stat reads moved to the view/edge, Diagram Editor (`bdaf5ee0`) ↔ `matrx-user/mermaid-editor` global edge seeded (`migrations/agent_surface_to_associations.sql`, applied + ledgered; aidream models regenerated). (2) `SurfaceManifest.inheritsFrom` + registry merge (values/roles/config, loud cycle/depth guards) + binding cascade in `fetchSurfaceBindingLayers` + manifest-sync mirrors of `parent_surface_name` and merged values. First families wired: transcripts ⊃ cleanup; pdf-extractor ⊃ extractor-chunker/analysis-studio/scanner. DB value mirror re-synced (649 rows). (3) Roles / config namespaces / full-screen admin editor documented above.

Route map + FE call sites updated; legacy `/pdf-widgets` and `/content-extractor` prefixes alias to the new names.
